### BT23-001 — Flickmon

#### Contract evidence

The committed catalog identifies Flickmon as a Blue level 2 Digi-Egg, form `Appmon`, attribute `Game`, and type `Flick`. It has no main, Security, or evolution text. Its sole clause is `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] If this Digimon has the [Appmon] trait, Draw 1`. `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-001` returned no entries.

#### Executable mapping and proof

`BT23-001.ts` registers only through `registerIrCard("BT23-001", compiled)`. The IR maps inherited `WhenAttacking` + `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` to `Draw 1` gated by `selfHasTrait(Appmon)`, with full coverage and no residual clauses. Registration supplies the per-source turn limit; the condition matcher evaluates the live host's complete form/attribute/type trait union; Draw moves one card from deck to hand.

`BT23-001.test.ts` proves catalog/IR shape, a legal Flickmon-under-Appmon stack, a non-Appmon negative, same-turn suppression, independent source identity for two stacked Flickmon sources, and a public breeding evolution from BT23-001 into off-color Appmon BT23-007. The evolution assertion captures physical egg/host instance IDs before the intent, verifies the permanent identity is preserved, verifies `stack[0]` remains the egg source, and verifies the host becomes `topCard` at zero cost.

#### Verification

Command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-001.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`

Result: 1 file passed; 6 tests passed. The reset test uses `startTurnLoop`, public attack/endPhase intents across two complete turns, hand-count deltas before each attack, neutral security cards, and public surrender to terminate the loop. No shared engine/testkit change was made.

#### Score

Catalog/rules 2/2; IR trace 2/2; behavior 2/2; peer/stack 2/2; delivery gates 0/2 pending coordinator lanes: **8/10 worker-ready**.

#### Fixture legality sweep (session 2)

Digi-Egg cards can never sit in a security stack or a main deck, so any fixture that seeded one was not realistic evidence. This sweep replaced 4 Digi-Egg fixture entries in security and deck lists with inert main-deck Digimon (BT1-009, BT1-013, BT1-027, BT1-028, BT1-045, BT1-047, BT1-050, BT1-064, BT1-065, ST1-02 — all with no effect, inherited, or security text). It also replaced 0 numeric `security: <n>` fixture(s), which the harness fills with the BT1-001 Digi-Egg by default. Raised the turn-loop attacker to `dp: 20_000` so it survives the now-legal 3000 DP security Digimon. `BT23-001.test.ts`: 6 tests pass.

#### Accepted score

9/10 — catalog/rules 2/2; IR trace 2/2; behavioral proof 2/2; peer and stack proof 2/2; delivery gates 1/2. Reviewed; evidence; collection gates passed on the working tree (sync/check, typecheck, lint, format, regression 3542/3542); commit and PR outstanding

### BT23-003 — Motimon

#### Source and contract

The committed catalog identifies BT23-003 as a Black level 2 Digi-Egg, form In-Training, with the Lesser and CS traits. Its inherited text is:

`[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn] When any of your [CS] trait Option cards are placed in the battle area, this Digimon may attack.`

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-003` returned no knowledge-base entries. The catalog record and printed clause are therefore the authoritative sources for this audit; historical reports are treated as unverified context.

#### Implementation and tests

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-003.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT23-003", compiled)`. The compiled IR models an inherited Your Turn subtrigger for `whenOptionPlayed`, restricted to the controller's Option cards with the exact CS trait, with an optional self attack that uses the normal suspension cost. The frequency is `OncePerTurn`, and the module reports full coverage with no residual clauses.

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-003.test.ts` contains nine focused tests. They cover the catalog and IR shape, public BT23-100 CS Option play and attack, same-turn suppression, non-CS and opponent-owned exclusions, explicit refusal of the optional attack, opponent-turn exclusion, and a legal breeding evolution. The evolution assertion keeps the physical egg source in `breeding.stack[0]` and the evolved card in `breeding.topCard`. The refusal case uses public `playCard`, starts with five memory, confirms the Option play event and its resolved card identity, confirms memory is exactly two after the cost, and confirms the opponent's security remains unchanged.

The production turn-loop reset test plays a CS Option during the first own turn (security 5 to 4), passes the first turn by paying the Option cost across memory and ends the opponent turn through public `endPhase`, and plays a second CS Option during the next own turn (security 4 to 3). Same-turn second play leaves the Motimon unsuspended and security unchanged, proving the per-source once-per-turn limit and its turn reset. The trait and controller exclusions use injected placement as supplemental matcher tests; opponent-turn exclusion has a public attack/Security origin.

The deterministic `advance.waitForMainPhase` helper waits for the phase and seat plus a quiet timing/resolution window. The companion `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/RUN-MAIN-READINESS.md` records the readiness mechanism regression: while a start-of-main timing effect is held, ordinary play and end-phase intents are rejected with `wrong-phase` and hand/memory remain unchanged; after release, public play succeeds. This prevents a phase publication race from hiding the inherited watcher.

Primitive paths exercised are public play and Option placement, the `whenOptionPlayed` matcher (controller, kind, exact trait), optional attack resolution and suspension, source-instance once-per-turn tracking, and turn reset. No shared engine gap remains in this card's focused evidence.

#### Verification

Command:

`pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-003.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`

Actual result: **1 file passed; 9 tests passed; 0 failed** (coordinator rerun: 0.794s).

Collection, build, and delivery gates remain coordinator-owned.

#### Score

Catalog/rules **2/2**; IR trace **2/2**; behavior **2/2**; peer/stack **2/2**; delivery **0/2** pending coordinator integration gates: **8/10 worker evidence**.

Coordinator review corrected the same-turn second-Option arrival predicate, supplied sufficient draw cards, and asserted exact cost, active controller, both Option placements, and refusal draw. Full delivery gates remain pending.

Digi-Egg cards can never sit in a security stack or a main deck, so any fixture that seeded one was not realistic evidence. This sweep replaced 14 Digi-Egg fixture entries in security and deck lists with inert main-deck Digimon (BT1-009, BT1-013, BT1-027, BT1-028, BT1-045, BT1-047, BT1-050, BT1-064, BT1-065, ST1-02 — all with no effect, inherited, or security text). ### BT23-007 — Musclemon

#### Contract and sources

The committed catalog record in `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies Musclemon as a red level-3 Digimon (play cost 3, 1000 DP), forms `Stnd.`/`Appmon`, attribute `Life`, and type `Muscle Training`. It has the standard red level-2 evolution for 0, the printed alternate `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[Appmon] trait: Cost 0`, and Link `[Appmon] trait: Cost 1`, link DP 2000, and linked `Piercing`. Its Security clause is: at the end of the battle, play this card without paying the cost.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-007` returned no entries. No local ruling or erratum ambiguity was found.

#### Implementation and proof

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-007.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT23-007", compiled)`. The Security effect arms `whenSecurityBattleEnded` and plays the retained self from `trash` with `payCost: false`; the linked static effect exposes Piercing; evolution and link requirements carry the exact traits, levels, and costs. Coverage is `full` with an empty residual list.

The colocated test covers the catalog and IR, a natural security attack proving delayed post-battle play, trash removal, and unchanged memory, alternate Appmon evolution with the egg source instance retained plus a non-Appmon rejection, valid link cost/2000 DP/Piercing, a real linked Piercing attack into a suspended 1000-DP Digimon that deletes the target and checks security, and invalid-host refusal with unchanged hand, link zone, and memory. This includes a legal Appmon host and a nonmatching peer. The assertions are state based and would fail if the card-specific Security source, link requirement, link DP, keyword, or combat behavior were removed.

Primitive paths inspected: security `whenSecurityBattleEnded` dispatch in `apps/api/src/engine/security/securityCheck.ts`; link requirement and linked keyword filtering in the effect kernel; linked DP through `ModifierLedger.linkDpOf`; alternate evolution matching in the evolution action path.

### BT23-008 — Greymon

#### Contract and sources

The committed catalog record in `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` identifies Greymon as a red level-4 Champion (play cost 6, 5000 DP), attribute `Vaccine`, types `Dinosaur`/`CS`, with red or blue level-3 evolution for 2. The alternate evolution is level 3 named Agumon or trait CS for 2. The printed clauses are Raid; once per turn Main, by placing this Digimon's top stacked card as its bottom digivolution card, may play one Gabumon or Nokia Shiramine from hand with play cost reduced by 2; and inherited Your Turn +2000 DP.

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-008` returned Q5216–Q5219. They establish that Greymon itself may be restacked, declaration is illegal with no evolution card, an egg-only exposed card is allowed then rule checked, and a source reaching 0 DP is rule deleted.

#### Implementation and proof

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-008.ts` registers only `registerIrCard("BT23-008", compiled)`. The Main action uses a mandatory self-restack cost followed by an up-to-one optional reduced paid play; target filtering is controller-owned exact-name Gabumon/Nokia. This ordering ensures declining the optional card selection still pays the printed processing cost. The static Raid, inherited Your Turn +2000 modifier, both alternate evolution recipes, full coverage, and empty residual list are present.

The colocated test covers exact catalog/IR, Q5216 restack and reduced payment, Q5217 empty-stack refusal, Q5218 egg-only rule checking, Q5219 0-DP rule deletion, mandatory restack despite declining the optional play, exact Gabumon versus Gabumon (X Antibody), both alternate evolution boundaries and a nonmatch, Raid redirection with an explicit post-resolution target-absence assertion, and inherited DP on/off controller turns. The near-name test inspects the public selection candidate IDs and proves Gabumon (X Antibody) is excluded before choosing exact Gabumon, so it fails under the former substring matcher. The Q5216 stack assertion proves the source instance moves from the top to the bottom, so the once-per-turn Main source is no longer exposed for a second public activation; the test does not misattribute that zone transition to frequency enforcement. The fixtures use real evolution stacks and named/near-name/nonmatching cards. State assertions would fail if restacking order, cost reduction, optional processing, source retention, inherited duration, exact-name filtering, or Raid were regressed.

Primitive paths inspected: `placeOwnTopAtStackBottom` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts`; optional and reduced `PlayWithoutCost` resolution in the IR interpreter; rule checks for exposed egg/0-DP cards; inherited continuous effects and turn ownership in the modifier ledger; Raid in the normal attack lifecycle.

#### Trait reference correction (session 2)

The animal-family branch matched traits exactly (`match: "trait"`). The printed text reads "[Avian], [Bird], [Beast], [Animal] or [Sovereign] in any of its traits (other than [Sea Animal])", which CR 2-3-2-4 defines as substring containment; the printed "(other than [Sea Animal])" carve-out only exists because "Sea Animal" contains "Animal". Under the old IR every compound trait — [Giant Bird], [Holy Beast], [Dark Animal] — silently failed the gate. Both the top-card and the inherited On Deletion branch now use `match: "traitContains"`. The [CS] branch stays `match: "trait"`, which is CR 2-3-2-3's "with the [XX] trait". This mirrors the accepted fix on BT23-078.

Red-then-green proof: with `match: "trait"` restored, three tests fail — the IR shape test, the behavioral "reads [Giant Bird] as [Bird] per CR 2-3-2-4 and plays it from hand" test (BT1-014 Kokatorimon, [Giant Bird], level 4), and the compound-trait matrix. With `traitContains` in place all 13 pass. BT1-033 Dolphmon ([Sea Animal]) is still refused, and the pre-existing positives (BT1-012 Biyomon, BT22-017 CS, BT23-023 Whamon boundary) stay green.

Exclusion scope: `excludeNameOrTrait` is evaluated card-level (`engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:189`) — a card with any [Sea Animal] trait is rejected outright, even if it also carries [Beast]. The printed text reads as a per-trait carve-out. No printed card combines [Sea Animal] with another qualifying animal-family trait at level 4 or lower, so the difference is unobservable today; the card-level spelling matches the whole BT15/BT23 family and BT23-078.

### BT23-024 — Poseidomon

Status: reviewed at 8/10; collection delivery gates and client App Fusion integration remain pending. No final 10/10 claim.

#### Contract and implementation

Sources: committed `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` BT23-024, local KB Q5246–Q5252 and Q6025–Q6026, and direct `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-024.ts`. Blue/white level 6, play 12, 12000 DP, ordinary blue level-5 evolution for 4, App Fusion Oujamon + Beautymon for 0. Evade and Link +1 apply. When Digivolving/When Attacking optionally links one eligible Appmon from own hand or this Digimon's evolution cards without paying its Link cost. All Turns, once per turn, becoming linked permits paying an unsuspend cost to prevent opposing Digimon from suspending through the opponent's turn, except all Digimon tied for highest play cost.

Production behavior remains exclusively `registerIrCard(cardId, compiled)`. The existing IR already represents the printed contract. The audit corrected the shared `GameEngine.highestPlayCostExemptions` helper: negative play-cost sentinels mean no printed play cost and must be excluded from the maximum calculation. Undefined costs remain excluded and valid zero costs remain eligible. The all-token test failed before this correction and passes after it; the failure was retained until fixed.

#### Reproducible behavioral evidence

The colocated suite has 18 passing tests, including three structural checks and these behavioral proofs:

- Public App Fusion consumes the exact physical Oujamon and Beautymon materials, pays zero, draws the evolution bonus, preserves the expected stack and allows entry Link refusal. Shared App Fusion mechanism regressions separately cover modifiers, legality and provenance.
- Public normal evolution from a realistic Musclemon / Coachmon / Oujamon stack pays 4 and draws once. Poseidomon links the own Musclemon source; another friendly stack remains unchanged. The source begins as an evolution card, so the assertion proves the actual source-to-Link transition.
- Public attack links the only eligible Appmon when invalid Poseidomon and non-Appmon hand candidates precede it. Invalid own cards and an opponent's physical control card remain in hand (Q5246).
- Public Link pays the suspended host's unsuspend cost and arms the restriction. An already unsuspended host cannot pay that cost and does not arm it.
- Mixed and all-token boards prove no-play-cost Amon/Umon are restricted. Ordinary highest-cost and tied-highest opponents are exempt, with lower-cost opponents restricted.
- A production turn loop proves actual lower-cost attack rejection and highest-cost attack success. The restriction expires through public turn changes, after which the formerly restricted attacker removes security.
- Public higher-cost play changes the highest-cost exemption. Public Calling From Darkness removes that entrant, recomputation exempts the prior highest again, and its public attack removes security. A separate supplemental direct-state fixture is not the duration proof.
- Public links preserve two links with Link +1; a third link presents a live trim choice. Selecting the first physical link trashes it, retains the second and third, and produces 17000 DP.
- Three public attacks across a real turn cycle prove linked unsuspend once-per-turn consumption and reset. Each newly linked physical material is asserted; the second activation leaves the host suspended, while the next turn's activation unsuspends it.
- Actual Raid combat from EX6-011 tests Evade acceptance and refusal. Acceptance suspends and preserves the exact Poseidomon; refusal moves it to trash after combat resolves.

The suspension mechanism is traced through `applySuspendRestrictionRecompute`, public attack legality and the ordinary suspension primitive. Link costs and watchers resolve through the effect interpreter. The App Fusion backend lifecycle has its own committed report, `APP-FUSION-MECHANISM.md`.

### BT23-026 — Lopmon

Source: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`; KB query `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-026` returned Q5255. The card is a yellow/green level-3 Beast/CS Rookie. Its printed conditional permission is represented by typed `baseGrantedDigivolve` metadata: Antylamon from hand, cost 3, ignoring requirements, only with the controller's exact Makiko Date. The former redundant activatable `YourTurn` Digivolve action was removed. The Kokomon alternate requirement uses exact name matching.

Focused public evidence covers:

- public Antylamon evolution with exact physical source/top/stack identities, cost 3, and the digivolution draw;
- missing-Makiko, opponent-only-Makiko, wrong-target, opponent-turn, breeding-base, and trash-source refusals (the same-ID trash mechanism also asserts unchanged physical hand, trash, deck and source stack);
- legal level-2 source matrix: BT7-006 Kokomon alternate cost 0, ST3-01 Tokomon normal cost 1, ST4-01 Motimon normal cost 1, and BT1-003 Upamon refusal;
- legal BT22-004 [CS] alternate evolution with source preservation and zero cost;
- P-105 Delay public evolution with reduced cost, training card in trash, and exact reveal/bottom physical instances;
- inherited once-per-turn suspension reaction, self-exclusion, public attack/Option origins, and opponent-turn behavior.

Clause mapping: `BT23-026.ts` `baseGrantedDigivolve` implements the conditional permission and exact Antylamon target; `BT23-026.test.ts` public grant, matrix, refusal, P-105, and inherited watcher tests provide observable coverage. Normal evolution uses public `digivolve`; the same-ID trash negative uses the documented `advance(...).verb.digivolveFromInstance` mechanism seam. Attack and Option origins use public intents.

Focused worker command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-026.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — **22 passed** after adding the level-2 matrix. The earlier 22-pass combined card/base-grant claim predates this matrix and is not a current combined count.

Coordinator client pricing: `pnpm --filter @aegis/web exec vitest run src/game/boardModel.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — **87 passed** (`logs/lopmon-client-pricing-retry.log`). These tests call the actual cost-options interface with own Makiko, missing Makiko, opponent-only Makiko and a card merely mentioning Makiko in its text. The helper remains private.

### BT23-027 — Angemon

Status: incomplete; no accepted ledger credit yet. Sources: committed catalog BT23-027, local KB Q5256/Q5257/Q6250, direct compiled IR, and the official Shakkoumon requirements recorded in SOURCE-RECONCILIATION.md.

Angemon has direct and inherited Barrier, ordinary yellow/black level-3 evolution for 3, and alternate Patamon or level-3 CS evolution for 2. On Play and When Digivolving draw one, then optionally DNA evolve two own Digimon into an exact Shakkoumon in hand on the controller's turn. Both DNA filters are corrected from substring to exact-name matching. Production registration remains exclusively registerIrCard.

The dependent BT23-032 module now carries the official CS level-4 alternate for 3 and both zero-cost DNA recipes (yellow level 4 plus black or blue level 4). Before correction, its missing requirements let the legacy fallback accept an invalid level-3 material and charge ordinary evolution cost. That behavior is not accepted evidence.

#### Reviewed evidence

The focused suite has 24 passing cases, including supplemental structural/injected-timing checks and these public proofs:

- Angemon hard play costs 5, draws its own effect card, and DNA evolves with legal Ankylomon for zero additional memory. Both physical materials, resulting top, On Play draw and DNA bonus draw are asserted.
- Normal yellow and black level-3 evolution pays 3; Patamon and off-color CS alternate routes pay 2. Each uses one hand Angemon and exactly two named deck cards, asserting both the evolution bonus and effect draw. Invalid alternate sources reject without mutation.
- Public On Play negative cases isolate one own material, an enemy-only second material, an invalid level-3 second material, Shakkoumon only in trash and Shakkoumon only in opponent hand. The result is present in own hand for the material-boundary cases. Draw, memory, exact unaffected material/hand/trash zones and no pending decision are asserted. A separate legal DNA choice is explicitly refused.
- Q5256 originates from a public Garurumon attack with inherited Betamon. It plays Angemon, which draws but cannot DNA evolve while restricted; the attacking blue level-4 Garurumon is otherwise a legal second material. Shakkoumon remains in hand and both battle-area Digimon remain. The first player's first-turn draw is skipped, so the asserted deck reduction is Angemon's own effect draw.
- Actual combat against direct Angemon and a Shakkoumon with inherited Angemon covers Barrier acceptance/refusal. The inherited refusal case explicitly declines its live source-play replacement choice before asserting quiet combat and final survival/security state.

#### Commands and limitations

Coordinator `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-027.test.ts src/cards/BT23/BT23-032.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` passed **2 files / 31 tests** in 2.03s (`logs/angemon-reviewed-dna-final.log`). Applicable Oxlint/Oxfmt and git diff --check passed. Prior shared/API/web typecheck and six-file integration passed before these final test-only assertion corrections; final delivery checks remain required.

Earlier worker-green claims are superseded: some material negatives omitted the result card, and a normal evolution fixture exhausted its deck before the required effect draw. Root corrected those fixtures. A temporary root assertion expecting an initial turn draw also failed; rule 6-3-1-1 skips that first-player draw, and the corrected test explicitly asserts the pre-attack deck count. The failed intermediate logs remain available.

Pending: Q5257 simultaneous double Alliance/Hudiemon/Angemon DNA ordering and retained first Alliance DP; Q6250 Barrier-versus-source-play ordering; natural opponent-turn entry as a negative DNA gate; final collection and delivery checks. Provisional components: catalog/rules 2, IR trace 2, behavior 1, peer/stack 1, delivery 0 = **6/10**, not independently accepted by the ledger.

Coordinator retained-red validation: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-027.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism -t 'Q5257|before playing the Angemon source'` failed both selected tests (24 other tests skipped), log `logs/angemon-timing-reproducers.log`. With all decision automation disabled, the Q5257 attack presents an `optional` decision instead of the simultaneous `orderTriggers` choice covering two Alliance instances and Hudiemon's When Attacking effect. This bounded test establishes the missing choice; it does not yet prove the later retained-DP/DNA/second-Alliance sequence. The earlier late wrong-phase response is superseded. Q6250 now awaits readiness and a real Barrier prompt: accepting Barrier consumes security and preserves Shakkoumon but never offers/plays its Angemon source. The Barrier-first endpoint fails; source-play-first ordering remains to be implemented and proven. Both are serialized engine follow-ups, with no extra ledger credit.

#### Exact-name sweep (session 2)

The printed route reads "[Digivolve] [Patamon]/Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2". The bracketed name with no "in name" wording is an EQUALITY gate, but the module used `names`, a substring gate in `engine/cards/cardData.ts` `matchGatedRequirement` (~line 481). Changed to `namesExact: ["Patamon"]`. No released card's name merely contains "Patamon", so the near-name negative uses a synthetic level-3 base without the [CS] trait ("BlackPatamon") passed straight to `matchingAlternateDigivolutionRequirement`: red before the fix (it matched the cost-2 route), green after. The positive [Patamon] route (BT1-048) and the level-3 [CS] route stay green. The DNA target [Shakkoumon] already used `match: "nameExact"`.

#### Final pass (session 2)

The card lane is closed. All three retained items are now public and green, one dead IR field
was found and fixed, and the module no longer needs `// @ts-nocheck`.

##### What changed

1. **Q5257 is now a full public proof.** The old test stopped at the `orderTriggers` prompt.
   It now drives the whole ruling: one window offering both `＜Alliance＞` instances plus the
   `[When Attacking]` effect; the 1st Alliance suspends Ankylomon (Hudiemon 7000 -> 12000);
   `[When Attacking]` returns the CS Tamer and replays `[On Play]` to play this Angemon free;
   Angemon's derived `[On Play]` draws and DNA digivolves Angemon + the suspended Ankylomon
   into Shakkoumon, which **arrives unsuspended**; the 2nd Alliance then suspends that
   Shakkoumon. Endpoints: max attacker DP during battle 20000 (so the 1st Alliance's +5000
   survived its ally becoming a digivolution card), opponent security 5 -> 2 by exact instance
   ids, no pending decision. `preferInstanceIds` pins the material pair, because
   Hudiemon + Ankylomon is also a legal Shakkoumon recipe.
2. **The opponent-turn DNA gate is public.** Previously only `advance.fire`. Angemon now
   reaches the board during the opponent's turn the one way the rules allow: seat 1 attacks
   inside the real turn loop, seat 0 answers the `[Counter]` window with `respondCounter` +
   `blast-digivolve:` (EX6-035 Cherubimon over BT23-029 Antylamon), and Cherubimon's
   `[When Digivolving]` plays Angemon free. Angemon draws; the DNA half is never offered even
   though Angemon + Ankylomon is a legal recipe and the `[Shakkoumon]` card is in hand.

3. **Q6250 is green.** The `barrier-then-source-play` seam was fixed by the engine lane
   (BARRIER-SOURCE-PLAY-MECHANISM.md). The test is a plain `it` and passes; the stale
   RETAINED RED comment was replaced with the mechanism reference.
4. **Defect found and fixed in the module.** Both DNA actions carried `from: ["hand"]`, which
   is **not a field of `DnaDigivolveAction`** — the result zone is read from
   `into.filter.zone` (`interpreter/actions/dna.ts` ~line 33, defaulting to `hand`). The key
   was silently ignored and only agreed with the default by luck. It is now `zone: "hand"` on
   the `into` filter, with a red-then-green proof: with `zone: "deck"` the new deck test fails

5. **`// @ts-nocheck` removed** from `BT23-027.ts`. That header was hiding defect 4 —
   `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` reports it as TS2353 as soon as the header is gone.
   The whole API package now typechecks clean, including the previously noted
   `combat/controller.ts:617` failure, which the engine lane fixed.

### BT23-028 — Coordemon

Sources: committed catalog `BT23-028`; KB Q5258–Q5262. Coordemon is yellow level 4, play cost 4, evo yellow level 3 for 2, DP 4000, Appmon/Sup./Entertainment/Coordinate, Link DP 3000. Printed clauses are Security end-of-battle self-play from trash, On Play/When Digivolving one opposing Digimon −3000 for the turn, Link Appmon cost 2, and When Linking restriction of one opposing Digimon’s When Digivolving activation until opponent turn end. Q5258–Q5262 define activation suppression, combined timing, and once-per-turn accounting.

Current focused tests cover catalog/IR, public security play timing, public link payment/DP/restriction, invalid non-Appmon refusal, and the public legal yellow level-3 evolution test. The evolution test asserts exact source instance retention, cost 2, exact drawn card instance in hand, top card, and −3000 DP endpoint.

Current focused result: 17/17 passed in the coordinator integration run below. The earlier focused command `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-028.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` had 16 passes and the Q5260 external reactivation red; that failure was reproduced before the shared activation fix. The bounded security/suppression rerun used the same command with `-t 'waits for its security|suppresses an opponent'` — 2 passed.

The public Security test uses a weak BT1-009 attacker (3000 DP), asserts the `securityChecked.battle` result (`attackerDeleted: true`, `securityDigimonDeleted: false`), requires the attacker’s exact physical instance in trash, and requires the Coordemon security instance’s `cardsMoved` event after the security battle event before it appears in the opponent battle area. It also asserts the attacker is no longer attacking and the Coordemon On Play −3000 endpoint is 7000. The committed `SecurityBattleResult` event shape exposes deletion outcomes but does not expose DP fields; fixture/catalog DP values remain asserted through the 3000-DP attacker and 4000-DP Coordemon behavior.

The public suppression test links Coordemon to an Appmon host, asserts the host’s exact base-plus-3000 linked DP, then uses `startTurnLoop()` and public `endPhase` to reach the opponent Main. The opponent publicly digivolves into Coordemon; the host remains at its linked DP, proving the opponent’s −3000 When Digivolving tail was suppressed. Removing suppression would fail that host-DP assertion.

An external reactivation probe uses EX6-043 Diaboromon with linked and unlinked controls. Both begin with zero tokens. After integration, the unlinked control creates one and the linked case creates zero. Before the fix the linked case also created one and failed the expected zero-token assertion. The original Q5260 engine gap was: Coordemon’s restriction is observable on Diaboromon, but its own top-card When Digivolving effect still reactivates through the external watcher. The probe command was `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-028.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism -t 'Diaboromon'` — 1 passed, 1 failed (linked expected 0, received 1).

The combined Q5259/Q5262 public test keeps the opponent in the same Main phase after evolving BT23-016 into BT23-021. It asserts the exact BT23-016 source remains in the stack, the restriction remains active, and BT23-007 remains in hand before immediately attacking; the attack links that exact BT23-007 through When Attacking without relying on a turn reset. Focused command `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-028.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism -t 'shared When Attacking'` — 1 passed.

The Q5261 by-placement boundary uses BT23-045 TigerVespamon’s mandatory `[On Play] [When Digivolving] By placing...` cost. The restricted case publicly evolves from BT23-044 while an eligible BT18-044 Royal Base card remains in hand; no security placement or opponent return occurs, the exact source remains physically in the opponent hand, and the original low-DP target remains in its owner’s battle area with security unchanged. The unlinked control places the exact BT18-044 card face-up in bottom security and returns the selected target to its owner’s hand. Command `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-028.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism -t 'TigerVespamon'` — 2 passed.

The public On Play boundary tests play Coordemon with its mandatory DP effect and verify own-controller exclusion, exact-one target selection, 3000-DP deletion, and 4000-DP survival at 1000. The isolated 4000-DP case confirms the survival boundary and quiet endpoint. The production turn-duration test now proves this same target returns from 1000 to 4000 at the actual current-turn boundary; the separate linked restriction survives through the opponent Main and expires after opponent turn end.

The public suppression expiry test uses production turn flow and observes the restriction during the opponent Main and cleared after opponent turn end.

Coordinator clause review: all printed clauses now have behavioral evidence, with Q5258–Q5262 covered by direct evolution, external Diaboromon reactivation, TigerVespamon by-cost suppression/control, and the combined When Attacking timing test. Link rejection preserves memory and physical card zone. The mandatory DP effect has exact-one/controller, 3000 deletion and 4000 survival boundaries; no optional refusal is invented. There is no printed inherited effect or once-per-turn cap on Coordemon itself. The shared borrowed/security-source activation mechanism has an additional public BT23-060/BT23-015 control pair. ### BT23-029 — Antylamon

#### Sources

- `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`: BT23-029 catalog identity, Yellow/Green level 5, play 7, normal level-4 evolution costs 4, and alternate evolution text.
- `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-029`: Q5265 (self-play triggers), Q5266–Q5269 (the restriction covers direct/effect activation and the “by” condition), and Q5270 (a blocked timing does not consume a once-per-turn activation).
- `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-029.ts`: compiled IR under audit.

#### Evidence run

Command: `cd apps/api && ./node_modules/.bin/vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-029.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`.

Result: **1 file passed, 29 tests passed**. The corrected Q5265 fixture uses legal Kazuchimon → Chaosmon evolution and a single target; the public self-play restriction passes. Alliance acceptance/refusal, exact target selection, public CS Tamer control, inherited lifecycle, masked-existing-Antylamon coverage, exact 4000-DP deletion, 5000-DP survival, self/enemy suspension exclusion, near-trait exclusion, same-turn inherited cap, and public next-turn watcher reset also pass. The masked test uses a neutral CS Tamer to avoid introducing an unresolved optional Betamon OnPlay choice.

Two additional public probes were added while the test lane was paused: carrier-self and opponent suspension exclusions with a 5000-DP target, and an Appmon-only near-trait play. They require a new focused run before being counted as green.

The focused run validates the 4000-DP exact-target deletion boundary and public same-turn watcher suppression followed by a real opponent-turn reset.

The tests cover catalog/IR shape, self-play restriction, public Beast/Beastkin/CS peer play and a nonmatching play, exact alternate names, normal Yellow/Green level-4 evolution, CS level-4 alternate evolution, public rejection of a CS wrong-level source and an unrelated Beastkin source, and a public inherited attack that applies -4000 DP and resets across a production turn transition. Evolution assertions capture the physical source instance, top card, memory cost, and the resulting draw card.

#### Clause mapping and primitive paths

- Alliance keyword and public choice paths: `BT23-029.test.ts` “declares Alliance”, “publicly accepts Alliance…” and “publicly refuses Alliance…”; IR `Static` keyword and production attack/`respondAlliance` intents.
- Self-play and played-trait restriction: “reacts when this card itself is played” and the peer matrix; `AllTurns` `SubTrigger(whenPlayed)` → `Restrict(cannotActivateWhenDigivolving, untilOpponentTurnEnd)`. Public tests cover both a newly played Antylamon and an already-masked Antylamon reacting to a later CS Tamer, independently of an OnPlay mirror.
- Evolution requirements: “publicly evolves Antylamon from %s” and “rejects an ineligible alternate source”; `digivolve` requirement validation → source stack/top-card replacement and draw.
- Inherited suspension clause and public lifecycle: “publicly applies inherited -4000 when another Digimon attacks, then resets next turn,” plus the structural test; public attack suspension → `SubTrigger(whenSuspended)` → `ModifyDP(-4000, forTheTurn)`.

#### Final pass (session 2)

The three gaps left open in session 1 are now closed with public tests.

##### 1. Mixed Beast/Beastkin/CS pool, including opponent-controlled cards

`traitPool` drives two matrices over the same six cards: Beast Digimon AD1-010, BT1-049 and BT1-031, Beastkin Digimon BT1-035, CS Digimon BT23-017, and CS Tamer BT23-078.

- "reacts to an own played %s and restricts exactly one opposing Digimon": each own play restricts the first opposing Digimon and leaves the second untouched, so the `count: 1` bound holds for every trait and both card kinds.
- "ignores the same %s when the opponent controls and plays it": the opponent holds and plays the same card on their own turn; neither of their two Digimon is restricted. This pins the `controller: "mine"` source filter against a controller mix-up.

Option cards with the [CS] trait (BT22-099, BT22-100, P-225) are not in the pool. Their play path is a [Main] activation with its own targets and colour-requirement clauses; adding one would test the Option, not Antylamon. The Digimon and Tamer cases already cover both `whenPlayed` shapes.

##### 2. Restriction removal at the exact opponent-turn end boundary

New test "keeps the restriction through every opponent phase and drops it at the opponent turn end" reads the `phaseChanged` event log instead of sampling state:

- The restriction is applied on the controller's own Main phase.
- After `endMainPhaseIfOpen(0)` and a wait for the opponent's Main, the log contains `0:End` and the restriction is still active. This rules out a `untilYourTurnEnd` mapping.
- After the opponent's Main ends, the test polls for the first moment the restriction disappears and captures the phase log emitted since. The first entry is `1:End`, and no second opponent phase appears. The clear therefore lands on the opponent's End phase, matching `EffectDuration.UntilOpponentTurnEnd` swept at `opponentTurnEnd` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/modifiers.ts` (`clearsAt`).
- The next own Main phase reports no restriction.

##### 3. Inherited clause is now public only

The `advance.fireSubTrigger` smoke test is gone. It is replaced by "publicly reacts only when another own Digimon suspends, not the carrier itself": under a real turn loop the carrier (BT23-035 over BT23-029) attacks first and leaves the 10000-DP opponent untouched, then BT1-035 attacks and the same opponent drops to 6000. `advance.fireSubTrigger`, `fireGlobal` and `fireForPermanent` no longer appear in the file.

- Fixture legality confirmed: every card id used in the file is a main-deck Digimon or Tamer (levels 3-7). No Digi-Egg appears in any security or deck fixture.

#### Evidence

Result: **1 file passed, 14 tests passed** (Vitest 5.0.0, 2026-09-07).

Tests assert public advertised Main activation pays exactly 1, primitive Main cost behavior, mixed ineligible cost-4/near-trait/opponent-zone rejection, Chuumon-name free play, mandatory Reboot and Blocker on one selected target with an untouched second target, actual keyword persistence through the opponent Main and expiry at the next own Main, free-play refusal, Alliance keyword shape, and public normal Yellow/Black plus alternate exact-name/CS evolution stacks with source identity, top card, memory, and draw assertions.

### BT23-031 — Angewomon

#### Card

Yellow Lv.5 Archangel/CS Digimon. Play cost 7, DP 6000.
Evolution costs: Yellow Lv.4 cost 3, Purple Lv.4 cost 3.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate route.
2. "When this card would be played from the hand, if you have [LadyDevimon] or [Mirei Mikagura], reduce the play cost by 3."
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` Add your top security card to the hand. Then, if you have 3 or fewer security cards, `＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞`.
4. Inherited: `＜Alliance＞`.

Catalog text matches the official card list. No catalog discrepancy.

#### Q&A covered

| Id | Ruling | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5276 | The clause still works at 0 security: no card can be added to hand, so only `＜Recovery +1＞` happens. | "Q5276: recovers from an empty security stack even though no card can be added to hand" |

Rules used: comprehensive §2-3-1-2 — a card reference written only in brackets matches the printed name exactly.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[CS]: Cost 3` | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 4, traits: ["CS"], cost: 3, isAlternate: true }]` | "digivolves from a [CS] Lv.4 of any colour source for 3 …" |
| Printed yellow/purple Lv.4 routes | `evoCosts` in the catalog | "digivolves from a yellow Lv.4 …", "… purple Lv.4 …" |
| Play-cost reduction | `Static` -> nested `Replacement wouldBePlayed` / `reduceCost 3` with a `youHave` gate; consumed at pay time by `wouldBePlayedSelfReducersFor` (`VERIFIED_SELF_REDUCER_CARDS` lists BT23-031) | "costs 4 instead of 7 …" (LadyDevimon, Mirei), "costs the full 7 …", "ignores a near-name …" |
| Security-to-hand then conditional Recovery | `OnPlay` and `WhenDigivolving` -> `SecurityManipulation toHand toTop`, then `SecurityManipulation addTop from deck` gated by `zoneCount security lte 3` | "moves the physical top security card to hand and recovers back to four …", "does not recover when five security cards leave four behind", "Q5276 …", the three digivolve tests |
| Inherited `＜Alliance＞` | `Static` effect, `isInherited: true`, `keywords: [Alliance]` | "grants inherited Alliance to its carrier", accept / refuse / security-count tests |

#### Behavioral endpoints proved

- Play with own [LadyDevimon] (BT23-067) on the field: memory 10 -> 6 (cost 4), Angewomon on the battle area, no pending decision.
- Play with own [Mirei Mikagura] (BT22-089) Tamer: memory 10 -> 6.
- No qualifier: memory 10 -> 3 (full cost 7).
- Controls, all full cost: "LadyDevimon (X Antibody)" (EX7-058) on the field, a LadyDevimon in hand, a LadyDevimon in trash, and the opponent's LadyDevimon.
- Security clause at 4: the physical top instance (BT23-100, which carries a `[Security]` effect) moves to hand and is the only hand card; the deck's top instance becomes security index 0, face down; security back to 4; the moved card's `[Security]` effect does not fire (no extra permanent on the board, card not trashed).
- Security clause at 5: ends at 4, deck untouched, top instance in hand.
- Security clause at 0 (Q5276): hand stays empty, security becomes 1 with the deck's top card, face down.
- Digivolve from BT1-055 (yellow Lv.4), BT11-078 (purple Lv.4), BT23-008 (red Lv.4 with [CS]): memory 5 -> 2 each, source card is the only stack card, bonus draw plus the security card both in hand, security back to 4 with the recovered instance on top, deck empty.
- Illegal source: BT1-046 (yellow Lv.3, no [CS]) is rejected; host top card and memory unchanged.
- Alliance accept: carrier ST3-10 with BT23-031 underneath gains `Alliance`; after `respondAlliance` with the ally, the ally is suspended, carrier DP 12000 -> 16000 (ally's 4000), `securityAttack` 2; the battle then deletes the defender and the carrier survives.
- Alliance refuse: `respondAlliance` with no ally leaves the ally unsuspended, DP 12000 and `securityAttack` 1, and the battle still resolves.
- Alliance security count: the boosted carrier attacking the player checks 2 security cards (opponent security 3 -> 1, two `securityChecked` events).

#### Defect found and fixed

`BT23-031.ts` gated the reduction with `match: "name"`, which is substring matching in
`matchNameOrTrait` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts`). The printed
reference is bracket-only, so comprehensive §2-3-1-2 requires an exact name. With the old IR,
"LadyDevimon (X Antibody)" (EX7-058) wrongly enabled the -3.

Fix: `match: "nameExact"` in `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-031.ts`. Confirmed as a real defect by
reverting the token and rerunning the near-name test, which then failed (memory 6 instead of 3).

#### Remaining gap (engine, not fixed here)

The printed clause says "would be played **from the hand**". The IR carries no origin-zone
condition, and the pay-time path does not add one: `GameEngine.fireBeforePayCost`
(`apps/api/src/engine/GameEngine.ts:4096`) receives `originZone` but only forwards it to interactive
sub-trigger reductions — `wouldBePlayedSelfReducersFor` reductions are applied for any origin. The
`WouldBePlayedSelfReducer` shape (`engine/effects/interpreter/registration/reducers.ts:25`) has no
zone field, so this cannot be fixed inside the card module.

No retained red test is attached, because the gap is currently unreachable: every catalog effect that
plays a Digimon from the trash does so "without paying the cost", and the paid effect-play path
(`finalizeEffectPlayCost`) is only reached from hand for Digimon. The defect is latent, not live.
Recommended engine work: carry `originZone` into the self-reducer evaluation and add an
`originZone: "hand"` gate to reducers compiled from "would be played from the hand" text.

```
# 1 file, 18 tests passed, 4.32s  (logs/BT23-031-vitest.log)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        # no BT23-031 errors (engine/combat/controller.ts belongs to another lane)
pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-031.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-031.test.ts   # clean
pnpm exec oxlint  apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-031.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-031.test.ts         # 2 no-explicit-any warnings on IR-shape reads, same style as BT23-028
git diff --check                           # clean
```

### BT23-032 — Shakkoumon

Status: worker report, no ledger credit claimed. Sources: committed catalog BT23-032, local KB
(Q5277, Q5278, Q5279, Q6250), the direct compiled IR in `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-032.ts`,
REVIEW-NOTES.md "Official special-requirement reconciliation (2026-09-07)" and BT23-027.md.

#### Card summary

Yellow/Black level 5, play cost 8, DP 8000, traits Mutant / Hudie / CS / Angel.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate evolution.
2. `[DNA Digivolve] Yellow Lv.4 + black/blue Lv.4: Cost 0`.
3. `[When Digivolving]` Until your opponent's turn ends, give 1 of their Digimon
   "[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks."
4. `[When Digivolving]` Then, if DNA digivolving, ＜De-Digivolve 1＞ 1 of your opponent's Digimon.
5. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` When this Digimon would leave the battle area other than by your
   effects, you may play 1 level 4 or lower yellow, black or [CS] trait Digimon card from its
   digivolution cards without paying the cost.
6. `[Inherited]` The same [All Turns] clause.

The catalog text matches the official card list, including the two special-evolution headings
restored in commit 91fb49da8. No catalog discrepancy found.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, printed EvoCosts | "declares the official catalog identity, CS alternate and both DNA recipes" | `digivolutionRequirement`, `dnaDigivolveRequirement` |
| 1 CS alternate cost 3 | "publicly evolves from a level-4 [CS] source for 3 even when its colour is off-recipe" (green Kuwagamon BT22-047), plus two rejections | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| Printed yellow/black EvoCost 4 | "publicly normal-evolves from a level-4 %s source for 4 and draws the evolution bonus" (BT1-051, BT10-062) | catalog `evoCosts` |
| 2 DNA cost 0 | "publicly DNA digivolves for 0 with a yellow level 4 plus a %s level 4" (BT23-050 black, BT23-018 blue) | `dnaDigivolveRequirement` |
| 2 negative | "refuses DNA digivolution with a level-3 material and charges no ordinary fallback cost" | as above |
| 3 grant + duration | "makes the chosen opponent Digimon attack at the start of their main phase, and only that turn" | `GrantAuraToOpponents`, `duration: untilOpponentTurnEnd` |
| 3 / Q5277 | "Q5277 gives the effect to a Digimon unaffected by effects, but it never triggers" | `grantCustomEffect` `isActive` re-check |
| 4 conditional De-Digivolve | "De-Digivolves 1 opponent Digimon only when it DNA digivolves" and "does not De-Digivolve when it digivolves normally" | `DeDigivolve` + `condition: isDnaDigivolving` |
| 5 / Q5278 colour branch | "plays a yellow level-4 card from its own digivolution cards when an opponent effect deletes it" | `PlayWithoutCost` `filter.colors` |
| 5 / Q5278 trait branch | same case with BT23-063 (purple, [CS]) | `target.orFilters[0].nameOrTrait` |
| 5 pool negative | "plays nothing when no digivolution card is a level 4 or lower yellow, black or [CS] Digimon" | `levelComparison lte 4` |
| 5 leave cause | "plays a source when the opponent deletes it in battle"; "does not play a source when its own controller's effect removes it" | `leaveCause: otherThanYourEffect` |
| 5 optional refusal | "declines the optional source play and trashes the whole stack" | `optional: true` |
| 5 own-stack scope | bystander stack asserted untouched in the opponent-deletion case | `target.source: "thisDigimon"` |
| 6 / Q5279 | "Q5279 applies the same replacement from a stack where Shakkoumon is a digivolution card" | `isInherited: true` copy |
| 5/6 once-per-turn declaration | "models both leave-play replacements as a once-per-turn optional own-stack play" | `frequency: OncePerTurn` |
| Q6250 | retained red `it.fails(...)` | engine seam, see below |

Q5278 and Q5279 both state the pool is "yellow or black, OR [CS]" (an OR, not an AND). The IR
already encodes that as `filter` (colours) plus `orFilters` (trait). Both branches are proven
behaviourally with cards that satisfy exactly one of them.

#### Evolution routes proven

- Normal, yellow: BT1-051 Reppamon base, pays 4 to 0, stack is exactly the base instance, one
  evolution-bonus draw, deck reduced by one.
- Normal, black: BT10-062 Golemon, same endpoints.
- CS alternate: BT22-047 Kuwagamon (green level 4, [CS]) pays 3 to 0. Green satisfies no printed
  EvoCost, so this isolates the alternate.
- Alternate rejections: level-3 [CS] (BT23-037) and level-4 non-CS (BT1-051). Both leave the base
  top card, the hand card and memory unchanged.
- DNA: no player intent declares a DNA digivolution, so the public route is BT23-027 Angemon's
  [On Play], which DNA digivolves two of its controller's Digimon into a Shakkoumon in hand.
  Angemon is the yellow level 4 material; the partner is BT23-050 (black) or BT23-018 (blue).
  Endpoints asserted: both material instances in the resulting stack, Shakkoumon on top, exactly
  one battle-area permanent, memory 10 -> 5 (Angemon's play cost 5, DNA cost 0), and both draws
  (Angemon's effect draw and the DNA evolution bonus) in hand in order.
- Illegal DNA pair: BT23-037 Tentomon is level 3. The DNA does not happen, Shakkoumon stays in
  hand, both Digimon stay on the battle area and memory is 5 — that is, no legacy ordinary-
  evolution fallback cost is charged. This is the negative the reconciliation note asked for.

#### What replaced the previous suite

The committed `BT23-032.test.ts` was six cases: five pure IR-shape assertions and three
`deletePermanent` replacement cases. The new suite keeps the two shape assertions that state the
printed contract, keeps every behaviour the old cases covered (own-stack scoping against an
unrelated friendly stack, the inherited copy, an ineligible stack card going to trash) and adds
the evolution routes, the DNA proofs, the grant lifecycle, the De-Digivolve condition, the battle
and own-effect leave causes and the refusal branch. No assertion was weakened.

#### Defects found and fixed

`BT23-032.ts` carried a file-level `// @ts-nocheck`. It was unnecessary: removing it leaves
`pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` clean. The IR itself needed no change — the OR pool,
the `otherThanYourEffect` leave cause and the granted effect text all match the printed card
and the rulings.

#### Retained red

`it.fails("Q6250 plays a source card after ＜Barrier＞ prevents the battle deletion")`.

Expected: with BT23-027 Angemon among its digivolution cards, accepting ＜Barrier＞ keeps
Shakkoumon on the battle area and the controller may still play Angemon from the digivolution
cards with the [All Turns] effect.
Actual: Barrier consumes the security card and Shakkoumon survives, but no source-play choice is
offered and Angemon stays in the stack.
Suspected seam: the Barrier prevention path in `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts` resolves
the prevention without re-entering the `wouldLeavePlay` replacement registered by
`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/replacement.ts`. This is the same open item
recorded against BT23-027 and owned by the engine lane; no fix attempted here.

#### Remaining gaps

- Once-per-turn is proven only as a declaration. A direct or inherited leave removes the
  permanent, so a second occurrence of the same replacement on the same permanent within one turn
  has no observable route; the reset on the next own turn is likewise unobservable. If the
  coordinator knows a card that returns a permanent to the battle area and re-triggers a leave in
  the same turn, that case can be added.
- Q5277 uses `advance().ledgers.continuous.addRestriction` to make the recipient unaffected,
  following the EX12-016 / Q6740 precedent, because no simple card grants an unconditional
  "isn't affected by your opponent's Digimon's effects" at a usable level. The grant handout and
  the missing activation are both asserted through the real turn loop.
- The clause-3 turn-loop case cannot use `advance().runTurn(1)`'s awaited form: the forced attack
  fires before seat 1's Main phase opens for input and the resulting memory swing ends that turn
  immediately, so `waitForMainPhase` cannot succeed. The test runs the production turn and settles
  on `turnEnded`, then asserts exactly one `attackDeclared` by the granted permanent against the
  player, seat 0's security down to 2, and the grant gone once the turn has ended.
- The inherited (Q5279) case is proven with the colour branch only; the trait branch is proven for
  the direct copy.
- The banlist entry (restricted to 1 copy since 2026-04-04) is not exercised; deck validation is
  outside this card's module.

  -> 1 file, 22 tests: 21 passed, 1 expected fail, 3.00s
  (`logs/BT23-032-focused.log`).
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-032.test.ts src/cards/BT23/BT23-027.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
  -> 2 files, 48 tests: 46 passed, 2 expected fail, 2.93s. The dependent Angemon suite is

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` -> clean.

- `git diff --check` -> clean.

### BT23-033 — Beautymon

Source records: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` (BT23-033) and `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-033` (Q5280–Q5281). The committed module is compiled IR and uses only `registerIrCard("BT23-033", compiled)`.

The catalog record is level 5, Yellow/Black, play cost 8, 8000 DP, Appmon, with Yellow or Black level 4 evolution for 4, Link [Appmon] cost 3, and Link DP 4000. The module includes Barrier, optional On Play and When Digivolving linking from trash or this Digimon's digivolution cards, the Your Turn once-per-turn When Linked security/DP sequence, and the linked All Turns protection sequence.

| Q5281 recovery then DP tail | `SecurityManipulation addTop` is conditional at five or fewer security, followed by DP reduction; existing tests assert six security and 4000 DP from six security cards |
| Your Turn once-per-turn | `SubTrigger whenLinked`, `frequency: OncePerTurn`; structural evidence only pending a public same-turn repeat/reset test |
| Linked protection | All Turns linked `Restrict cannotReturnToHandOrDeck` and `GrantStatic protection beDeDigivolved` until opponent turn end; public Link test asserts both restrictions |
| Link cost/DP | Catalog/module cost 3 and Link DP 4000; public link test asserts memory payment and host DP |

Existing tests include catalog/IR checks, public On Play linking, public Link payment and DP, and stack-source ownership. The pre-existing security fixtures now use real main-deck Digimon (`BT1-009` through `BT1-014`) rather than DigiEgg cards.

The focused suite now also contains a public attack into Beautymon's Barrier with both accept and refusal branches. Acceptance asserts the physical top security card is trashed, Beautymon remains in battle, and the barrier prompt is closed; refusal asserts Beautymon leaves battle. These tests use a real opposing BT1-024 attacker and real security cards.

### BT23-034 — Sakuyamon

Sources: committed catalog `BT23-034`; KB Q5282-Q5286 and Q5289-Q5292.

#### Card summary

Yellow level 6 Digimon, play cost 11, DP 11000, Mega, Data, traits Shaman/Zaxon/CS.
Printed evolution: yellow Lv.5 for 3, plus the alternate `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3`.

Printed clauses:

1. `When this card would be played, if you have a Tamer with the [Zaxon] trait, reduce the play cost by 5.`
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] Until your opponent's turn ends, 1 of their Digimon can't activate [When Digivolving] effects and gets -6000 DP.`
3. `[On Deletion] Place this card face up as the bottom security card.`

The catalog text matches the official card list. No discrepancy to report.

#### Clause -> test -> IR map

| Clause / Q&A | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog fields, alternate route | `matches every catalog field...` | `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Clause 1 (with Tamer) | `pays 5 less with a Zaxon Tamer...` | `Static` / `Replacement wouldBePlayed reduceCost 5` |
| Clause 1 (without Tamer) | `does not reduce the play cost without a Zaxon Tamer` | same |
| Clause 1 IR shape | `reduces its play cost by 5 when you have a Zaxon Tamer` | same |
| Clause 2 shared once-per-turn IR | `once per turn restricts and weakens one opposing Digimon...` | three triggers, one `sharedUseKey` |
| Clause 2 duration | `expires the restriction and the -6000 at opponent turn end` | `duration: untilOpponentTurnEnd` |
| Clause 3 IR | `places itself face up at the bottom of security on deletion` | `SecurityManipulation placeAsSecurity faceUp` |
| Evolution routes | `accepts off-color level-5 CS evolution and rejects...` | `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Q5282 | `suppresses the opponent's When Digivolving half...` | `Restrict cannotActivateWhenDigivolving` |
| Q5283 | same test, attack half | same |
| Q5284 | `blocks an external activation... (restricted=true/false)` | same |
| Q5285 | `does not process the By cost of a suppressed effect (restricted=true/false)` | same |
| Q5286 | `suppresses the opponent's When Digivolving half...`, attack half after the blocked digivolve | same |
| Q5289 | `places the deleted card face up behind the existing security card` | `placeAsSecurity` |
| Q5290 | `checks the face-up placed card as a normal security card` | `placeAsSecurity` |
| Q5291 | `triggers the Security effect of a face-up security card` | engine rule, no card IR |
| Q5292 | `re-hides every face-up security card on a shuffle` | engine rule, no card IR |

#### Behavioral evidence

All behavioral tests drive public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `endPhase`, `surrender`)
through `startTurnLoop()`. No injected timing API is used anywhere in this file.

**Clause 1.** With BT23-086 Yuugo (Zaxon Tamer) on the board, playing Sakuyamon from memory 10
leaves memory 4 (cost 6). The exact hand instance leaves the hand and becomes a battle-area top
card, and the single opposing Digimon ends at 4000 DP with the restriction on. Without a Zaxon
Tamer the same play leaves memory -1 (cost 11).

**Clause 2, Q5282/Q5283/Q5286.** Seat 0 plays Sakuyamon and restricts the only opposing Digimon
(BT2-037 Angewomon, 10000 -> 4000). After a real turn pass, seat 1 digivolves that same permanent
into ST24-07 ShineGreymon, whose `[When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn]` effect would
play a Tamer and apply -9000 DP. Nothing happens: the ST3-12 Tamer instance is still in seat 1's
hand and Sakuyamon is still at 11000 (Q5282). Seat 1 then attacks in the same turn; the same shared
effect activates on the `[When Attacking]` timing, moving that exact Tamer instance from hand to
battle area and dropping Sakuyamon to 2000 (Q5283 and Q5286 — the blocked timing did not consume
the once-per-turn use).

**Q5284.** Paired test against EX6-043 Diaboromon, whose `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When an
opponent's Digimon is played, you may activate 1 of this Digimon's [When Digivolving] effects`
would otherwise play a Diaboromon Token. With Sakuyamon's restriction on Diaboromon, two opponent
Digimon plays produce zero extra permanents in seat 1's battle area. The unrestricted control
produces at least one.

**Q5285.** Paired test against BT23-045 TigerVespamon's mandatory `[On Play] [When Digivolving] By
placing 1 [Royal Base] or [Zaxon] trait Digimon card ... face up as the bottom security card,
return 1 of your opponent's Digimon ...`. Restricted, the BT18-044 source stays in seat 1's hand,
seat 1's security stack is byte-identical to the pre-digivolve instance list, and seat 0's Digimon
stays on the board — the `by` cost is not processed at all. The control places that exact instance
face up at the bottom of security and returns seat 0's Digimon to hand.

**Q5289.** Sakuyamon attacks in seat 0's Main (which suspends it and weakens the future attacker
from 18000 to 12000), then seat 1 attacks the suspended Sakuyamon and wins the battle. Seat 0's
security stack becomes exactly `[existing face-down card, Sakuyamon]`, with the pre-existing card
still `faceUp: false`, the Sakuyamon instance last and `faceUp: true`, and Sakuyamon not in trash.

**Q5290.** With that same state as the fixture (BT23-034 face up as the only security card), a
3000-DP attacker attacks the player. The check runs normally: the face-up card fights as a
Security Digimon, the attacker's exact instance ends in seat 1's trash, the security stack is
empty, and the checked card's exact instance ends in seat 0's trash. Being trashed after a won
security battle is not a deletion, so the `[On Deletion]` clause does not re-place it.

**Q5291.** BT21-041 Calendamon (`[Security] At the end of the battle, play this card without paying
the cost`) sits face up as the only security card. A 3000-DP attack on the player checks it; the
`[Security]` effect still activates and that exact instance ends in seat 0's battle area.

**Q5292.** Seat 0's security holds two face-up cards and one face-down card. Playing EX3-029
Airdramon runs its `[On Play] ... Then, shuffle your security stack`. Afterwards every remaining
security card is `faceUp: false`.

**Duration.** The restriction and the -6000 are both observable through the whole opponent turn and
both gone at the start of seat 0's next Main (target back to 10000).

#### Fixture notes

Two tests (Q5290, Q5291) seed a face-up security card directly. That is the exact state the Q5289
test proves BT23-034's `[On Deletion]` produces; no card in the pool places a Zaxon or Royal Base
Digimon carrying a triggered `[Security]` effect face up, so there is no shorter public route to a
face-up card with a `[Security]` effect. All security fixtures use real main-deck Digimon; no
Digi-Egg is placed in security or deck.

#### Mutation check

Rewriting the card's `restriction` value from `cannotActivateWhenDigivolving` to `attack` fails 7 of
the 17 tests, including all four suppression tests. The suppression assertions are load-bearing.

  — 1 file / 17 tests passed, 2.20s. Log: `logs/BT23-034-focused.log`.

  on the three paired restricted/control tests); both patterns match the accepted BT23-028 style.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-034.test.ts` — clean after formatting.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no diagnostic mentions BT23-034. The unrelated
  `src/engine/combat/controller.ts` failure belongs to the engine lane.
- `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Defects

None. `BT23-034.ts` is unchanged: the IR already models all three clauses correctly, and the
engine's `isTimingActivationDisabled` gate (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/timingActivation.ts`)
already blocks triggering, external activation, `by` processing and once-per-turn accounting.

No `it.fails` or `it.skip` in this file. Remaining gaps:

- Q5290 and Q5291 use a seeded face-up security card rather than chaining off a live `[On Deletion]`

- The Q5292 shuffle uses `Math.random`, so the test asserts only the face-down invariant, not a
  permutation.

### BT23-035 — Dynasmon

#### Card summary

The committed catalog record `BT23-035` matches the official card list entry in `logs/official-bt23.html` field for field: Dynasmon, Yellow/Red, Digimon, Lv.6, play cost 12, DP 12000, evolve costs 4 from Lv.5 (Yellow) and 4 from Lv.5 (Red), form Mega, attribute Data, types Holy Warrior/Royal Knight/CS, rarity R. No catalog discrepancy; no catalog or effects.json edit was needed.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[Witchelny]/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate evolution route.
2. `＜Barrier＞` — static keyword.
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] By trashing your top security card, all of your opponent's Digimon get -6000 DP for the turn.`
4. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When your security stack is removed from, this Digimon gains ＜Security A. +1＞ until your turn ends. Then, if you have 3 or fewer security cards, ＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞.`

Rulings covered: Q5293, Q5294, Q5295, Q5296.

#### Clause to test to IR map

| Clause / ruling | Test | IR node |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog fields, alternate route data | `matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause` | `digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| Clause 1, printed routes | `evolves publicly from a Yellow Lv.5 for 4 and from a Red Lv.5 for 4, drawing 1 each time` | catalog `evoCosts` |
| Clause 1, alternate route | `evolves publicly from a Witchelny Lv.5 for the alternate cost of 3` | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| Clause 1, negative | `refuses a Lv.5 source that is neither Yellow/Red nor a Witchelny/CS trait holder` | route validation |
| Clause 2 | `exposes Barrier through the live keyword seam` | `Static.keywords` |
| Clause 3, On Play | `reduces current and later-played opposing Digimon for the turn` | `OnPlay.actions[0]` |
| Clause 3, unpayable cost | `does not reduce DP when its security-trash cost cannot be paid` | `OnPlay.actions[0].cost` |
| Clause 3, refusal | `may decline the top-security processing condition before trashing or reducing DP` | `optional`, `abortOnDecline` |
| Clause 3, shape | `requires trashing the top security card to reduce all opposing Digimon by 6000` | `OnPlay` / `WhenDigivolving` |
| Clause 3, When Digivolving and duration | `applies the same -6000 DP through a public evolution and restores it at the real turn end` | `WhenDigivolving.actions[0]`, `duration: forTheTurn` |
| Clause 4, controller scope and Recovery source | `ignores opposing security removal, then buffs and recovers the deck TOP card from its own removal` | `SubTrigger.sourceFilter`, `addTop` |
| Clause 4, Recovery condition | `skips Recovery while more than 3 security cards remain but still grants Security Attack +1` | `condition.zoneCount lte 3` |
| Clause 4, once per turn (Q5295) | `caps the security-removal trigger per turn and re-arms it on the next own turn` | `frequency: OncePerTurn`, `duration: untilYourTurnEnd` |
| Clause 4, shape | `gains Security Attack +1 and conditionally recovers at end of the security-removal trigger` | `AllTurns.actions[0]` |
| Q5293 | `deletes a later-played 0-DP opposing Digimon before its On Play effect can activate` | rule check ahead of pending activation |
| Q5294 | `resolves the checked Security effect before its own security-removal trigger` | `securityCheck.ts` ordering (CR 15-16-10-2) |
| Q5296 | `checks an extra security card after Barrier trashes its own security` — RETAINED RED | ＜Barrier＞ cost / `whenSecurityRemoved` |

#### Ruling coverage

**Q5293.** BT1-070 Kuwagamon (Lv.4, 4000 DP, `[On Play] Suspend 1 of your opponent's Digimon`) is played onto the opponent's board after Dynasmon's player-wide -6000 DP is live. The test asserts Kuwagamon's exact instance is in the opponent trash, the opponent battle area is empty, and Dynasmon is still unsuspended — the observable proof that the pending `[On Play]` never activated because the rule check deleted the 0 DP Digimon first. The opponent cannot play a Digimon during the turn player's turn through an intent, so the play is driven through the effect-play verb; that is the production path Q5293 describes.

**Q5294.** ST22-08 Offensive Plug-In V sits on top of the audited player's security stack with Dynasmon in play. The opponent attacks the player. The `[Security]` effect deletes the attacker, and the test then asserts Dynasmon still gained ＜Security A. +1＞ and still performed Recovery, and that the attacker's `cardsMoved` event index is lower than the recovered card's. That is the ordering CR 15-16-10-2 requires: the `[Security]` effect activates immediately, the security-removal watcher runs after it.

**Q5295.** A real turn loop runs seat 0's turn, then seat 1's. On the opponent's turn the first removal grants ＜Security A. +1＞ with 4 security cards left, so Recovery does not qualify. A second removal in the same turn is refused: the keyword stays at 1 and the deck is asserted unchanged card for card even though the stack is now at 3 and would otherwise qualify. After the turn boundary, the test asserts the grant is still 1 (it expires at the end of the controller's own turn, not the opponent's), then a third removal takes the count to 2 and recovers the exact deck top.

**Q5296.** Retained red — see below.

#### Defects found

**Fixed locally in `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-035.ts`:**

1. `// @ts-nocheck` removed. The module typechecks without it.
2. ＜Recovery +1 (Deck)＞ was compiled as `SecurityManipulation { op: "placeAsSecurity", from: ["deck"], source: { filter: { controllerDefault: "mine" }, count: 1 } }`. That target filter opened a `selectCards` decision over the whole deck, so the controller could recover **any** deck card instead of the top one, and the effect stalled whenever nothing answered the prompt. Recovery takes the deck's top card by rule (§16-6). The action now uses the same shape as EX1-027, which prints the identical clause: `{ op: "addTop", controller: "mine", source: "deck", amount: 1, condition: … }`. The behavioral test asserts the recovered card is the exact deck top, face down, with the second deck card untouched.

**Retained red (engine lane):**

`it.fails("checks an extra security card after Barrier trashes its own security")`.

Suspected seam: `CombatController` pays ＜Barrier＞ with `this.access.flipTopSecurityToTrash(perm.controllerSeat)` (`apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts:1287` calling `apps/api/src/engine/state/access.ts:639`). `flipTopSecurityToTrash` is a bare zone move: it emits nothing and never publishes `whenSecurityRemoved` or `whenCardTrashedFromSecurity`, unlike `primitives.trashFromSecurity`.

Observed end state (Dynasmon attacks into a 13000 DP Security Digimon, Barrier accepted): event stream `attackDeclared, securityRevealed, barrierPrompt, barrierResolved, securityChecked`; Dynasmon survives and its own top security card is in trash; ＜Security A. +1＞ is 0; no Recovery happened (own security 0, deck untouched); the opponent's second security card is still in their stack. Expected per Q5296: the Barrier trash is a removal from the controller's own security stack, so the `[All Turns]` trigger gives ＜Security A. +1＞ and, at 0 security cards, Recovery, and the check loop — which re-reads Strike before each card — checks a second card.

Note this is only reachable at all because ＜Barrier＞ *is* offered during a Security Digimon battle; the prompt fires. The gap is the missing event publication, not the prompt.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-035.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, 17 tests: 16 passed, 1 expected fail (the Q5296 retained red), 2.18s. Log: `logs/BT23-035-focused.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no error mentioning BT23-035.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` on both files — formatted.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` on both files — 3 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` warnings in the structural IR assertions, the same style the exemplar BT23-028 suite uses. No errors.

#### Remaining gaps

- Q5296 stays unproven until the Barrier cost publishes the security-removal buses. That is an engine change, so the reproducer is retained rather than weakened.
- The `[On Play]` cost path is proven through the exact security card trashed and the empty stack, but the interaction where paying that cost itself triggers the card's own `[All Turns]` watcher is not asserted; no ruling in scope covers it.
- Dynasmon is never played for its printed cost of 12 in these fixtures (memory is set directly), so the play cost itself has catalog-level evidence only.

| Delivery gates | 0 | Coordinator owned. |
