#### Card summary

Yellow/Red level 6 Digimon, play cost 12, DP 12000, forms Mega, attribute Vaccine, traits Beastkin / Boss / CS. Ordinary evolution: yellow level 5 for 4, red level 5 for 4.

Printed clauses:

| Id | Printed text |
| --- | --- |
| C1 | `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[Leomon] in name or w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` |
| C2 | When this card would be played, if your opponent has a Digimon with 10000 DP or more, reduce the play cost by 5. |
| C3 | `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` 1 of your other Digimon may digivolve into a level 6 or lower Digimon card with `[Leomon]` in its name or the `[CS]` trait in the hand without paying the cost. |
| C4 | `[End of Your Turn] [Once Per Turn]` 1 of your Digimon gains `＜Raid＞` for the turn. Then, that Digimon may attack. |

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Content | Clause |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5297 | The C3 destination is 1 level 6 or lower Digimon card with `[Leomon]` in its name, or 1 level 6 or lower `[CS]` trait Digimon card. | C3 |
| Q5298 | C4 may grant `＜Raid＞` and then decline the attack. | C4 |

#### Clause to test plan

| Clause | Planned proof |
| --- | --- |
| Catalog | Exact `getCardDefinition` match plus `compiled.digivolutionRequirement`, coverage and residual. |
| C1 | Public `digivolve` intent on four legal routes: ordinary yellow level 5 (`BT3-039`, cost 4), ordinary red level 5 (`BT1-024`, cost 4), alternate index 0 from a Leomon-name level 5 whose colour blocks the ordinary route (`BT9-063` LoaderLeomon, black, cost 3), alternate index 1 from a `[CS]` level 5 whose colour blocks the ordinary route (`BT23-044` Lilamon, green, cost 3). Each asserts source instance retained in the stack, exact memory paid, and the bonus draw's exact instance in hand. Negative: a level 4 source is refused and memory is untouched. |
| C2 | Public `playCard` with an opponent Digimon at exactly 10000 DP (`BT1-024`) pays 7; at 9000 DP or lower (`BT23-033`, 8000) pays the full 12. Both assert final memory and the card physically on the battle area. |
| C3 / Q5297 | Public `playCard` (On Play) and public `digivolve` (When Digivolving) each digivolve one *other* Digimon from hand for free: Leomon-name destination (`BT23-044` Lilamon into `BT4-061` BanchoLeomon) and `[CS]` destination (`BT23-031` Angewomon into `BT23-034` Sakuyamon). Assert the exact destination instance is the recipient's top card, the recipient's original instance is in the stack, and no extra memory left the effect. Negatives: a level 7 `[CS]` card whose requirement the recipient satisfies (`BT23-047` Examon off a level 6 `[CS]` base) is not offered — with a public control proving the same evolution *is* legal when paid for; a level 6 card that is neither Leomon-name nor `[CS]` (`ST3-10` Magnadramon) is not offered; the source itself is excluded (`excludeSelf`); the optional prompt can be declined. |
| C4 / Q5298 | Real turn loop (`startTurnLoop` plus public `endPhase`). Decline branch (Q5298): `＜Raid＞` is granted at end of turn and the chosen Digimon stays unsuspended with the security stack untouched. Accept branch: the same Digimon attacks — suspended, security consumed. Once per turn: the grant repeats on the next own turn and is absent during the opponent's turn (`forTheTurn` expiry). |

Fixture rule: security uses real main-deck Digimon; no Digi-Egg cards outside breeding.

#### Clause to test to IR mapping (as built)

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog | `matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause` | `compiled.digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| C1 | `publicly digivolves into BanchoLeomon from a %s source` (4 cases), `refuses a level 4 source ...` | `digivolutionRequirement[0..1]` |
| C2 | `pays the reduced play cost at the exact 10000-DP opponent boundary`, `does not reduce its play cost below the 10000-DP boundary`, `reduces its play cost when ...` (shape) | `effects[0]` Static / Replacement `wouldBePlayed` |
| C3 / Q5297 | `On Play digivolves another Digimon into a level 6 %s card from hand for free` (2 cases), `When Digivolving digivolves ...`, `never offers a level 7 CS card ...`, `lets that same level 7 CS card be reached by paying for it publicly`, `never offers a level 6 card that has neither ...`, `excludes itself ...`, `may decline the free digivolution ...`, `lets one other Digimon digivolve ...` (shape) | `effects[1]`/`effects[2]` Digivolve, `into.levelComparison lte 6`, `nameOrTrait`, `excludeSelf`, `payCost: false`, `from: ["hand"]` |
| C4 / Q5298 | `Q5298: grants Raid at end of turn and still allows declining the attack`, `attacks with the same Digimon that gained Raid when the attack is accepted`, `fires once on each of your own turns and never on the opponent's turn`, `grants Raid and attacks the same Digimon at end of turn` (shape) | `effects[3]` EndOfYourTurn, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, GainKeyword Raid `forTheTurn`, Attack `sameTarget`, `optional` |

#### Evidence notes

C1 uses four public `digivolve` intents. `BT9-063` (black) and `BT23-044` (green) cannot use either printed ordinary evolution colour, so their cost-3 success proves the two alternate routes rather than the yellow/red `evoCosts`. Every case asserts the source instance id in the stack, memory reaching exactly 0 from the exact cost, the drawn instance id in hand, and no pending decision. The level 4 negative asserts the refusal plus untouched memory, board and hand.

C2 is proved at the printed boundary: a 10000-DP `BT1-024` reduces the cost to 7 (memory 10 to 3); an 8000-DP `BT23-033` leaves the full 12 (memory 12 to 0).

C3 is driven only through public `playCard` (On Play) and public `digivolve` (When Digivolving). Both destination halves of Q5297 are exercised: a `[Leomon]`-name destination (`BT23-044` into `BT4-061`) and a `[CS]`-trait destination (`BT23-031` into `BT23-034`). The level cap is proved with a paired control: `BT23-046` Rosemon (green level 6 `[CS]`) satisfies `BT23-047` Examon's printed green level 6 evolution requirement, so the effect's refusal to offer Examon can only come from the level 6 cap — the companion test digivolves the same pair publicly for cost 5 and succeeds. A second negative uses `ST3-10` Magnadramon: a level 6 yellow card whose requirement the recipient meets but which is neither `[Leomon]`-named nor `[CS]`. `excludeSelf` is proved by playing BanchoLeomon with no other Digimon on the board. The decline branch keeps the destination in hand and the recipient's stack empty.

C4 runs on the production turn loop (`startTurnLoop` plus public `endPhase`). The `＜Raid＞` grant has `forTheTurn` duration and the turn ends as soon as the effect finishes, so the grant is only observable while the "may attack" prompt is still pending. Both branch tests therefore leave that prompt unanswered, assert `hasKeyword(bancho, "Raid")` and the unsuspended attacker live, then answer it with a public `respondDecision`. Q5298's answer is the decline branch: the Digimon stays unsuspended, the opponent keeps all 3 security cards, no `securityChecked` event is emitted, and nothing is attacking. The accept branch consumes one security card, suspends the same Digimon and emits `securityChecked`. Frequency is proved by counting `effectTriggered` events for `BT23-036/ir-3-0` across three real turns: 1 after the first own turn, still 1 after the opponent's turn, 2 after the second own turn.

#### Results

Focused command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-036.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file / 22 tests passed, 6.60s (`logs/BT23-036-focused.log`).

`pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` passed. `pnpm exec oxfmt` applied; `pnpm exec oxlint` reports only the pre-existing `no-explicit-any` warnings on the three IR shape guards, which match the style of the sibling BT23 card tests. `git diff --check` is clean.

#### Defects found

None. `BT23-036.ts` was not changed: the compiled IR already matches the printed text and both rulings, and every clause is behaviorally reachable through public intents.

Note on the catalog: `effectText` matches the official card. No discrepancy to report.

#### Remaining gaps

- The `＜Raid＞` grant cannot be asserted after the end-of-turn window closes, because its `forTheTurn` duration ends with the turn. The tests assert it live at the pending decision instead, which is the strongest available observation; there is no engine defect here.
- The `[Once Per Turn]` marker cannot produce a same-turn refusal for an `[End of Your Turn]` trigger, since the window opens once per turn. The proof is the per-turn trigger count across three real turns.
- The C3 level cap negative depends on `BT23-047` Examon having no compiled alternate requirement; its printed `[Digivolve] Lv.6 w/[CS] trait: Cost 5` is absent from `effects.json`. That is a catalog gap on **BT23-047**, not on this card, and it does not weaken the negative (the ordinary green level 6 route is used instead). The coordinator may want to route it to the Examon lane.

Both `[On Play]` / `[When Digivolving]` digivolve filters print "with [Leomon] in its name or the [CS] trait" and correctly use `match: "name"`. No change.

### BT23-037 — Tentomon

#### Card

Catalog source: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`. Green/Yellow level-3 Rookie, play cost 3, 1000 DP, Vaccine, traits Insectoid/Hudie/CS. Printed evolution costs: Green level 2 for 1, Yellow level 2 for 1.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS] trait: Cost 0` — alternate evolution route.
2. `[Your Turn] When this Digimon would digivolve into a Digimon card with the [CS] trait, reduce the digivolution cost by 1.`
3. Inherited: `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] You may play 1 play cost 5 or lower Digimon card with the [Hudie] trait from your hand without paying the cost. The Digimon this effect played can't digivolve and is deleted at the end of your opponent's turn.`

The catalog text uses non-breaking spaces in three places. The test normalises them and asserts both printed strings in full. No catalog discrepancy against the official text was found; the card module needed no change.

#### Q&A covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-037` returns Q5299, Q5300, Q5565, Q5566.

| Ruling | Test | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5299 — no trigger from the breeding area | "reduces the CS digivolution cost by 1 only from the battle area" (7 cases) | pass |
| Q5300 — the newly played Digimon may be chosen for a pending Alliance | "offers the newly played Digimon to a pending Alliance on the attacking host" | pass |
| Q5300 — the ally's DP reaches the attacker | "adds the played ally's DP to the attacking host" | retained red (`it.fails`) |
| Q5565 — the played Digimon is deleted at the timing | "deletes the played Digimon at the end of the opponent's turn, not the controller's" | pass |
| Q5565 — a prevented deletion leaves the prohibition intact | "keeps the evolution prohibition when the deletion is prevented" | pass (closed session 2) |
| Q5566 — both processings happen at the same end-of-turn boundary | "resolves the deletion at the same end-of-turn boundary as the opponent's effect" | pass |
| Q5566 — the turn player chooses the order | "offers the turn player the end-of-turn processing order" | retained red (`it.fails`) |

#### Clause to test to IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Tests |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS]: Cost 0` | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 2, traits: ["CS"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` | "digivolves for 0 over a level-2 CS card and rejects a level-2 non-CS card" |
| `[Your Turn]` CS cost reduction | `YourTurn` -> `Replacement wouldDigivolve` with `sourceFilter.zone: "battleArea"` and `into.nameOrTrait [CS]` | the 7-case reduction table, plus "offers no CS reduction on the opponent's turn" |
| Inherited play | `WhenAttacking` (inherited, `OncePerTurn`) -> `PlayWithoutCost` from hand, `playCostLte: 5`, `[Hudie]` trait, `bindResultAs: "playedHudie"` | "plays only a hand Hudie Digimon of play cost 5 or lower", the once-per-turn tests, the decline test |
| "can't digivolve" | `Restrict digivolve` on `boundRef: playedHudie`, `duration: "permanent"` | "permanently blocks digivolution of the played Digimon while an untouched twin still digivolves" |
| "deleted at the end of your opponent's turn" | `DelayedDelete` with `timing: "endOfOpponentTurn"` | the deletion-boundary test and both Q5566 tests |

Behaviour is proved through public intents only: `digivolve`, `attack`, `respondAlliance`, `endPhase`, `respondDecision`, `surrender`, driven by the real `startTurnLoop`. No injected timing seam is used; the only non-intent calls are `advance().waitForMainPhase` and one `advance().verb.unsuspend` to re-arm the same attacker inside one turn.

#### Evidence detail

**Q5299.** The digivolve intent selects its own cost path (`intent.useAlternateCost`, `apps/api/src/engine/actions/digivolve.ts` step 5), so the table drives both routes and reads the installed reduction through `observe().costReduction("wouldDigivolve", …)` before paying:

| Zone | Target | Route | Reduction | Memory paid |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| battle area | BT23-041 (CS) | ordinary | 1 | 2 (printed 3) |
| breeding | BT23-041 (CS) | ordinary | 0 | 3 |
| battle area | BT23-041 (CS) | alternate | 1 | 1 (printed 2) |
| breeding | BT23-041 (CS) | alternate | 0 | 2 |
| battle area | BT23-020 (CS, off-colour) | alternate only | 1 | 1 |
| breeding | BT23-020 (CS, off-colour) | alternate only | 0 | 2 |
| battle area | BT1-051 (non-CS) | ordinary | 0 | 2 |

Each case also asserts the exact top card and single-source stack, and one evolution bonus draw by exact deck and hand contents. The non-CS row separates the printed `[CS]` condition from the route; the off-colour rows make the reduction visible where only one route exists. A further test shows the reduction is 0 while `turnSeat` is the opponent.

**Inherited clause.** The host is `BT23-050` Ankylomon over a level-3 `[CS]` Tentomon, which is a real public evolution route. On a real attack the effect plays only `BT23-020` Seadramon (cost 5, Hudie); `BT23-055` (cost 7, Hudie), `BT1-009` (no Hudie), `BT23-081` (Hudie Tamer) all stay in hand, and `BT23-017` (Hudie, cost 3) stays in the trash, proving the hand-only source. The board ends with exactly two permanents, no pending decision and no loud gap. The catalog has no Hudie Digimon of play cost exactly 6, so the upper boundary is proved at 7.

**Restriction.** The played Seadramon rejects a `digivolve` intent into `BT23-055` on its legal alternate route with 8 memory available; memory is unchanged and the permanent stays. A second Seadramon that this effect did not play accepts the identical intent and pays 3, so the block is bound to the played instance and not to the card id.

**Once per turn.** One host plays a Hudie on its first attack, plays nothing on a second attack in the same turn (asserted before the turn ends, not merely awaited), and plays the second copy on the next own turn after both players end their phases through the real loop. A separate test shows the cap is per Tentomon copy: two hosts, each with its own Tentomon source, each play one Hudie in the same turn.

**Decline.** With `autoDeclineOptional` nothing is played, the candidate stays in hand, and after both turn boundaries the battle area still holds only the host and the trash is unchanged — no deletion was scheduled.

**Deletion boundary.** The played Digimon survives the controller's own turn end and is deleted at the opponent's turn end, with exactly one matching card in the trash. The host and its Tentomon source are asserted intact.

#### Retained reds

1. **Alliance DP on the legacy branch.** `it.fails("adds the played ally's DP to the attacking host")`. Q5300's own content — the played Digimon appears in `alliancePrompt.eligibleAllyIds` and is suspended by `respondAlliance` — passes. The DP benefit does not arrive. The host `BT23-041` has no printed `[When Attacking]` effect on its top card, so `combineAllianceTiming` (`apps/api/src/engine/GameEngine.ts`) is false and `CombatController.declareAttack` takes the legacy `!combineAllianceTiming` Alliance loop in `apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts`. Expected 14000 (6000 base + 3000 from BT23-041's own suspend effect + the 5000-DP ally); observed 6000, so the pre-existing `forTheTurn` grant is dropped as well. `src/engine/combat/allianceDecision.test.ts` still passes, so the combined branch is healthy. The engine lane owns Alliance attack timing, so this is not adjusted here.

2. **Q5566 ordering choice.** `it.fails("offers the turn player the end-of-turn processing order")`. Both processings do land at the same boundary (proved by the passing companion test: BanchoLeomon's ＜Raid＞ attack still sees the played Digimon and redirects onto it, leaving security at 5). But no `orderTriggers` request is ever made. The deletion is a one-shot `endOfTurn` SubTrigger subscription installed by `delayedDeletePlayed` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts` and owned by the non-turn player, while `[End of Your Turn]` is a System-A timing effect owned by the turn player; the two are never combined into one ordering window across controllers, so the deletion always resolves last. `src/engine/effects/delayedDeletePlayed.test.ts` only covers the same-controller collision (Q5564), which is why the shared suite does not prove this card.

3. ~~**Q5565 prevention half.**~~ Closed in session 2, see below.

#### Q5565 closed (session 2)

The prevention half is now green and the `it.fails` is gone.

Two causes, fixed in two lanes:

1. The BT23-073 lane removed `leaveCause: "otherThanYourEffect"` from Eater Bit's `wouldLeavePlay` replacement. The printed text has no "opponent's effect" qualifier, so `causeAllows` must stay at "any" and the prevention now answers a deletion the controller's own card scheduled.
2. The residual failure in this file was the fixture, not the intent shape. Eater Bit's prevention is an alternative cost ("delete this Digimon" or "place it under your breeding [Mother Eater]"), so it raises a chooseOption decision. The fixture ran with `autoAcceptOptional` and `autoSelectCards` but no `autoChooseOption`, so that decision was never answered; the end-of-turn boundary stalled and `waitForMainPhase(0)` threw "Seat 0's Main phase did not become ready". Adding `autoChooseOption: true` closes it. `permanentId` plus `useAlternateCost` on the digivolve intent was not the cause — the same pair digivolves successfully on the untouched twin in the sibling test.

Endpoints, unweakened: the played Digimon is still in the battle area under its exact instance id and not in the trash, Eater Bit is in the trash and off the board, `isRestricted(played, "digivolve")` is true, and a public `digivolve` intent on it is rejected with the card left in hand and memory unchanged at 8.

Both are replaced.

### BT23-038 — FunBeemon

#### Card summary

Lv.3 Green/Black Digimon. Play cost 3, DP 1000. Traits: Insectoid, X Antibody, Royal Base, CS.
Printed evo costs: Green Lv.2 cost 1, Black Lv.2 cost 1.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[Royal Base]/[CS] trait: Cost 0`
2. `[Security] [All Turns] All of your [Royal Base] trait Digimon get +1000 DP.`
3. `[On Play] Reveal the top 3 cards of your deck. Add 1 card with [Royal Base] in its text and
   1 card with the [CS] trait among them to the hand. Return the rest to the bottom of the deck.`
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP.`

Catalog matches the official text. No discrepancy found. The catalog uses a non-breaking space
before `trait` / `in its text`; the test asserts the exact catalog string including ` `.

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Subject | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5301 | "[X] in its text" spans name, traits, effects, inherited effects, (Rule) and every requirement line | "adds a [Royal Base]-in-text card that has no [Royal Base] trait…" (BT19-084 Winr, trait `LIBERATOR`, only mention is in its `[Main]` effect text) and "spends two distinct physical cards…" (BT23-042, matched through its trait) |

Engine seam: `matchNameOrTrait` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:296`
treats `match: "text"` as the union of effective names, traits (substring) and every printed text
field (`effectText`, `inheritedEffectText`, `securityEffectText`, `linkEffect`, `linkRequirement`,
`dualEffect`, `optionEffect`). Requirement lines live inside `effectText` in this catalog, so the
requirement axis of Q5301 is covered by that union.

Untestable axis: no card in the catalog carries `Royal Base` **only** in `inheritedEffectText`
(checked across all 4388 catalog entries), so the inherited-text axis of Q5301 cannot be
discriminated with real data for this token. Same for a requirement-only card: all four
`Royal Base`-in-text-without-the-trait cards (BT19-084, BT23-083, EX11-063, P-230) carry it in a
`[Main]` / `[On Play]` body, not in a requirement header.

#### Defects found

No defect in `BT23-038.ts`. The IR matches every printed clause; `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []`.

Defects in the previous test file, all fixed here:

1. The reveal clause was driven by `advance(...).fire(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)` — injected timing,
   no behavioral credit. Replaced with the public `playCard` intent, asserting memory paid and the
   permanent on the board.
2. The reveal test never proved the overlapping pools. Its `royalBaseText` and `cs` fixtures were
   disjoint, so a "same card added twice" bug would not have been caught. Two new tests cover the
   overlap (BT23-042 matches both slots) in both directions.
3. The remainder-order assertion was vacuous: exactly one card remained. Now two remain, under an
   untouched fourth deck card, and the exact bottom order is asserted.
4. The digivolution test used `battleArea: [{ card: "EX11-003" }]` — a Lv.2 Digi-Egg placed in the
   battle area, an illegal fixture. It also only asserted `{ ok: true }`, never the cost, so it
   passed while the engine charged the printed cost 1 instead of the printed alternate cost 0.
   Rebuilt as a breeding-area evolution with memory, stack identity and the digivolution draw
   asserted, plus two negatives.
5. The security-aura test set `s.inst(...).faceUp = true` by hand and compared against a captured
   `currentDP` rather than an absolute value, so a zero-delta bug would have been invisible. It also
   had no face-down control and no removal control. All three are now absolute-value assertions.
6. The inherited-DP test preassembled `under: ["BT23-038"]` under BT23-046 Rosemon, a Lv.6 that
   BT23-038 cannot legally sit under, and asserted only a delta. Replaced with a real evolution
   chain into BT23-042 Waspmon plus a real battle and a control.

#### Notable engine behavior (not a defect)

`applyIntent(..., { type: "digivolve" })` uses the 0-cost alternate route only when the intent sets
`useAlternateCost: true`. When both a printed evo cost and an alternate requirement match the same
base, the controller chooses (`apps/api/src/engine/actions/digivolve.ts:493-503`). Both branches are
now proved, so a regression that silently forces one route goes red.

#### Remaining gaps

- The inherited-text and requirement-header axes of Q5301 cannot be discriminated for the
  `[Royal Base]` token with real catalog data (see above). Covered by the shared `matchNameOrTrait`
  union instead of a card-level fixture.
- The aura's reach into the breeding area is not asserted. Whether "all of your Digimon" includes a
  breeding-area Digimon is a general targeting question owned by the engine lane, not by this card.
- The removal control moves the security card to the trash directly and recomputes, rather than
  resolving a real security check. A full security-check route would also fire BT23-038's other
  clauses and would not isolate the aura teardown.

### BT23-039 — Perorimon

#### Catalog and rules

`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` records a Green level-3 Stnd./Appmon, play cost 3, 1000 DP, attribute `Entertainment`, type `Gourmet`, normal evolution from Green level 2 for 0.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[Appmon] trait: Cost 0` — alternate evolution route.
2. `[On Play] Reveal the top 3 cards of your deck. Add 1 card with the [Appmon] trait and 1 card with the [Game] or [Invincible (App Name)] trait among them to the hand. Return the rest to the bottom of the deck.`
3. `[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 1`, link DP 2000.
4. `[When Linking] You may suspend 1 of your opponent's Digimon.`

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-039` returns no entries. No Q&A ids are indexed for this card in `KB-INDEX.md`, so there is no ruling to cover or to contradict.

#### Implementation trace

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-039.ts` only calls `registerIrCard("BT23-039", compiled)`; behavior comes from the shared interpreter.

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate evolution | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 2, traits: ["Appmon"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` | hatched off-color level-2 Appmon route plus level-2 non-Appmon negative |
| On Play reveal | `RevealAdd` `revealCount: 3`, two `add` slots (`Appmon` trait; `Game`/`Invincible` trait), `rest: "deckBottom"` | four public play tests (two distinct adds, Invincible branch, single overlapping card, no match) |
| Link cost/DP | `linkRequirement: [{ cost: 1, traits: ["Appmon"] }]`, catalog `linkDp: 2000` | link test asserts memory 3 -> 2 and host DP +2000; non-Appmon host rejection |
| When Linking suspend | `AllTurns` + `isLinked`, `SubTrigger whenLinked` with `sourceFilter.isSelfRef`, optional `Suspend` on `controller: "opponent"` | suspend test (own Digimon untouched), refusal test, no-legal-target test, replacement test |

Distinct physical selection across the two add slots is enforced by `runRevealAdd` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/reveal.ts`: each slot filters `!taken.has(...)`, so one card can satisfy only one slot. Trait comparison is exact-token in `matching/definition.ts` (`ref.match === "trait"` compares normalized whole traits), so `[Game]` and `[Invincible]` cannot substring-match unrelated traits.

#### Behavioral proof

All entry points are public intents (`playCard`, `linkCard`, `hatchEgg`, `digivolve`). No injected timing API is used; the previous `advance.fire(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)` reveal test was replaced with a real play.

Reveal fixtures use real catalog traits: `BT21-009` Gatchmon (Appmon only), `BT23-016` Dokamon (Appmon + `Game` attribute), `BT23-024` Poseidomon (Appmon + `Invincible` type), `BT1-009` (neither). Every case asserts the physical instance ids in hand, the exact remaining deck order, the memory paid, and `assertNoLoudGap`.

The evolution case now uses the only legal source route: a level-2 Appmon is a Digi-Egg card, so the test hatches `BT23-001` Flickmon (Blue) from the egg deck with `hatchEgg`, then digivolves Perorimon in breeding for 0 and asserts the stack identity, cost 0 and the single evolution bonus draw. The negative hatches `BT21-003` Yokomon (Blue level-2 non-Appmon) and expects `invalid-evolution`, which isolates the alternate route from the Green normal route.

Defect fixed: the previous evolution test claimed an "off-color level-2 Appmon" but used `BT23-002` Yokomon — Green and non-Appmon — so it actually exercised the printed Green normal evolution and proved nothing about the Appmon alternate. It also seeded the level-2 card straight into the battle area, which no legal route produces.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-039.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, **13 tests passed**, 2.1 s.
- Mutation check (reverted): setting the second add filter to a nonexistent trait and flipping the suspend target to `controller: "mine"` failed 5 tests, including both public reveal tests and the suspend test. The assertions are load-bearing.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit` — no errors in `BT23-039.ts` / `BT23-039.test.ts` (the pre-existing `src/engine/combat/controller.ts` failure belongs to the engine lane).
- `pnpm exec oxlint` — one `no-explicit-any` warning on the IR-shape cast, matching the accepted style in BT23-016/BT23-028.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt` — formatted. `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Remaining gaps

- The second add filter carries a dead token: `"Invincible (App Name)"` never matches, because trait normalization only strips spaces and hyphens, so the parenthesised form is not a real trait. The live token `"Invincible"` does the work and `BT23-024` proves it. No behavior is wrong, so the token was left in place as printed-text provenance.
- Slot order is fixed (Appmon first, then Game/Invincible). A player who spends the only overlapping card on the first slot loses the second add. That matches the printed sequence and is the player's choice, but it is not separately proven as a rules question because no ruling addresses it.
- No retained red tests and no engine seam suspected for this card.
- No catalog discrepancy found: the committed text matches the official wording of all four clauses.

### BT23-040 — Wormmon

Sources: committed catalog `BT23-040`; KB Q5302; comprehensive rules 4-6-8 and 8-1-2-1.

#### Card summary

Green Rookie Digimon, level 3, play cost 4, DP 3000, traits Larva/Hudie/CS. Printed evolution cost is green level 2 for 1.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS] trait: Cost 0` — alternate requirement.
2. `[Start of Your Main Phase] By placing 1 of your [Erika Mishima] as this Digimon's bottom digivolution card, this Digimon may digivolve into [Hudiemon] in the hand or trash with the digivolution cost reduced by 2.`
3. Inherited: `[All Turns] All of your Digimon with the [Hudie] trait get +1000 DP.`

Q&A covered: Q5302 (the Erika placement cost takes her only from the battle area, never from hand or trash).

#### Defects found and fixed in the card module

All four are local to `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-040.ts`. No engine or catalog file was touched.

| Defect | Was | Now | Why |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Substring name matching | `match: "name"` for `[Erika Mishima]` and `[Hudiemon]` | `match: "nameExact"` | A printed bracketed card name is an exact reference. Substring mode would also accept any longer name containing it. |
| Erika's own stack rode along | no `shedOwnCards` | `shedOwnCards: true` on the place cost | Erika leaves the battle area when she becomes a digivolution card, so cards under her are trashed (CR 4-6-8). The old IR attached them to the new stack instead. Same shape as BT12-027, BT12-083, BT12-102. |
| Double refusal | `cost.optional: true` plus `optional: true` | `optional: true` only | With both flags the engine asks twice: pay Erika, then decline the digivolution — the controller could throw Erika away for nothing. The printed clause has one "may". |
| Forced alternate requirement | `useAlternateCost: true` | field removed | Hudiemon has both a printed green Lv.3 requirement (cost 5) and an alternate CS Lv.3 requirement (cost 4). CR 8-1-2-1 gives the choice to the player; the interpreter already opens that `chooseOption` when the field is absent. Forcing the alternate silently charged 2 where the controller may want the printed route. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog fields, alternate requirement | "matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause" | `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Start of Main IR shape, exact names | "compiles the printed Start of Main clause with exact names and a mandatory shedding cost" | `StartOfYourMainPhase` action + `matchNameOrTrait` seam |
| Cost source, bottom placement, shed, printed cost less 2, one bonus draw | "places the battle-area Erika at the bottom of a real stack..." | `cost.place` + `reduceCost: 2` |
| Requirement choice | "lets the controller take the alternate CS requirement instead, for one memory less" | absence of `useAlternateCost` |
| Trash source for the result | "digivolves into a Hudiemon in the trash when the hand holds none" | `from: ["hand","trash"]` |
| Q5302 zone gate | "cannot pay with an Erika Mishima in the hand or the trash (Q5302)" | `cost.target.filter.zone: "battleArea"` |
| Exact name and controller gate | "cannot pay with another Hudie CS Tamer or with the opponent's Erika" | `nameExact` + `controller: "mine"` |
| Optional refusal | "keeps Erika and her stack on the field when the controller declines" | `optional` + `abortOnDecline` |
| No payload, no payment | "does not pay the cost when no Hudiemon is available to digivolve into" | `canAttemptDigivolve` gate |
| Own-turn only, and re-arms next own turn | "never fires on the opponent's Main phase" | `StartOfYourMainPhase` trigger |
| Inherited aura across peers, opponents and source removal | "inherits +1000 DP for every own Hudie Digimon and loses it when the source leaves" | `AllTurns` `ModifyDP`, `isInherited` |
| Inherited IR shape | "inherits the all-Hudie continuous DP bonus" | same |
| Alternate Lv.2 CS route and negative | "digivolves for 0 from a level-2 CS Digi-Egg in breeding and rejects a non-CS egg" | `digivolutionRequirement` |

#### How the behavioral cases are driven

Every dynamic case uses public intents and the production turn loop. Wormmon's alternate requirement names a level-2 CS source, and every level-2 card is a Digi-Egg, so the only legal stack starts in breeding: the test publicly digivolves Wormmon over BT23-002 Yokomon in the breeding area for 0, then moves it out with `moveFromBreeding`, then runs `runOneTurn()` and waits for the real Main phase so `[Start of Your Main Phase]` fires naturally. The earlier committed test placed BT23-002 in the battle area, which is not a legal Digi-Egg zone (the turn loop trashes it), and reached the timing with `advance().fire(...)`.

Endpoints asserted in the main positive case: stack instance order `[Erika, Yokomon, Wormmon]` with Hudiemon's exact hand instance on top; Erika's permanent gone from the battle area; the exact card that was under her in trash; memory 3 (5 at Main, +1 from Erika's own start-of-main gain, then 5 - 2 = 3 paid for the printed requirement); hand exactly the draw-phase card plus the single evolution bonus draw; `assertNoLoudGap`.

Memory is read while Main is still open, because ending Main hands memory to the opponent. Erika's own `[Start of Your Main Phase]` memory gain resolves first under the harness's default trigger ordering; each memory assertion states that +1 explicitly.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-040.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, 13 tests passed, 2.25s (`logs/BT23-040-vitest.log`).
- Set regression: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23 --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 97/103 files passed, 8 failures (`logs/BT23-040-set-regression.log`). Seven of those failures are in other lanes' in-flight files (BT23-013, 041, 044, 101, 102) and reproduce with this card's module reverted. The eighth is `BT23-catalog-sync.test.ts`: the persisted `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` record for BT23-040 no longer matches the module. That file is coordinator-owned; BT23-029, 031 and 035 are listed in the same mismatch.
- Typecheck: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no errors in `BT23-040.ts` or `BT23-040.test.ts`. Pre-existing unrelated errors remain in `src/engine/combat/controller.ts` and other lanes' files.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` clean; `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` clean; `git diff --check` clean.

#### Remaining gaps

- No printed card today has a name that contains "Erika Mishima" or "Hudiemon" as a strict substring, so the exact-name regression is asserted at the `matchNameOrTrait` seam rather than through a board fixture. A behavioral fixture becomes possible only when such a card is printed.
- The card placed under Erika in the shed test is laid by the Board Spec. Erika herself prints no effect that stacks cards under her; the engine has a dedicated place-under-Tamer primitive that other cards use, and the assertion under test is the destination of those cards, not how they got there.
- `effects.json` sync for this card is not done here (forbidden for this lane).

### BT23-041 — Kabuterimon

#### Card

Catalog `BT23-041`: Green/Yellow level 4 Digimon, play cost 5, DP 5000, Champion, Vaccine,
traits Insectoid/Hudie/CS, evolution Green level 3 for 3 and Yellow level 3 for 3.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2` (alternate route, off-color allowed).
2. `＜Alliance＞`.
3. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon suspends, 1 of your Digimon gains
   ＜Piercing＞ and +3000 DP for the turn.`

Knowledge base: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-041` returns no entries, and
`KB-INDEX.md` lists 0 Q&A for this card. No ruling to cover. Rules used:
`comprehensive §16-24-1` for ＜Alliance＞ and `§16-7` for the ＜Piercing＞ security check.

The catalog text carries a non-breaking space in `w/[CS] trait`; the test asserts that
exact form. No catalog discrepancy found.

#### Card module

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-041.ts` is unchanged. Its IR already matches the printed text:
one `Static` effect carrying the `Alliance` keyword, one `AllTurns` effect with
`frequency: "OncePerTurn"` wrapping a `whenSuspended` SubTrigger whose `sourceFilter` is
`isSelfRef`, granting `Piercing` and `+3000` `forTheTurn` to the same
`controller: "mine", kind: ["Digimon"]` target (`sameTarget: true`), plus the alternate
`digivolutionRequirement` `{ level: 3, traits: ["CS"], cost: 2, isAlternate: true }`.
No defect found, so no edit was needed.

#### Clause -> test -> IR map

| Clause | Test | IR / engine seam |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Suspension by attacking grants both effects | "suspends by attacking, then the chosen Digimon wins its battle at +3000 DP and pierces for a security check" | `AllTurns` SubTrigger `whenSuspended`; `combat/controller.fireSuspended` |
| ＜Piercing＞ actually pierces | same test (second attack: 3000 + 3000 beats a 4000 DP defender, then one more security card is checked) | `GainKeyword Piercing`; `resolveDigimonBattle` pierce hand-off |
| `[All Turns]` covers the opponent's turn and an opponent's effect | "also triggers when an opponent's effect suspends it during the opponent's turn" (opponent plays BT1-110 Flower Cannon) | `Suspend` primitive -> `whenSuspended` bus |
| `[Once Per Turn]` | "grants only once in a turn even when it suspends a second time" (attack, unsuspend publicly with BT4-108, attack again) | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Once-per-turn reset and `for the turn` expiry | "expires the grant at the turn boundary and triggers again on the next turn" (real `runOneTurn` loop, own turn -> opponent turn -> own turn) | `duration: "forTheTurn"`; `TurnStateMachine.clearDurations` |
| Recipient is "1 of **your** Digimon" | "offers only the controller's own Digimon and rejects an opponent's Digimon as the recipient" | `target.filter.controller: "mine"` |
| Self-ref subject only | "does not react when another Digimon suspends" | `sourceFilter.isSelfRef` |
| ＜Alliance＞ | "exposes Alliance on the live board" and "suspends an ally for Alliance without re-triggering its own suspension clause" | `Static.keywords`; `combat/controller.resolveAllianceEffect` |
| Digivolve routes | "digivolves from a Green/Yellow level-3 source on the normal route" (BT23-037, cost 3) and "... an off-color level-3 [CS] source on the alternate route" (BT23-017 Blue/Purple CS, cost 2) | `evoCosts`; `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Illegal source | "rejects a level-3 source that is neither Green/Yellow nor [CS]" on both routes (BT1-009) | same |

#### Evidence detail

- The behavioral core is one attack chain. Kabuterimon attacks the player, so it suspends
  through the production combat path; the trigger's target prompt is biased to the ally
  (BT1-009, 3000 DP). Endpoints asserted: Kabuterimon suspended, ally has ＜Piercing＞ and
  6000 DP, Kabuterimon unchanged at 5000, one security card gone. The ally then attacks a
  suspended 4000 DP BT1-014 — a battle it wins only because of the +3000 — and its
  ＜Piercing＞ removes a second security card. Final endpoints: opponent battle area empty,
  ally still on the board, opponent security 3 -> 1, no pending decision.
- Every attack by Kabuterimon opens an ＜Alliance＞ prompt (§16-24-1 puts the keyword on the
  attacker), so each test answers it explicitly with `respondAlliance`. The Alliance test
  accepts and asserts the ally suspends, the opponent takes 2 security checks instead of 1
  (the extra ＜Security A. +1＞), and the suspension clause still fired exactly once — the
  ally's suspension is not the source's own.
- The turn test drives real turns (`runOneTurn` + `waitForMainPhase` + `endPhase`). It
  asserts the grant is present during the granting turn, gone once that turn closes, still
  gone through the opponent's whole turn, and granted again after a fresh attack on the next
  own turn.
- The invalid-target branch does not use auto-selection. It reads the live `chooseTargets`
  request, asserts the candidate list holds both own Digimon and not the opponent's, and
  shows the engine refusing a forged opponent id. Note the refusal code is the generic
  `decision-pending`: `engine/decisions/index.ts` `satisfiesMin` drops ids outside the
  candidate set, so the response never reaches a target validator. The decision stays open
  and the legal answer then resolves it. This is an engine-wide reporting coarseness, not a
  card defect, and is left as-is (engine files are out of this lane's scope).
- Fixtures are legal: security stacks hold main-deck Digimon (BT1-010), no Digi-Eggs are
  placed outside breeding, and every pre-laid battle-area Digimon is a card that could have
  been played from hand.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-041.test.ts
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, 14 tests, all passed (~123 ms of test
  time). Log: `logs/BT23-041-vitest.log`.
- Peer/stack: same command over `BT23-041`, `BT23-029` (evolves from BT23-041 and shares the
  Alliance path), `BT1-110`, `BT4-108` (the two Options used as suspension/unsuspension
  vehicles) and `src/engine/combat/controller.test.ts` — 5 files, 82 tests passed. Log:
  `logs/BT23-041-peer.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no errors in `BT23-041.ts` / `BT23-041.test.ts`.
  Two pre-existing errors in `src/cards/BT23/BT23-045.test.ts` belong to another lane.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` on both files: only the two `no-explicit-any` warnings that the accepted
  BT23-028/BT23-030 IR-shape style also produces. `pnpm exec oxfmt` applied; `git diff
  --check` clean.

#### Defects and gaps

- No card-module defect found; `BT23-041.ts` is unedited.
- No retained red tests, no skips.
- Gap: the ＜Alliance＞ DP bonus and ＜Security A. +1＞ are attack-scoped, so after the attack
  resolves only the extra security check is observable. The test asserts the security-count
  endpoint and that the attacker's DP is back to 5000, rather than the mid-attack DP.
- Gap: the forged-target refusal is asserted as `decision-pending`. A more precise
  `invalid-target` code would need an engine change in `engine/decisions/index.ts`.

### BT23-042 — Waspmon

#### Card summary

Green/Black Lv.4 Champion, play cost 4, DP 4000. Traits: Cyborg, X Antibody, Royal Base, CS.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[Royal Base]/[CS] trait: Cost 2` — alternate digivolution requirement.
2. `[Security] [All Turns] All of your [Royal Base] trait Digimon get +1000 DP.`
3. `[When Digivolving] If you have 1 or fewer Tamers, you may play 1 Tamer card with [Royal Base] in its text from your hand without paying the cost.`
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP.`

Catalog text matches the compiled IR. No catalog discrepancy found.

#### Rulings covered

- **Q5303** — "X in its text" spans name, traits, effects, inherited effects, requirements.
  The IR uses `nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["Royal Base"], match: "text" }]`, and
  `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts` resolves `match: "text"`
  as the union of names, traits and every text field — the ruling's reading.
  The behavioral proof deliberately uses a **text-only** positive: BT23-083 Fei is a Tamer
  with traits `Zaxon`/`CS` (no `Royal Base` trait) whose printed effect names `[Royal Base]`.
  A trait-only positive is not available: the catalog holds no Tamer with the `Royal Base`
  trait, so the trait half of the union is untestable from the card pool.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, alternate requirement | "matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause" | `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Alternate Lv.3 route, cost 2 | "digivolves from level-3 BT23-038 (alternate=true) leaving 1 memory" | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| Printed Green/Black route, cost 3 | "digivolves from level-3 BT23-038 (alternate=false) leaving 0 memory" | printed `evoCosts` |
| Alternate route from a CS-only Lv.3 | "digivolves from level-3 BT23-037 (alternate=true) leaving 2 memory" | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| Illegal source negative | "rejects a level-3 source without the Royal Base or CS trait" | — |
| Free Tamer play, text-only match (Q5303) | "plays a Tamer that only names Royal Base in its text, for free, from hand" | `PlayWithoutCost`, `payCost: false`, `from: ["hand"]` |
| Tamer-count boundary 1 vs 2 | "still plays the Tamer at exactly one own Tamer and stops at two" | `condition.permanentCount lte 1` |
| Controller boundary | "counts only your own Tamers, not the opponent's" | `condition.filter.controllerDefault: "mine"` |
| Optional refusal | "keeps the Tamer in hand when the controller declines" | `optional: true` |
| Nonmatching and out-of-zone rejection | "ignores a nonmatching Tamer in hand and a matching Tamer in the trash" | target filter, `from: ["hand"]` |
| Security aura, friendly-only, both turns | "boosts only friendly Royal Base Digimon from face-up security, on both turns" | `isSecurity`, `controller: "mine"`, trait gate |
| Face-down exclusion | "does not boost while the security card stays face down" | `isSecurity` |
| Inherited DP through real evolution | "carries the inherited +1000 DP after a real evolution" | `isInherited` |
| IR shape (structural) | three `compiled.*` shape tests | all effects |

Endpoints asserted: exact memory after each digivolve, physical instance ids across
hand/trash/battle area, stack contents and top-card identity, the digivolution bonus draw,
exact DP values, and `pendingDecision === undefined` after every case settles.

#### Engine behavior confirmed during the audit

- When both the printed EvoCost and the alternate requirement match one source, the
  `digivolve` intent must set `useAlternateCost: true` to pay the alternate cost. This is the
  documented intent contract (`apps/api/src/engine/actions/digivolve.ts:65`), not a defect.
  The previous test file never exercised either path through a public intent, so this was
  not visible.
- The permanent's `stack` holds the under-cards only; `topCard` is separate.

#### Defects found

- The previous `BT23-042.test.ts` proved the `[When Digivolving]` clause by injecting the

### BT23-043 — CannonBeemon

#### Card summary

Green/Black Lv.5 Ultimate Digimon. Play cost 8, DP 8000. Traits: Cyborg, X Antibody, Royal Base, CS, Insectoid. Printed evolution costs: Green Lv.4 for 4, Black Lv.4 for 4.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[Royal Base]/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate evolution route.
2. `[Security] [Opponent's Turn] All of your Digimon with the [Royal Base] trait gain ＜Blocker＞`.
3. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon would leave the battle area other than by your effects, by flipping your top face-up security card face down, it doesn't leave.`
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When any of your [Royal Base] trait Digimon would leave the battle area other than by your effects, by flipping your top face-up security card face down, 1 of those Digimon doesn't leave.`

Catalog text matches the official card list. No discrepancy found; `cards.json` was not edited.

#### Q&A covered

| Q&A | Subject | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5304 | Concurrent All Delete / QueenBeemon (BT19-053) ordering | "leaves QueenBeemon's security placement available for the Digimon it did not save (Q5304)" |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, alternate requirement, full coverage | "matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause" | `digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| Security Blocker aura, structure | "grants Blocker to all of your Royal Base Digimon in Security" | effect `trigger: OpponentsTurn`, `isSecurity: true`, `GainKeyword` |
| Aura scope (friendly Royal Base only) | "grants Blocker live only to friendly Royal Base Digimon on the opponent's turn" | `target.filter.controller: "mine"` + `nameOrTrait` trait match |
| Aura boundaries: own turn, face-down security copy | "grants nothing on your own turn or from a face-down security copy" | `trigger: OpponentsTurn` + face-up security gate |
| Aura is usable: public block | "lets the granted Royal Base Digimon actually block an opponent's attack on the player" | `GainKeyword Blocker` |
| Aura negative: non-Royal-Base cannot block | "does not let a non-Royal-Base Digimon block on the strength of this Security effect" | trait filter |
| Main replacement structure | "prevents this Digimon from leaving except by its owner's effects" | `Replacement/wouldLeavePlay`, `sourceFilter.isSelfRef`, `leaveCause: otherThanYourEffect`, `frequency: OncePerTurn` |
| Cost targets the TOP FACE-UP card; stack order preserved | "flips the TOP FACE-UP security card, skipping face-down cards above it, and keeps the stack order" | `cost.kind: flipSecurity`, `position: top`, `faceUp: true` |
| Own-effect exclusion | "does not protect itself from its own controller's effect" | `leaveCause: otherThanYourEffect` |
| Unpayable cost | "cannot pay without a face-up security card, so the deletion goes through" | all-or-nothing `flipSecurity` |
| Optional refusal | "leaves the battle area when its controller declines the optional prevention" | `optional: true`, `abortOnDecline: true` |
| Once per turn refusal | "prevents only once per turn and recovers the ability on the next turn" | `frequency: OncePerTurn` |
| Once per turn reset | "resets the once-per-turn prevention on a later turn" | `frequency: OncePerTurn` |
| Inherited replacement structure and single-target scope | "inherits protection for one qualifying Royal Base Digimon" | `isInherited`, `target.count: 1`, no `affectsAll` |
| Inherited prevention pays and saves | "uses its inherited effect to flip security and preserve another Royal Base Digimon" | inherited `Replacement` |
| Saves exactly one of two threatened Digimon | "saves exactly one of two simultaneously threatened Royal Base Digimon" | `target.count: 1` + `OncePerTurn` |
| Trait boundary | "does not extend inherited protection to a non-Royal-Base Digimon" | `nameOrTrait` trait match |
| Controller boundary | "does not extend inherited protection to the opponent's Royal Base Digimon" | `controller: "mine"` |
| Q5304 ordering | "leaves QueenBeemon's security placement available for the Digimon it did not save (Q5304)" | prevention + BT19-053 `instead` replacement |
| Alternate route, cost 3, one bonus draw, source stack | "digivolves for 3 from a level-4 Royal Base/CS card (BT23-042 / BT23-041)" | `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Printed route boundary (cost 4) | "charges the printed Green level-4 cost of 4 when the alternate route is not declared" | `evoCosts` |
| Illegal source negative | "rejects a level-4 source without the Royal Base or CS trait" | `digivolutionRequirement.traits` |

Deletions in the replacement tests run through the production `deletePermanent` primitive inside an explicit `enterEffectResolution(seat)` window, so the "other than by your effects" gate sees the real resolving controller rather than a turn-seat fallback. The Blocker and evolution proofs use public intents (`attack`, `declareBlock`, `digivolve`).

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-043.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
  - 1 file, 23 tests, 23 passed, ~2.1 s. Log: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-043-vitest.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no errors in `BT23-043.ts` or `BT23-043.test.ts`. Pre-existing failures in other lanes' files (`BT23-037.test.ts`, `BT23-040.test.ts`, `engine/combat/controller.ts`) remain and were not touched.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` — only the repo-wide `no-explicit-any` warnings on structural IR assertions, matching the accepted BT23 test style.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` — clean after formatting.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-catalog-sync.test.ts src/engine/deckCardAuditBT23.test.ts` — 290/291 pass. The one failure lists `BT23-029, BT23-031, BT23-035, BT23-040, BT23-044` as unsynchronized persisted IR; BT23-043 is not among them, so this is a pre-existing gap owned by other lanes.

#### Defects found and fixed

- `BT23-043.ts` opened with `// @ts-nocheck`, so the compiled IR was never type-checked. Removed. The module type-checks cleanly without it; no IR change was needed.
- The previous test suite had three material weaknesses, all replaced:
  - It relied on the turn seat to stand in for the resolving effect controller, so the own-effect exclusion was proved only indirectly.
  - It used `BT23-015` in security. Security fixtures now use real neutral main-deck Digimon (`BT1-009`..`BT1-012`).
  - The evolution test asserted only `{ ok: true }` — no cost, source stack, or draw. It also silently exercised the printed cost-4 route, not the printed cost-3 alternate route, because the intent did not declare `useAlternateCost` / `alternateRequirementIndex`.

#### Behavioral findings worth the coordinator's attention

1. **The cost already flips the top FACE-UP card, not the top card.** `primitives.flipTopSecurity` scans from the top for the first face-up card. Proved with two face-down cards above two face-up ones: index 2 flipped, indices 0, 1 and 3 unchanged, stack order and instance identity preserved.

2. **Q5304 result, with one open interpretation question.** With All Delete-style simultaneous deletion of CannonBeemon, QueenBeemon (BT19-053) and TigerVespamon, and the controller ordering CannonBeemon's prevention first:
   - CannonBeemon's prevention pays (top face-up security flipped face down) and the deletion is prevented.
   - QueenBeemon's placement is **not** erased: it still runs afterward, and every Royal Base Digimon affected by the deletion effect reaches the bottom of security face up in battle-area order. Nothing reaches the trash.
   - Open question: the engine also sweeps CannonBeemon itself into security through QueenBeemon's placement, because CannonBeemon was affected by the deletion effect. Q5304's wording ("you can place all of your [Royal Base] trait Digimon that were affected by the deletion effect into your security stack") does not exclude the prevented Digimon, so this reads as correct — but it is not stated outright. The test asserts the current behavior and names it in a comment. If the coordinator reads the ruling as excluding the prevented Digimon, this becomes an engine defect in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/leavePrevention.ts` (`consultLeavePrevention` runs an `instead` replacement even after a `prevent` already succeeded for the same permanent: the `preventSucceeded` guard is checked only on the `prevent` branch, after the `instead` branch).

3. **Ordering must be driven, not defaulted.** When both CannonBeemon's prevention and QueenBeemon's placement are eligible for the same permanent, `GameEngine.orderReplacements` raises an `orderTriggers` decision. The harness's default `autoOrderTriggers` keeps the offered order, which puts QueenBeemon first and hides CannonBeemon's prevention entirely. The Q5304 test answers that decision explicitly. Sibling lanes touching this family should do the same.

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained red tests. No `it.fails` / `it.skip` in this file.
- The Blocker aura is proved on a battle-area Royal Base Digimon while CannonBeemon sits face up in security. Blocking with a Digimon that gains the keyword *through* a different route in the same turn is not covered; it is not a printed clause of this card.
- The once-per-turn reset is proved by advancing a full production turn with `advance.runTurn` and asserting `turnCount` advanced, then re-running the prevention. It does not separately prove a reset across a *change of turn player*, because `runOneTurn` from a hand-laid mid-turn board does not reliably hand the turn over. The once-per-turn ledger is turn-start-scoped in `GameEngine` (`tracker` reset), so this is a fixture limit, not a behavior gap.
- Q5304 is the only indexed ruling for this card, and it is covered.

### BT23-044 — Lilamon

#### Card summary

Sources: committed catalog `BT23-044`; KB Q5305, Q5306; comprehensive rules 3-4-5-8.

Green Digimon, level 5, play cost 7, DP 7000, evo Green level 4 for 3, Data, Ultimate, traits `Fairy` and `CS`.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate digivolution route.
2. "When this card would be played from the hand, if you have [Yuuko Kamishiro] or a [CS] trait Digimon, reduce the play cost by 3."
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` "By suspending 1 Digimon, until your opponent's turn ends, their effects can't return 1 of your Digimon with [Vegetation], [Plant] or [Fairy] in any of its traits or the [CS] trait to hands or decks."
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn]` "When this Digimon deletes your opponent's Digimon in battle, trash their top security card."

Rulings covered:

| Ruling | Content | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5305 | The suspend cost may suspend either player's Digimon | "pays the entry cost by suspending an opponent's Digimon and offers no Tamer as a candidate" |
| Q5306 | The inherited effect does not activate when this Digimon and the opponent's Digimon are deleted at the same time | "does not trash security when both battling Digimon are deleted" |

#### Clause to test to IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cost reduction, condition met | "charges 4 memory with %s in the battle area, otherwise 7" (Yuuko row, CS row) | `Static` → `Replacement wouldBePlayed` → nested `reduceCost 3` with `youHave` |
| Cost reduction, condition absent | same test, "neither" row | same |
| Cost reduction, breeding area does not count | "ignores a CS Digimon in the breeding area..." | `condition.filter.zone: "battleArea"` |
| Suspend cost, either player's Digimon, no Tamers | "pays the entry cost by suspending an opponent's Digimon..." | `cost: { kind: "suspend", target.filter: { controller: "any", kind: ["Digimon"] } }` |
| Protection target is "1 of your Digimon" with the listed traits | same test (candidate list excludes the [CS] Tamer) | `Restrict.target.filter: { controller: "mine", kind: ["Digimon"], or: [...] }` |
| Optional entry effect, decline branch | "leaves every Digimon unsuspended and unprotected when the entry effect is declined" | `optional: true, abortOnDecline: true` |
| `[When Digivolving]`, protection against an opponent effect, expiry | "digivolves for 3 on the Green route, draws 1 and protects itself..." | `WhenDigivolving` → `Restrict cannotReturnToHandOrDeck`, `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` |
| Same board without the protection (negative control) | "lets an opponent's return effect move the same Digimon when the entry effect is declined" | same |
| "their effects" — the controller's own return effects still work | "still lets its own controller return the protected Digimon with their own effect" | `byOpponentEffectsOnly: true` |
| Alternate digivolve route | "digivolves from an off-color CS level 4 for 3 on the alternate route, with or without the flag" | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 4, traits: ["CS"], cost: 3, isAlternate: true }]` |
| Illegal source | "rejects a level 3 non-CS source" | same |
| Inherited security trash | "trashes the opponent's top security after its carrier deletes a Digimon in battle" | `AllTurns` inherited → `SubTrigger whenDeletesInBattle` → `SecurityManipulation trashTop` |
| Q5306 mutual deletion | "does not trash security when both battling Digimon are deleted" | same |
| `[Once Per Turn]` refusal and reset | "trashes security once per turn and again on the next turn" | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Security Digimon battle is not a battle-area deletion | "does not trash security after its carrier wins against a Security Digimon" | same |

#### Defects found and fixed (all local to `BT23-044.ts`)

1. **Breeding area counted toward "if you have ... a [CS] trait Digimon".** The generated `youHave` filter carried no `zone`, and the zone-less scan in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/scaling.ts:95-100` walks the battle area *and* the breeding area. Comprehensive rules 3-4-5-8 forbids referencing information on cards in breeding areas. Fixed by adding `zone: "battleArea"`. Red without the fix: the play cost dropped to 4 with only a hatched CS Digimon.
2. **A `[CS]` trait Tamer was offered as a protection target.** The `Restrict` target filter had no `kind`, but the printed text says "1 of your Digimon". With Yuuko Kamishiro (a `[CS]` Tamer) on the board, she appeared in the `chooseTargets` candidate list and could absorb the protection. Fixed by adding `kind: ["Digimon"]`. Red without the fix.
3. **The protection blocked the controller's own return effects.** "their effects can't return" is opponent-scoped. `restriction: "cannotReturnToHandOrDeck"` normalizes to `beReturned` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/restrictions.ts:62-68`, and that path only records the opponent-only scope when the IR action carries `byOpponentEffectsOnly`. Fixed by adding it to both trigger copies. Red without the fix: the controller's own BT4-102 Aqua Viper could not return the protected Digimon.
4. **`[Yuuko Kamishiro]` matched as a substring.** `match: "name"` is a substring match (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:352`); a printed bracket name is exact (KB Q1231/Q1232). Changed to `match: "nameExact"`. This has no behavioral test because BT22-083 is currently the only card whose name contains "Yuuko Kamishiro", so both modes agree today; the structural assertion pins the mode.

#### Engine gap reported, not fixed

**Hand-origin gate on `wouldBePlayed` self-reducers is missing.** The printed condition is "when this card would be played **from the hand**". `WouldBePlayedSelfReducer` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/registration/reducers.ts:200-250`) records no origin zone, and `GameEngine.fireBeforePayCost` applies every collected reducer at `apps/api/src/engine/GameEngine.ts:4280` without consulting its `originZone` parameter. The two intent-driven call sites (`GameEngine.ts:5482`, `GameEngine.ts:6615`) pass `"hand"`, but `finalizeEffectPlayCost` (`GameEngine.ts:994-1006`) is reachable with `originZone` `"trash"` or `"security"`, and the reduction would then apply against the printed wording.

No retained red test is attached: a scan of the catalog found no implemented card that plays another card from trash or security *while paying its play cost*, so the wrong branch is currently unreachable through public intents. Every other card with the same printed wording (BT16-089, BT17-068, BT23-031, BT23-067, EX5-034, EX6-039, ST20-12/13, ST21-12/13) shares the gap, so the fix belongs to the engine lane, not this card.

#### Peer / stack proof

- The same restriction verb appears on BT22-058 Dreammon ("[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon gets linked, your opponent's effects can't return 1 of your Digimon to hands or decks until their turn ends") and BT24-056 Dezipmon. Both are candidates for the same `byOpponentEffectsOnly` review; neither is in this worker's scope.
- The alternate route and inherited effect are exercised on a real stack: the inherited tests place BT23-044 as a digivolution card under BT1-080 Titamon, and the digivolve tests build a real BT23-041 (Green level 4 CS) and BT22-022 (Blue level 4 CS) stack rather than pre-assembling one.
- The once-per-turn test drives the *defender* branch of `whenDeletesInBattle` (`apps/api/src/engine/combat/controller.ts:1688-1709`): two opponent attackers are deleted by the same suspended host inside one opponent turn, then a third deletion on the controller's next turn proves the reset.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-044.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, 19 tests passed, 2.13s. Log: `logs/BT23-044-vitest.log`.
- Fix-proof run with the four IR fixes reverted — 5 failed / 14 passed, confirming each behavioral fix has a red reproducer.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — clean.
- `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-044.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-044.test.ts` — 2 pre-existing `no-explicit-any` warnings on the structural IR assertions; no errors.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` on both files — clean.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/engine/deckCardAuditBT23.test.ts src/cards/BT23/BT23-catalog-sync.test.ts src/cards/BT23/BT23-046.test.ts src/cards/BT23/BT23-041.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 312 passed, 1 failed: `BT23-catalog-sync` now lists `BT23-044` among the module/`effects.json` mismatches (alongside BT23-029, -031, -035, -040, -045, -047 from other workers). `effects.json` is coordinator-owned; it needs a resync.

#### Remaining gaps

- No behavioral proof that `[Yuuko Kamishiro]` is exact-name matched; the catalog has no near-name card to discriminate with.
- The hand-origin gate on the cost reduction is untested for the reason above.
- The protection's "or the [CS] trait" and `[Vegetation]`/`[Plant]` branches are proved only through `[CS]` and `[Fairy]` (Lilamon and BT23-041). The other two trait tokens are asserted structurally.

### BT23-045 — TigerVespamon

Sources: committed catalog `BT23-045`; KB Q5307, Q5308, Q5309, Q5310, Q5331; comprehensive rules 15-6, 15-7, 15-9, 15-14-5.

#### Card

Green/Black level 6 Mega, play cost 8, DP 12000, Virus, traits Cyborg / X Antibody / Royal Base / Zaxon / CS / Insectoid, ACE with Overflow -4. Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[Royal Base]/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate route.
2. `[Hand] [Counter] ＜Blast Digivolve＞`.
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] By placing 1 [Royal Base] or [Zaxon] trait Digimon card from your hand or trash face up as the bottom security card, return 1 of your opponent's Digimon with as much or less DP as this Digimon to the hand.`
4. `[All Turns] When this Digimon suspends, by flipping your top face-up security card face down, 1 of your Digimon unsuspends.`

The catalog text matches the official card list. No catalog change was needed or made.

#### Defect found and fixed (Q5331)

The IR modelled the by-placement as unconditionally `optional: true` with `abortOnDecline`, so the controller could always refuse it. Q5331 says the opposite: once the effect activates the controller **must** process the "by" condition; only when no [Royal Base]/[Zaxon] Digimon card sits in the (public) trash may the controller decline, because the hand is hidden.

`BT23-045.ts` now emits two mutually exclusive `Return` actions per trigger, gated on the same `selfHasMinTrash` check:

- trash holds a qualifying card → mandatory action, cost payable from hand or trash;
- trash holds none → optional action with `abortOnDecline`, cost payable from hand only.

Exactly one branch can ever resolve. The literal ruling text says the placed card must be one of the trash cards; the module keeps hand and trash both selectable in the mandatory branch, since the ruling's point is that the payment cannot be refused, and forbidding the hand choice would remove a legal choice the rules never take away.

#### Clause -> test mapping

| Clause / ruling | Test | Endpoints asserted |
| --- | --- | --- |
| On Play placement + return | "pays the trash placement face up at security bottom…" | exact cost instance appended as last (bottom) security card with `faceUp: true`, gone from trash, exact opponent top-card instance in the opponent's hand, 13000-DP Digimon untouched, memory 10 -> 2, no pending decision |
| Q5331 mandatory branch | "keeps the trash placement mandatory even when the controller declines every prompt" | same endpoints under `autoDeclineOptional` |
| Q5331 optional branch | "may decline the placement when the only eligible cost card is in hand" | security list unchanged, exact hand instance still in hand, target still on board |
| Hand payment accepted | "pays a hand placement when the controller accepts…" | exact hand instance at security bottom, non-trait trash card untouched |
| No eligible cost card | "does nothing when no [Royal Base] or [Zaxon] Digimon card is available" | security unchanged, target still on board, no pending decision |
| DP bound / no legal target | "does not spend the placement when the opponent has no Digimon at 12000 DP or less" | cost card stays in trash, security unchanged |
| When Digivolving + alternate route | "takes the Lv.5 [Royal Base]/[CS] route for 3…" | `useAlternateCost: true` charges 3 (10 -> 7), BT23-043 stays as the single digivolution card, placement + return endpoints |
| Printed route | "charges the printed 4 when the alternate Lv.5 route is not declared" | memory 10 -> 6 |
| Illegal source | "refuses a level-4 [Royal Base] source on both the printed and the alternate route" | both intents rejected, memory unchanged, card still in hand |
| ＜Blast Digivolve＞ (attack ends) | "Blast Digivolves from hand through the [Counter] window, pays no memory, and ends the attack by returning the attacker" | `respondCounter` with `blast-digivolve:<permanentId>` closes the window, memory unchanged, BT23-043 stays as the single digivolution card, the placement lands face up at security bottom, the attacker returns to its owner's hand, the attack ends with no security check and no pending decision |
| ＜Blast Digivolve＞ (attack resolves) | "Blast Digivolves through the [Counter] window and lets the attack finish when another Digimon is returned" | same counter path with a bystander as the return target; the attacker survives, the attack checks exactly one security card, and the placed cost card stays at the bottom of the stack |
| All Turns unsuspend | "flips the top face-up security card face down to unsuspend one of your Digimon when it attacks" | real `attack` intent suspends the source, the exact top security card becomes `faceUp: false`, the chosen ally unsuspends |
| Cost unpayable | "cannot unsuspend while no security card is face up" | ally and source stay suspended, both security cards stay face down |
| Optional refusal | "may decline the security flip and leave the ally suspended" | security card stays face up, ally stays suspended |
| Q5307 | "leaves the placed card revealed in the security stack across the turn boundary" | the placed card is still the bottom card and still face up after the turn passes to the opponent; it is the only face-up card |
| Q5308, Q5309 | "checks a face-up security card exactly like a face-down one and fires its [Security] effect" | the same fixture run face up and face down produces identical endpoints; ST22-08's [Security] effect deletes the attacker in both |
| Q5310 | "turns every face-up security card face down when the stack is shuffled" | after the placement leaves one face-up card, playing EX3-029 (search + shuffle) leaves zero face-up cards |
| ACE Overflow -4 | "charges the ACE Overflow -4 when it leaves the battle area" | memory 0 -> -4 |
| IR shape | two structural tests | both branches, their conditions, source zones, `destination: "security"`, `position: "bottom"`, `faceDown: false`, and the `flipSecurity` cost |

All behavioral tests drive public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `endPhase`, `surrender`) inside the production turn loop. The single injected-timing use is the Overflow test's `advance().verb.deletePermanent`, which stands in for an opponent removal and proves a catalog field, not a printed clause.

#### Fixture notes

- The turn loop's Active phase unsuspends the board, so the "suspended ally" fixtures arm `isSuspended` after seat 0's Main opens rather than in the Board Spec.
- Every fixture keeps a spare playable card in hand. Without one the engine auto-passes the Main phase and the later assertions read a state from the opponent's turn.

#### Open interpretation, pinned not blessed

"Your top face-up security card" is executed as "the topmost face-up card in the stack", not "the top card, and only while it is face up". No ruling settles the wording, and the strict reading would make the clause almost dead on a card that places face-up cards at the bottom. The test `flips the topmost face-up card even when a face-down card sits above it` pins the current behavior with a comment, so a change to it must be deliberate. This is not claimed as correct.

#### Observation outside this lane

In the Q5308/Q5309 fixture, the checked ST22-08 leaves the security stack to its owner's **hand** rather than the trash after its [Security] effect resolves. The behavior is identical face up and face down, so it is not a face-up-security defect and it is not BT23-045's clause; it belongs to the security-check lane. The test asserts face-up/face-down parity and the [Security] deletion instead of blessing that destination.

#### Blast Digivolve fixture correction (session 2)

The original ＜Blast Digivolve＞ test drove the counter with a bare `digivolve` intent. That left the opponent's [Counter] window open and the attack unresolved, so the test proved the digivolve but not the clause's public path or its end state. Both Blast tests now follow the BT23-027 pattern: the real turn loop, seat 1 attacks, seat 0 answers with `respondCounter` and the `blast-digivolve:<permanentId>` effect key, and the test asserts the attack's end state (security stack by instance ids, attacker fate, no pending decision). The suite grew from 22 to 23 tests and stays green.

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-045.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file / 23 tests passed, 6.1s (`logs/BT23-045-focused.log`).
- Peers: `... BT23-045.test.ts BT23-028.test.ts BT23-034.test.ts BT23-043.test.ts` — 4 files / 79 tests passed (`logs/BT23-045-peers.log`). BT23-028 and BT23-034 use TigerVespamon as a probe and were not edited; the mandatory-branch change does not disturb them, because both pay from hand.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no errors in `BT23-045.ts` or `BT23-045.test.ts` (`logs/BT23-045-typecheck.log`). Pre-existing failures in other cards' tests (BT23-048, BT23-049, engine combat controller) belong to other lanes.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` — only the repo-wide `no-explicit-any` warnings that the peer BT23 tests also carry, from the `as any` IR casts. `pnpm exec oxfmt` — clean.

#### Remaining gaps

- No ruling covers whether the [All Turns] flip is likewise compulsory when payable. Q5331's reasoning (public zone => no choice) would make it mandatory, but nothing states it, so the clause stays optional and both branches are proven.
- Q5310 is proven through EX3-029's shuffle, the only public shuffle path reachable from a legal fixture; the shuffle primitive itself is engine-owned.
