### BT23-046 — Rosemon

#### Card summary

Lv.6 Green Digimon. Play cost 11, DP 11000. Attribute Data. Traits: Fairy, CS. No inherited effect.
Printed evo cost: Green Lv.5 cost 3.

Printed clauses (catalog `effectText`):

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3`
2. `＜Fortitude＞`
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] By suspending 1 Digimon or Tamer, until your opponent's turn
   ends, 1 of their Digimon or Tamers can't unsuspend.`
4. `[Opponent's Turn] [Once Per Turn] When one of your opponent's Digimon attacks, you may change
   the attack target to 1 of your suspended Digimon with [Vegetation], [Plant] or [Fairy] in any of
   its traits or the [CS] trait.`

Catalog matches the official card list. No discrepancy found. The catalog uses a non-breaking space
in `[CS] trait`, `[Fairy] in` and `[CS] trait.`; the test asserts the exact string, ` ` included.

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Subject | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5311 | The `By suspending 1 Digimon or Tamer` cost may suspend either player's Digimon or Tamer | "pays the By cost with its controller's own Tamer…" (own side, public `playCard`) and "pays the By cost with an opponent's Tamer…" (opponent side). Both also assert the offered cost candidate set spans both seats. |
| Q5312 | The redirect target set is the controller's own suspended Digimon with Vegetation / Plant / Fairy traits, or with the CS trait | "offers only own suspended Vegetation/Plant/Fairy/CS Digimon…" asserts the exact candidate set; "never triggers when the only suspended Digimon carries none of the printed traits" is the negative. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog + full IR shape | "matches every catalog field and the complete compiled clause set" | all four `compiled.effects` triggers plus `digivolutionRequirement` |
| 3 (IR shape, both triggers) | "by suspending one Digimon or Tamer, restricts one opposing Digimon or Tamer from unsuspending" | `OnPlay` / `WhenDigivolving` -> `Restrict` |
| 4 (IR shape) | "once per opponent turn may redirect an attack to a suspended Vegetation/Plant/Fairy/CS Digimon" | `OpponentsTurn` `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` -> `SubTrigger whenOpponentAttacks` -> `RedirectAttack` |
| 3 (cost, Q5311 own side) | "pays the By cost with its controller's own Tamer and still restricts an opposing Digimon" | `cost.target.filter.controller: "any"` |
| 3 (cost, Q5311 opponent side) + controller boundary | "pays the By cost with an opponent's Tamer and restricts a different opposing Digimon" | same cost filter; `target.filter.controller: "opponent"` |
| 3 (decline) | "may decline the optional By condition without paying or restricting" | `optional` + `abortOnDecline` |
| 3 (When Digivolving half, duration) | "holds the restriction across the opponent's unsuspend step and drops it when that turn ends" | `WhenDigivolving`, `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` |
| 4 (redirect) | "redirects an opponent's player attack onto a suspended Fairy Digimon and spares the security stack" | `RedirectAttack` |
| 4 (Q5312 candidates) | "offers only own suspended Vegetation/Plant/Fairy/CS Digimon as the new attack target" | `target.filter` `controller: "mine"`, `suspended: true`, two-entry `nameOrTrait` union |
| 4 (no candidates) | "never triggers when the only suspended Digimon carries none of the printed traits" | same filter, empty candidate set |
| 4 (decline) | "may decline the redirect and let the attack reach the security stack" | `optional: true` |
| 4 (once per turn + reset) | "redirects only the first attack of an opponent turn and resets on their next turn" | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| 2 (keyword seam) | "exposes Fortitude through the live keyword seam" | `Static` `keywords: [Fortitude]` |
| 2 (behavior) | "replays itself for free when a stacked copy is deleted in battle" | comprehensive §16-27 |
| 1 (printed route) | "digivolves for 3 from a green Lv.5 base on the printed route and draws the digivolution card" | printed `evoCosts` |
| 1 (alternate route) | "digivolves for 3 from an off-color Lv.5 [CS] base through the alternate route" | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| 1 (color/trait negative) | "rejects a Lv.5 base that is neither green nor [CS]" | neither route matches |
| 1 (level negative) | "rejects a Lv.3 green [CS] base — the alternate route is level gated" | `level: 5` gate |

Every behavioral test drives public intents only (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `endPhase`,
`respondDecision`) except the two decline-path On Play probes, which use `advance().fire(OnPlay)`;
the accepted On Play path is proved through a real `playCard` for 11 memory.

The duration test is a full four-turn boundary walk on the real turn loop: the restriction is
observable right after a public digivolve, the restricted Digimon still fails to unsuspend during
the opponent's unsuspend step, the restriction is gone once that turn ends, and the same Digimon
unsuspends normally on the opponent's following turn.

The once-per-turn test suspends the redirect target publicly (it attacks), proves the first opponent
attack is redirected (security stack untouched, 3000-DP attacker dead against the 6000-DP target),
proves the second attack of the same turn goes through to security, then returns through a full own
turn and proves the third attack — on the opponent's next turn — is redirected again.

#### Defects found and fixed (all local to the test file)

1. "digivolves for 3 from an off-color level-5 CS card" used `BT23-044` Lilamon, which is **green**
   Lv.5. It exercised the printed evo cost, not the alternate route, and asserted only `{ok: true}` —
   no memory, no stack identity, no digivolution draw. Replaced by separate printed-route and
   alternate-route tests, each asserting cost 3, exact stack identity and the drawn card.
2. The evolution negative used `BT1-008` Frimon — a Digi-Egg seeded in the battle area, an illegal
   fixture. Replaced by `BT1-021` MetalGreymon (Red Lv.5, non-CS) for the colour/trait axis and
   `BT23-037` Tentomon (green Lv.3 `[CS]`) for the level axis.
3. "accepts the optional By condition" asserted the unsuspend restriction on the **same** opponent
   permanent it had suspended as the cost, so it could not tell the cost apart from the target. It
   also never covered Q5311's "either player" axis, and never proved the target is opponent-only.
   Replaced by two tests that pick cost and target explicitly and assert both candidate sets.
4. The redirect test had no security stack on either side, so "the attacker left the battle area"
   was equally consistent with a security trade. It also never asserted that the redirect target
   survived. Rewritten with a real security stack, exact security instance ids before and after,
   hand size and the target's DP.
5. Fortitude had only a keyword-seam assertion. Added a real battle: a stacked Rosemon is deleted by
   a 15000-DP attacker, the digivolution card goes to trash, and Rosemon returns to the battle area
   with an empty stack for zero memory (comprehensive §16-27).
6. No coverage existed for the once-per-turn cap or its reset, the `untilOpponentTurnEnd` duration,
   the When Digivolving half of clause 3, or the Q5312 candidate set. All four are now behavioral.

No change was needed in `BT23-046.ts`. The IR matches the printed text; the two-entry `nameOrTrait`
array is a union, not an intersection (`matching/definition.ts:163-168`), which is the printed "or".

Both key assertions were mutation-checked: forcing the first redirected attack to expect a security
loss, and adding the non-qualifying Digimon to the expected candidate set, each turn the suite red.

#### Engine behaviour worth reusing (not defects)

- A `chooseTargets` target set with exactly one candidate resolves without prompting. Tests that
  want to inspect a candidate list must seed at least two candidates.
- `state.pendingDecision` does not carry `options`. The candidate list must be read from the
  recorded decision stream (`s.decisions`) by `decisionId`.
- On the real turn loop, a permanent seeded as `suspended` unsuspends during its controller's first
  unsuspend step. A loop test that needs a suspended own Digimon on the opponent's turn must suspend
  it during the controller's own turn — attacking with it is the public way.

#### Remaining gaps

- The `[Plant]` token of the trait disjunction has no fixture: no Digimon in the catalog (all 4388
  entries checked) carries the `Plant` trait. `[Vegetation]`, `[Fairy]` and `[CS]` are each proved
  with a real card, and the token list itself is pinned in the IR-shape test.
- Fortitude's interaction with the Rosemon replay's own `[On Play]` clause is not asserted: the
  replay is a play, so clause 3 should offer itself again. The replay test runs with
  `autoAcceptOptional` and asserts only the Fortitude endpoint. This is a composition question the
  coordinator may want covered once, not a defect found here.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-046.test.ts --maxWorkers=1
  --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file / 18 tests passed, 2.4s. Log:
  `logs/BT23-046-focused.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no errors in `BT23-046.ts` or `BT23-046.test.ts`. Other
  BT23 files and `src/engine/combat/controller.ts` carry pre-existing errors owned by other lanes.
- `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-046.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-046.test.ts` —
  3 `no-explicit-any` warnings on the IR-shape assertions, matching the accepted style in
  `BT23-030.test.ts`. No errors.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-046.test.ts` — clean. `git diff --check` — clean.

### BT23-047 — Examon

#### Card

Green/Red/Blue level 7 Digimon, play cost 15, DP 15000, Mega, Data, traits Holy Warrior / Royal Knight / CS. Printed EvoCosts: green, red and blue Lv.6 for 5.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.6 w/[CS] trait: Cost 5`
2. `[DNA Digivolve] Green Lv.6 + blue Lv.6: Cost 0`
3. `＜Piercing＞`
4. `＜Security A. +1＞`
5. `＜Partition (green Lv.5 & blue Lv.5)＞`
6. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` suspend 5 opposing Digimon or Tamers; none of the opponent's Digimon can unsuspend in their next unsuspend phase; then this Digimon may attack.
7. `[Your Turn] [Once Per Turn]` when the opponent's security stack is removed from, trash 1 of their Option cards in the battle area, then delete 1 of their suspended Digimon or Tamers.

Rulings covered: Q5313, Q5314, Q5315.

#### Defects found and fixed

Both fixes are local to `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-047.ts`.

1. **Missing evolution requirements.** The module carried no `digivolutionRequirement` and no
   `dnaDigivolveRequirement`, so the persisted IR had both null. The `[CS]` alternate was
   unreachable, and `dnaDigivolveCostFor` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:6234`)
   fell back to a per-material EvoCost match — which would have let a **single green Lv.6 plus
   any second material** DNA digivolve for 5. Added:
   - `dnaDigivolveRequirement: [{ cost: 0, materials: [{ color: "Green", level: 6 }, { color: "Blue", level: 6 }] }]`
   - `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 6, traits: ["CS"], cost: 5, isAlternate: true }]`
2. **Invalid duration marker.** The `Restrict` action used
   `duration: "untilTheirNextUnsuspendPhase"`, which is not a member of `EffectDurationRef`
   (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/durations.ts:3`). `toDuration`
   (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/duration.ts:10`) has no case for it and fell
   through to `default: EffectDuration.UntilEachTurnEnd` — the lock expired at the end of the
   controller's own turn, before the opponent's unsuspend phase, so clause 6 did nothing.
   Corrected to `"untilOpponentNextUnsuspendPhase"`, which maps to
   `EffectDuration.UntilNextUntap` and is swept at `ownerActivePhaseEnd`, after the unsuspend
   it must block. The bug was invisible because the module carried `// @ts-nocheck`; that
   directive was removed and the module now typechecks clean.

#### Clause to test to IR map

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, both requirements | `declares the official catalog identity, the [CS] alternate and the green+blue DNA recipe` | `digivolutionRequirement`, `dnaDigivolveRequirement` |
| Printed green Lv.6 route | `publicly digivolves from a printed green Lv.6 for 5 and draws the evolution bonus` | catalog `evoCosts` |
| `[Digivolve]` CS alternate | `publicly digivolves from an off-colour Lv.6 [CS] source for 5 through the alternate path` | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| Illegal source negative | `rejects a yellow Lv.6 source without the [CS] trait through ...` (2 rows) | both requirement paths |
| `[DNA Digivolve]` | `publicly DNA digivolves for 0 from a green Lv.6 plus a blue Lv.6 and merges both stacks` | `dnaDigivolveRequirement[0]` |
| DNA negatives | `refuses the DNA route from two green Lv.6 materials ...`, `refuses the DNA route when the blue material is only level 5` | `dnaDigivolveRequirement[0]` |
| Keyword presence | `carries Piercing, Security Attack +1 and Partition as live keywords` | three `Static` effects |
| `＜Security A. +1＞` | `checks two security cards in one attack from ＜Security A. +1＞` | `SecurityAttack` keyword |
| `＜Piercing＞` | `pierces the surplus DP into security after winning a Digimon battle` | `Piercing` keyword |
| `＜Partition＞` accept | `replays the exact green Lv.5 and blue Lv.5 sources through ＜Partition＞ on an opposing effect deletion` | `Partition` keyword |
| `＜Partition＞` refuse | `declines ＜Partition＞ and leaves every card in the trash` | `Partition` keyword |
| Clause 6, decline branch | `suspends five opposing Digimon or Tamers on play and offers the attack, which may be declined` | `OnPlay` `Suspend` / `Restrict` / `Attack` |
| Clause 6, accept branch | `accepts the On Play attack, suspending Examon and checking two security cards` | `OnPlay` `Attack` (`optional`) |
| Clause 6, unsuspend lock | `blocks the opponent's next unsuspend phase and releases the one after it` | `Restrict` `untilOpponentNextUnsuspendPhase` |
| Clause 7 + Q5313/Q5314/Q5315 | `trashes the Option its own attack placed and deletes a suspended opposing Digimon, once per turn` | `YourTurn` `SubTrigger whenSecurityRemoved` |
| Clause 7 reset | `resets the once-per-turn security trigger on the controller's next turn` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Clause 7 empty branch | `resolves harmlessly when the opponent has no Option and no suspended Digimon` | same |
| Controller boundary | `ignores removal from the controller's own security stack` | `sourceFilter: { controller: "opponent" }` |

#### Evidence notes

**Evolution.** Every route goes through a public intent. The printed route uses BT1-080 Titamon
(green Lv.6, no effect text): memory 5 to 0, the exact Titamon instance is the only card in the
stack, and the exact deck card lands in hand. The `[CS]` alternate uses BT23-058 Craniamon, which
is **black** — it can never satisfy a printed green/red/blue EvoCost, so a legal digivolution from
it can only be the alternate. The intent sets `useAlternateCost: true` and
`alternateRequirementIndex: 0`; an explicit index never silently falls back
(`apps/api/src/engine/actions/digivolve.ts:472`). The negative uses ST3-10 Magnadramon (yellow
Lv.6, no `[CS]`) and asserts `{ ok: false, reason: "invalid-evolution" }`, unchanged memory, and
the card still in hand on both the plain and the alternate-declared path.

**DNA.** There is a public `dnaDigivolve` intent
(`apps/api/src/engine/actions/dnaDigivolve.ts:42`), so the DNA proof needs no injected timing.
Both materials are built publicly in the same test: BT1-076 MegaKabuterimon digivolves into
BT1-080 Titamon for 2, BT1-038 Monzaemon into ST2-10 Plesiomon for 2, then the DNA digivolve pays
0. All three draws are asserted by exact instance id (two evolution bonuses plus the DNA
procedure's own draw, CR §8-2-3-3), the deck is empty, and the merged stack holds exactly the four
source instance ids. Two negatives pin the requirement: green Lv.6 + green Lv.6 (wrong colour) and
green Lv.6 + blue Lv.5 (wrong level) are both rejected with no memory paid and both materials still
on the board. Before the fix the wrong-colour pair would have been legal for 5 through the
per-material fallback.

**Keywords in combat.** `＜Security A. +1＞` is proved by a real attack on the player: security
drops from 4 to 2 in one attack, both cards reach trash, and Examon ends suspended. `＜Piercing＞`
is proved by a real Digimon battle against a suspended 3000 DP body: the victim's exact card
instance reaches trash and the pierced security check turns over **two** cards, because
`＜Security A. +1＞` modifies the number of cards checked in every security check this Digimon
performs (CR §16-4-1, quoted in the test).

**Partition.** The only public route that puts a green Lv.5 and a blue Lv.5 under one Examon is
the DNA merge, so the Partition tests reuse it. CR §16-29 excludes the holder's own effects, so
the deletion is driven from the opponent's seat (`s.state.turnSeat = 1`, then the effect-deletion
verb) — no player intent deletes an opposing Digimon by effect on this board, and the verb is the
production primitive. The accept branch asserts the battle area now holds exactly the
MegaKabuterimon and Monzaemon instance ids with empty stacks, and that the two Lv.6 cards and
Examon are the only cards in trash. The refuse branch answers the 0..1 selection with an empty
response (min 0 / max 1 asserted) and leaves all five cards in trash.

**On Play / When Digivolving.** The decline branch plays Examon publicly for 15 (memory 10 to
-5), asserts exactly five of six opposing bodies suspended and the unsuspend restriction on all
six, and that Examon is unsuspended with opposing security untouched. The accept branch attacks
the player and checks two security cards. The unsuspend lock is proved through
`startTurnLoop()`: Examon arrives by the `[CS]` alternate (cost 5, so memory stays positive and
the turn does not end on the spot), the restriction is observed armed, seat 1's Main phase is
reached with the Digimon **still suspended** and the restriction now expired, and after one full
round seat 1's next unsuspend phase stands it up.

**Q5313 / Q5314 / Q5315.** All three are covered by one public attack. The opponent's top security
card is P-035 Red Memory Boost!, whose `[Security]` effect places itself in the battle area — the
exact scenario in Q5315. Examon attacks the player, `＜Security A. +1＞` checks two cards, the
`[Security]` effect resolves first (Q5313), and the pending `[Your Turn]` effect then trashes that
exact P-035 instance and deletes the suspended opposing Digimon while the standing one survives.
Q5314 ("trashes an Option placed by an effect") is proved by the same assertion pair: the trashed
card is one `placedByEffect` marks (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:1223`), set only by
the place-in-battle-area path. A second attack in the same turn removes another security card and
changes nothing else, proving the once-per-turn cap. A separate turn-loop test proves the reset:
two removals on turn 1 delete only one suspended Digimon, and after a full round the third removal
deletes the second one. The empty-board test shows the trigger resolves quietly with no pending
decision when the opponent has no Option and no suspended body. The own-security test shows a
removal from the controller's own stack does not fire it.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-047.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, **20 tests passed**, 6.4s. Log: `logs/BT23-047-vitest.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no errors in `BT23-047.ts` or `BT23-047.test.ts`. Pre-existing unrelated failures remain in `src/engine/combat/controller.ts` (engine lane) and in `src/cards/BT23/BT23-048.test.ts` / `BT23-049.test.ts` (other lanes).
- `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-047.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-047.test.ts` — clean.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt` applied to both files.

#### Coordinator actions required

`packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` still holds the pre-fix record for BT23-047 (no
requirements, `duration: "untilTheirNextUnsuspendPhase"`).
`src/cards/BT23/BT23-catalog-sync.test.ts` fails its "keeps every record synchronized" case,
listing BT23-047 alongside eight cards owned by other lanes. Catalog and effects.json edits are
coordinator-owned; the sync is needed to close this.

#### Remaining gaps

- No public intent exists that deletes an opposing Digimon by effect on a bare board, so the
  Partition trigger is driven by the production effect-deletion verb rather than a card. A future
  peer test could substitute an opponent removal card.
- The `[CS]` alternate and the printed EvoCost both cost 5, so no fixture can distinguish which
  path was billed by memory alone. The off-colour base isolates the alternate instead.
- ＜Partition＞ on a battle death is untested because battle deletions never reach the primitive
  that implements it, matching CR §16-29's "other than by ... a battle" exclusion.

### BT23-048 — Gotsumon

#### Card

- Lv.3 Rookie, Black/Red, play cost 3, DP 1000, Data. Traits: Rock / Hudie / CS.
- Evolution costs: Black Lv.2 cost 1, Red Lv.2 cost 1.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS] trait: Cost 0` — alternate evolution route.
2. `[On Play] Reveal the top 3 cards of your deck. Add 1 card with the [Hudie] trait and 1
   Tamer card or Option card with the [CS] trait among them to the hand. Return the rest to
   the bottom of the deck.`
3. Inherited: `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] You may play 1 play cost 5 or lower Digimon
   card with the [Hudie] trait from your hand without paying the cost. The Digimon this
   effect played can't digivolve and is deleted at the end of your opponent's turn.`

The catalog text matches the official card list. No discrepancy to report.

#### Rulings covered

- **Q5316** — with this card in the digivolution cards of a Digimon with ＜Alliance＞, the
  Digimon played by the inherited effect during that attack may be chosen as the ally.
  Covered behaviorally.
- **Q5567** — the played Digimon is deleted at the end of the opponent's turn. Covered
  behaviorally. The second half ("if it isn't deleted by another effect, it's still prevented
  from digivolving") is a gap — see below.
- **Q5568** — an end-of-turn trigger and this deletion are simultaneous pending processing, so
  the turn player chooses the order. Retained red; the engine never offers the choice.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause / ruling | Test | IR it exercises |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity | `matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause` | whole `compiled` |
| Clause 2 shape | `reveals 3 and adds one Hudie card plus one CS Tamer/Option, bottoming the rest` | `RevealAdd` |
| Clause 3 shape | `inherited effect optionally plays a Hudie Digimon...` | `PlayWithoutCost` / `Restrict` / `DelayedDelete` |
| Clause 2 endpoints | `publicly plays for 3 memory, adds one Hudie and one CS Tamer, and bottoms the rest` | `RevealAdd` add[0], add[1], `rest: deckBottom` |
| Clause 2 empty branch | `bottoms all 3 when the reveal holds no Hudie card and no CS Tamer or Option` | `RevealAdd` with no match |
| Clause 3 free play + eligibility | `publicly attacks and plays a Hudie Digimon free, leaving ineligible cards in hand` | `PlayWithoutCost` `payCost:false`, `playCostLte:5`, Hudie filter |
| Clause 3 optional | `declines the inherited play and leaves the hand untouched` | `optional` / `abortOnDecline` |
| Clause 3 lock | `refuses a public digivolve onto the Digimon this effect played` | `Restrict digivolve permanent` |
| Clause 3 once per turn | `refuses a second inherited play in the same turn and allows it again on the next own turn` | `frequency: OncePerTurn` |
| Q5567 | `keeps the played Digimon through the end of my turn and deletes it at the end of the opponent's turn (Q5567)` | `DelayedDelete endOfOpponentTurn` |
| Q5316 | `uses Alliance on the Digimon the inherited effect just played (Q5316)` | `PlayWithoutCost` inside the attacker's window + ＜Alliance＞ |
| Q5568 | `offers the turn player the order of the end-of-turn trigger and this deletion (Q5568)` — `it.fails` | `DelayedDelete` vs `EndOfYourTurn` window |
| Alliance ordering | `orders inherited [When Attacking] against Alliance in one window` — `it.fails` | engine `combineAllianceTiming` |
| Clause 1 | `digivolves for 0 from a Lv.2 CS Digi-Egg on both cost branches and rejects a non-CS egg` | `digivolutionRequirement` alternate |

Every behavioral test drives public intents (`playCard`, `attack`, `digivolve`,
`respondAlliance`, `respondDecision`) plus `advance().runTurn()` for real turn boundaries. The
only Advance Surface verb used is `verb.unsuspend`, needed so the same attacker can declare a
second attack in one turn for the once-per-turn refusal.

#### What the tests pin

- **On Play**: memory 3 -> 0 through a public `playCard`; hand ends as exactly
  `[BT22-083, BT23-050]`; deck ends as exactly `[BT1-010, BT1-011, BT1-009]`, proving the
  unchosen card goes to the BOTTOM, not the top. No `actionRejected` event.
- **On Play, no match**: hand stays empty and all 3 revealed cards land at the bottom in
  revealed order.
- **Inherited play**: memory is unchanged at 3 (the card is played free), the cost-7 Hudie
  (BT23-055) and the non-Hudie (BT1-009) both stay in hand, and the played permanent carries
  the `digivolve` restriction. No decision is left pending.
- **Digivolution lock**: a public `digivolve` of BT23-041 onto the played Digimon returns
  `ok: false`; the permanent keeps its top card and the evolution card stays in hand.
- **Once per turn**: after the first attack the second copy is still in hand; a second attack
  in the same turn (attacker unsuspended) leaves it in hand; after a full seat 0 turn and a
  full seat 1 turn the third attack plays it. The intermediate state is asserted at each step.
- **Q5567**: the played Digimon survives the whole of my turn and is in the trash after the
  opponent's turn ends. The host permanent is untouched, so the deletion is scoped to the
  Digimon this effect played.
- **Q5316**: the ally the test chooses is the Digimon the inherited effect just played; it ends
  suspended, and the opponent loses 2 security cards (the normal check plus ＜Alliance＞'s extra),
  proving ＜Alliance＞ actually resolved with that ally.

#### Defects found

##### 1. ＜Alliance＞ never orders against an inherited [When Attacking] (engine, retained red)

`GameEngine.combineAllianceTiming` (apps/api/src/engine/GameEngine.ts ~line 891) decides whether
to pool ＜Alliance＞ into the attacker's [When Attacking] window with:

```ts
effectsOf(EffectTiming.OnUseAttack, this.cardSourceOf(permanent.topCard)).length > 0
```

That reads the TOP CARD only. An attacker whose only [When Attacking] effect is inherited from a
digivolution card — exactly the BT23-048 case — falls back to the legacy path, where ＜Alliance＞
always runs last and no `orderTriggers` decision is raised. Comprehensive Rules §15-4 and KB
Q5257 make the two simultaneous triggers the controller orders.

Q5316's answer still comes out right, because the fixed order (inherited play, then ＜Alliance＞)
happens to be the order the ruling needs. The controller simply loses a choice the rules give
it, and a card that wanted ＜Alliance＞ to resolve first cannot get it.

Retained as `it.fails("orders inherited [When Attacking] against Alliance in one window")`, seam
name `alliance-window-ignores-inherited-when-attacking`. Engine-owned; not fixed here.

##### 2. Q5568 ordering is never offered (engine, retained red)

Seam name `end-of-turn-order-with-delayed-delete`. Fixture: seat 1 holds EX9-033 Kaguyamon,
whose `[End of Your Turn]` effect fires at the same moment as this card's end-of-opponent-turn
deletion. Both DO fire — seat 1 receives its optional prompt and the played Digimon is deleted —
but they never meet in one window, so no `orderTriggers` decision is raised for the turn player.
Suspected seam: the `endOfTurn` SubTrigger registration behind the `DelayedDelete` action in
apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter.ts, which does not join the end-of-turn window's
`collectPending` pool that the simultaneous-trigger resolver orders.

Retained as `it.fails(...)`. Engine-owned; not fixed here.

##### 3. Fixture defects in the previous test file (fixed here)

The prior BT23-048 test file was largely structural and had two illegal or non-proving fixtures:

- The alternate-evolution test put a Lv.2 Digi-Egg (BT23-002) in the BATTLE AREA. Digi-Eggs live
  in breeding. Now `breeding: { card: "BT23-002" }`, and the negative uses BT1-007, a non-CS egg.
- It asserted `{ ok: true }` on the alternate route without pinning the cost. Both branches now
  assert memory stays at 0 — the implicit alternate match and the explicit
  `useAlternateCost: true` request — and the explicit branch also asserts the resulting stack.

The old file also drove the whole inherited clause through `advance().fire(OnUseAttack, ...)` and
`advance().fireSubTrigger("endOfTurn")` with a hand-set `turnSeat`, which proves the IR wiring but
not the game. Those paths are replaced with a public `attack` and real turns.

No defect was found in `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-048.ts`. The module is unchanged.

#### Remaining gaps

- **Q5567 second half** — "if it isn't deleted by another effect when it would be deleted, it's
  still prevented from digivolving". Reaching this needs a save effect to intercept the delayed
  deletion. The attempt with BT23-073 Eater Bit's "doesn't leave" replacement never fired against
  the `DelayedDelete`, which points at BT23-073 or the deletion path, not at BT23-048. Out of this
  lane's allowed edits and not written up as a BT23-048 red. Recommend it as a follow-up for the
  engine or BT23-073 lane.
- The Q5316 test drives ＜Alliance＞ down the legacy path (see defect 1). When that seam is fixed
  the test will need to answer an `orderTriggers` decision first; the `it.fails` companion is the
  marker for that.

#### Commands and results

Run from the worktree root.

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-048.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
  -> **1 file, 14 tests: 12 passed, 2 expected fail**, 3.2 s.
  Log: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-048-vitest.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` -> no errors in BT23-048.ts or BT23-048.test.ts.
  Pre-existing unrelated errors remain in `src/cards/BT23/BT23-049.test.ts` and
  `src/engine/combat/controller.ts` (other lanes).
- `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-048.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-048.test.ts`
  -> 2 `no-explicit-any` warnings on the two IR-shape assertions, the same pattern the exemplar
  BT23-028/BT23-030 tests use. No errors.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-048.test.ts` -> clean.
- `git diff --check` -> clean.

### BT23-049 — Monodramon

#### Card

- Set BT23, Lv.3 Rookie, Black, Vaccine, Mini Dragon / CS, play cost 3, DP 1000.
- Printed evolution cost: Black Lv.2 for 0.
- Effect text: `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS] trait: Cost 0` and `[Start of Your Main Phase] By trashing 1 card with the [Dragonkin], [Cyborg], [Device] or [CS] trait from your hand, ＜Draw 1＞ and gain 1 memory.`
- Inherited: `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP.`

Catalog text matches the official BT23 card list dump
(`docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/official-bt23.html`, entry `BT23-049`). No discrepancy.

#### Rulings

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-049` returns no knowledge-base entries, and
`KB-INDEX.md` lists 0 Q&A ids for this card. The governing prose is the
optional-processing-condition block in `data/kb/rules/comprehensive.md`:

- §15-7-1: "by X, Y" is an optional processing condition.
- §15-7-2: if the condition is not executed, nothing after it is executed.
- §15-7-3: the condition cannot be partly executed — exactly one card, never two.
- §15-7-4: the player chooses whether to execute the condition.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Printed clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity and printed text | catalog record | matches the catalog record and the official printed text |
| `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS] trait: Cost 0` | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 2, traits: ["CS"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` | digivolves for 0 off an off-color Lv.2 [CS] source through the alternate route |
| Printed Black Lv.2 EvoCost | `evoCosts` | digivolves for 0 off the printed Black Lv.2 EvoCost without the alternate flag |
| Illegal source | — | rejects an off-color Lv.2 source without the [CS] trait |
| `[Start of Your Main Phase] ... ＜Draw 1＞ and gain 1 memory` | `StartOfYourMainPhase` -> `Draw` + `GainMemory 1` | trashes exactly 1 matching card, draws 1 and gains 1 memory on the real Start of Your Main Phase |
| `By trashing 1 card with the [Dragonkin], [Cyborg], [Device] or [CS] trait from your hand` | cost `trash`, `nameOrTrait` tokens with `match: "trait"` | accepts a [Dragonkin] / [Cyborg] / [Device] / [CS] card as the payment and never the non-matching card (4 cases) |
| Unpayable condition (§15-7-2) | `abortOnDecline: true` | aborts the whole clause when no hand card carries a printed trait |
| Declinable condition (§15-7-4) | `optional: true` | lets the controller decline the optional processing condition |
| One payment only (§15-7-3) | cost on the draw only, `GainMemory` uncosted | carries one declinable trash cost gating the draw, with the memory gain uncosted; hand/deck deltas in the payment test |
| "Your" main phase | `StartOfYourMainPhase` turn-owner gate | does not fire on the opponent's main phase (and fires again on the next own turn) |
| `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP` (inherited) | `AllTurns` `ModifyDP +1000`, `isInherited` | grants the inherited host +1000 DP on both turns |

Every behavioral test drives the production turn loop (`startTurnLoop()` +
`waitForMainPhase`) and public intents. No injected-timing `fire()` calls remain: the
previous suite reached the trigger with `advance().fire(EffectTiming.OnStartMainPhase, ...)`,
an injected timing that earns no behavioral credit.

Trait matching is exact, not substring: `matchNameOrTrait`
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/matching/definition.ts:355`) compares normalized
traits with `===` for `match: "trait"`. The four trait fixtures each carry exactly one of
the printed tokens (AD1-004 Dragonkin, AD1-003 Cyborg, P-159 Device, BT22-008 CS), and
BT1-010 Agumon (Reptile) is never taken.

#### Defect found and fixed

The `[Start of Your Main Phase]` cost was **not declinable**. `BT23-049.ts` set
`abortOnDecline: true` but not `optional: true`, so the engine paid the trash cost with no
prompt whenever a matching card was in hand. That contradicts §15-7-4: the controller may
always choose not to execute an optional processing condition. Fixed locally by adding
`optional: true` to the cost-bearing `Draw` action, matching the house pattern for the same
prose shape (`apps/api/src/cards/BT13/BT13-062.ts:44-46`). The decline test failed before
the fix and passes after it.

#### Fixture defects fixed

The previous suite placed Digi-Egg cards (BT23-002 Yokomon, BT1-003 Upamon) directly in the
battle area to prove the digivolution routes. Digi-Eggs belong in breeding; both routes now
start from a breeding permanent and assert source-stack identity, the 0 memory cost, and the
digivolution bonus draw.

The previous alternate-route test also asserted only `{ ok: true }`, which proves nothing
about the cost. The route now passes `useAlternateCost: true` from an off-color (Green)
Lv.2 CS source, where the printed Black EvoCost cannot apply, and pins memory at 3 before
and after.

persisted record in `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` lacks the new
`optional: true`. `effects.json` is coordinator-owned, so the sync is a coordinator step.

### BT23-050 — Ankylomon

#### Card

Catalog `BT23-050`: Black/Yellow level 4 Digimon, play cost 5, DP 5000, evolution costs Yellow Lv.3 for 3 and Blue Lv.3 for 3, form Champion, attribute Free, traits Ankylosaur/Hudie/CS. The catalog text matches the official card list; no discrepancy found.

Printed clauses:

- `[Digivolve] [Armadillomon]/Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2` — two alternate routes.
- `＜Blocker＞`
- `[On Play] [When Digivolving] 1 of your opponent's Digimon gets -2000 DP until their turn ends. Then, if it's your turn, 2 of your Digimon may DNA digivolve into [Shakkoumon] in the hand.`
- Inherited: `＜Blocker＞`

Rulings covered: Q5317, Q5318 (shared with BT23-017 Betamon), Q5319 (shared with BT23-101 Hudiemon and BT23-084 Erika Mishima).

#### Clause to test map

| Clause / ruling | Test | IR node |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog fields, both alternate routes, full residual-free coverage | matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause | `digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| `[Shakkoumon]` is an exact printed name | same test asserts `match: "nameExact"` | `OnPlay/WhenDigivolving actions[1].into.nameOrTrait[0]` |
| ＜Blocker＞ printed and inherited | exposes Blocker directly and from a realistic inherited stack | both `Static` effects' `keywords` |
| Play cost 5, -2000 DP on exactly one opposing Digimon | publicly plays for 5, reduces exactly one opposing Digimon and may decline the DNA | `actions[0]` ModifyDP |
| Optional refusal leaves both materials and the hand card | same test (`autoDeclineOptional`) | `actions[1].optional` |
| DNA accept: two own Digimon become Shakkoumon | publicly DNA digivolves two of your Digimon into an unsuspended Shakkoumon for no extra memory | `actions[1]` DnaDigivolve, `payCost` |
| No legal DNA (one own Digimon / level-3 partner / Shakkoumon in trash) | publicly reduces DP but refuses the DNA with %s (3 rows) | `materials.count: 2`, `from: ["hand"]` |
| `Then, if it's your turn` | applies the DP reduction but skips the DNA clause when played on the opponent's turn | `condition: { kind: "isYourTurn" }` |
| Q5317 zero-DP deletion waits for the whole effect | deletes the Digimon taken to 0 DP only after the DNA digivolution resolves (Q5317) | `GameEngine.deleteZeroDpDigimon` runs from `ruleProcess`, after the effect resolves |
| Q5318 Betamon's inherited play blocks the DNA | refuses the DNA after Betamon's inherited attack play restricts digivolution (Q5318) | `canDnaDigivolve` restriction gate |
| Q5318 control: the same pair is otherwise legal | control for Q5318: the same pair DNA digivolves when Ankylomon is played normally | same |
| Q5319 the DNA result is unsuspended and can be an ＜Alliance＞ ally | leaves the DNA result unsuspended and eligible as an Alliance ally (Q5319) | DNA merge result, `alliancePrompt` |
| Both alternate routes; the same pair fires from `[When Digivolving]` | publicly digivolves from the %s route and fires the same pair (2 rows) | `digivolutionRequirement`, `WhenDigivolving` actions |
| Illegal source refused on both alternate and printed routes | refuses an ineligible level-3 source on both the alternate and the printed routes | `digivolutionRequirement` |
| `until their turn ends` duration | keeps the -2000 through the opponent's turn and restores it on the next own turn | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` |

#### Evidence details

All behavioral tests drive public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `respondDecision`, `respondAlliance`, `endPhase`, `surrender`). No test uses `advance().fire`.

- **-2000 DP.** Playing Ankylomon for exactly 5 memory (6 to 1) reduces one chosen opposing Digimon from 5000 to 3000 and leaves the second opposing Digimon at 5000. Both own Digimon and the Shakkoumon card in hand are unchanged when the optional DNA is declined.
- **DNA accept.** A suspended BT23-027 Angemon plus the played Ankylomon merge into the exact Shakkoumon hand instance. The result is unsuspended, its stack is exactly those two instances, the seat has one permanent left, memory is 6 - 5 = 1 (Shakkoumon's `[DNA Digivolve]` cost is 0), and the hand holds only the DNA bonus draw's exact instance.
- **Refusals.** Three fixtures prove the DP clause still resolves while the DNA does not: one own Digimon, a level-3 partner, and Shakkoumon in trash instead of hand. Each asserts the exact battle-area instance set, the exact hand instance set, the exact trash card ids, and no pending decision.
- **Q5317.** With the target at 2000 DP, the -2000 takes it to 0. The event log shows `cardPlayed` for BT23-032 with `mechanic: "dna"`, then `effectResolved` for `BT23-050/ir-6-0`, and only then the target's `cardsMoved` from battleArea to trash. The DNA therefore resolved while the 0-DP Digimon was still on the board, which is exactly what Q5317 states. Endpoint: the target's exact instance is the only card in the opponent trash and the opponent battle area is empty.
- **Q5318.** BT23-018 Garurumon with BT23-017 Betamon underneath attacks; Betamon's inherited effect plays Ankylomon (cost 5, Hudie) without paying, under a permanent digivolution restriction. The board is then exactly Blue Lv.4 Garurumon plus Black/Yellow Lv.4 Ankylomon, which is the only pair that could satisfy Shakkoumon's `Yellow Lv.4 + black/blue Lv.4`. The DNA is refused: Shakkoumon stays in hand, the seat still has two permanents, and `isRestricted(anky, "digivolve")` is true. A control test plays the same Ankylomon normally onto the same host and the DNA succeeds, so the refusal is caused by the restriction and not by an illegal pair.
- **Q5319.** Hudiemon (BT23-101) with Erika Mishima (BT23-084) underneath stays on the board while Ankylomon's On Play consumes the suspended ally and Ankylomon itself. The result enters unsuspended. Hudiemon then attacks publicly; the `alliancePrompt` event lists the new Shakkoumon permanent among `eligibleAllyIds`, `respondAlliance` suspends it, and the opponent's two security cards are both checked — the extra ＜Alliance＞ security check. This proves the operative half of Q5319 (an unsuspended DNA result is a legal ＜Alliance＞ ally).
- **`if it's your turn`.** Shakkoumon's `[All Turns]` leave-the-battle-area replacement is used as the public route to an `[On Play]` on the opponent's turn: the opponent deletes Shakkoumon in battle, Ankylomon is played from its digivolution cards, the mandatory -2000 lands on the attacker (12000 to 10000), and the DNA clause does not fire even though a second Lv.4 and a Shakkoumon card in hand are available (the same pair the Q5318 control proves is legal on your own turn).
- **Evolution.** Both alternate routes are taken publicly with `useAlternateCost: true` and the matching `alternateRequirementIndex`. BT1-027 Armadillomon pays the printed 2; BT23-037 Tentomon pays 1 because its own `[Your Turn]` effect reduces a digivolution into a `[CS]` card by 1. Each asserts the source and Ankylomon instances inside the resulting Shakkoumon stack, the -2000 endpoint, and the two bonus draws (digivolution then DNA) as exact instances in order. BT1-009 Monodramon (Red Lv.3, no CS) is refused on both alternate indices and on the printed route, with hand, source and memory unchanged.
- **Duration.** Through `startTurnLoop()` and public `endPhase`, the target stays at 3000 during the opponent's Main phase and is back at 5000 on the next own Main.

#### Defect found and fixed

`BT23-050.ts` compiled the DNA result filter as `{ tokens: ["Shakkoumon"], match: "name" }`. `matching/definition.ts:352` implements `"name"` as a substring test, while `"nameExact"` (line 354) is the exact test. A printed `[Shakkoumon]` is an exact-name condition, and the peer card BT23-027 already uses `nameExact` for the same clause. Both occurrences (OnPlay and WhenDigivolving) are now `nameExact`. This is local to the card module; no engine or catalog change was needed.

#### Commands and results

#### Exact-name sweep (session 2)

The printed route reads "[Digivolve] [Armadillomon]/Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2". The bracketed name with no "in name" wording is an EQUALITY gate, but the module used `names`, a substring gate in `engine/cards/cardData.ts` `matchGatedRequirement` (~line 481). Changed to `namesExact: ["Armadillomon"]`. No released card's name merely contains "Armadillomon", so the near-name negative uses a synthetic level-3 base without the [CS] trait ("BlackArmadillomon") passed straight to `matchingAlternateDigivolutionRequirement`: red before the fix, green after. The positive [Armadillomon] route (BT1-027) and the level-3 [CS] route stay green. The DNA target [Shakkoumon] already used `match: "nameExact"`.

### BT23-051 — Golemon

#### Card

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Name | Golemon |
| Set / rarity | BT23 / U |
| Colors | Black, Red |
| Level / form | 4 / Champion |
| Play cost | 5 |
| DP | 5000 |
| Attribute | Virus |
| Types | Mineral / Hudie / CS |
| Digivolve costs | 3 from Black Lv.3; 3 from Red Lv.3 |

Printed text (official card list, `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/official-bt23.html` lines 4137-4200):

```
[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2
＜Alliance＞
＜Blocker＞
[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon suspends, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 4000 DP or less.
[Your Turn] This Digimon can't attack your opponent's Digimon.
```

The catalog record in `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` matches the official list
field for field. No discrepancy, no catalog edit needed.

#### Rulings

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-051` returns no knowledge-base entries, and `KB-INDEX.md`
line 57 also lists 0 Q&A ids. There is no card-specific ruling to cover. The generic
mechanics involved are Alliance timing (`ALLIANCE-TIMING-MECHANISM.md`, Q5257) and the
once-per-turn reset, both exercised through the real turn loop.

#### Clause -> test -> IR map

| # | Printed clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| C1 | Card identity | catalog record | "matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause" |
| C2 | `[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2` | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` (`level: 3`, `traits: ["CS"]`, `cost: 2`, `isAlternate: true`) | "digivolves from a Lv.3 CS source for exactly 2 memory and draws"; "pays the printed 3 on the normal route from a red Lv.3"; "rejects a non-CS off-color Lv.3 source on either route"; "rejects a Lv.4 CS source on the alternate route" |
| C3 | `＜Alliance＞` | `effects[0]` Static keyword | "resolves its own Alliance by suspending another Digimon, not itself"; "deletes when an ally's Alliance suspends it rather than only when it attacks"; keyword-seam test |
| C4 | `＜Blocker＞` | `effects[1]` Static keyword | "blocks on the opponent's turn, suspends, and deletes there under its own per-turn allowance"; keyword-seam test |
| C5 | `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon suspends, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with 4000 DP or less.` | `effects[2]`: `AllTurns` / `OncePerTurn` / `SubTrigger whenSuspended` (`sourceFilter.isSelfRef`) -> `Delete` (`controller: "opponent"`, `kind: ["Digimon"]`, `dp lte 4000`, `count: 1`) | "suspends on a public player attack and deletes exactly one opponent Digimon at 4000 DP or less"; "does nothing when every opponent Digimon is above 4000 DP and never touches its own board"; "refuses a second suspension's delete in the same turn"; "deletes again on the next own turn through the real turn loop"; the block test (opponent's turn); the Alliance-ally test; structural test "once per turn deletes one opposing Digimon..." |
| C6 | `[Your Turn] This Digimon can't attack your opponent's Digimon.` | `effects[3]`: `YourTurn` `Restrict` `cantAttackDigimon`, `isSelf`, `permanent` | "can attack the opponent player but not an opposing Digimon"; the block test proves the restriction does not stop blocking; structural test "cannot attack opponent Digimon during your turn" |

##### What each behavioral test pins

- **Player attack, delete on suspension.** Golemon attacks the player through the `attack`
  intent. The Alliance prompt is declined, the exact 4000-DP opponent permanent id is gone,
  the 2000-DP and 4001-DP permanents remain (battle area length 2), the deleted card's
  instance id is in the opponent trash, Golemon is suspended, the opponent's own board is
  untouched (length 2), security drops 3 -> 2, and no decision is pending.
- **DP boundary and controller boundary.** With only a 4001-DP opponent Digimon, nothing is
  deleted (its instance id is not in trash, trash holds only the checked security card) and
  neither of the controller's own Digimon leaves.
- **Once per turn, same turn.** After the first attack deletes one, the board is arranged
  with the effect verb `unsuspend` (no public intent unsuspends mid-turn on this board) and
  a second public `attack` re-suspends Golemon. The second Digimon survives; security dropped
  to 1, proving two attacks actually happened.
- **Once per turn, reset.** Through `startTurnLoop()`: own Main -> attack (first delete),
  the turn passes, opponent ends their Main, own Main again with Golemon unsuspended and the
  second Digimon still present (asserted), then a second attack deletes it.
- **Blocker on the opponent's turn.** The opponent attacks the player, `declareBlock` puts
  Golemon in the way; Golemon suspends, its delete removes the 4000-DP bystander, combat then
  removes the 3000-DP attacker, Golemon survives suspended, and security stays at 3.
- **Alliance, both directions.** Golemon's own Alliance offers the other Digimon and not
  itself, suspends the ally, and Golemon reaches 8000 DP with `securityAttack` 2 while the
  block window is held open by a 9000-DP ＜Blocker＞ wall. In the other direction, a
  Cendrillmon (EX11-024) Alliance that suspends Golemon fires Golemon's own delete.
- **Digivolution.** The alternate CS route pays exactly 2 (memory 2 -> 0) from an off-color
  Lv.3 CS source (BT23-062 Dracmon, Purple), keeps the source as the only stack card, and
  draws the one card the deck was staged with. The normal route pays the printed 3 from a
  red Lv.3. A plain blue Lv.3 (BT1-029) is rejected on both routes with `invalid-evolution`
  and no memory spent; a Lv.4 CS card (BT23-041) is rejected on the alternate route.

Fixture note: `BT23-037` Tentomon was the previous alternate-route source. It carries
"reduce the digivolution cost by 1" for CS targets, so it could never prove the printed
cost 2. The suite now uses BT23-062, which has no cost modifier.

#### Commands and results

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-051.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  Test Files  1 passed (1)
  Tests  16 passed (16)
  Duration  3.28s
```

Log: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-051-vitest.log`.

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck   # no BT23-051 diagnostics; unrelated pre-existing
                                     # errors in other lanes' files remain
pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-051.test.ts
                                     # 2 no-explicit-any warnings on the pre-existing
                                     # structural-IR casts, matching the suite's house style
pnpm exec oxfmt apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-051.test.ts   # formatted
git diff --check                     # clean
```

#### Defects found

1. **Prior suite proved nothing behavioral for the suspension trigger.** It drove the
   trigger with `advance().verb.suspend`, an injected-timing call. Replaced with public
   `attack`, `declareBlock` and `respondAlliance` intents.
2. **Prior digivolve test never pinned a cost.** It asserted `{ok: true}` only, and used a
   source (BT23-037) whose own text reduces the CS digivolution cost by 1, so the printed
   "Cost 2" was never exercised. Both routes now pin memory paid, stack identity and the
   bonus draw.
3. **Prior once-per-turn test never proved the reset.** No turn loop ran, and the second
   suspension was injected. Both the same-turn refusal and the next-own-turn reset are now
   driven by real intents, with the intermediate state asserted at each step.

No card-module change was needed: `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-051.ts` is correct as
compiled. It still carries the generator's `// @ts-nocheck` header, which is the
auto-generated marker the whole IR-card family shares; removing it is a generator-lane
decision, not a card fix.

#### Retained reds / skips

None. All 16 tests are green with no `it.fails` or `it.skip`.

#### Remaining gaps

- The `[Your Turn]` qualifier on the attack restriction cannot be falsified from the other
  side: no rule lets a Digimon attack during the opponent's turn, so "the restriction is
  absent on the opponent's turn" has no observable consequence. The block test shows the
  restriction does not leak into blocking, which is the only adjacent behavior.
- Mid-turn re-suspension has no public route on a minimal board, so the same-turn refusal
  arranges the board with the `unsuspend` effect verb. The suspension being tested is still
  produced by a public attack intent.
- The delete has no target-choice branch to refuse (it is mandatory and not "up to"), so
  there is no optional-refusal test; the no-legal-target case is covered instead.

### BT23-052 — Consulmon

#### Card

- Black, Digimon, Level 4, play cost 4, DP 4000.
- Evolution cost: Black, Level 3, 2 memory.
- Forms: Sup., Appmon. Attribute: Life. Type: Saving.
- Link: DP +3000, requirement `[Link] [Appmon] trait: Cost 2`.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Security]` At the end of the battle, play this card without paying the cost.
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` 1 of your opponent's Digimon can't attack players until their turn ends.
3. `[Link] [When Linking]` This Digimon gains `<Reboot>` and `<Blocker>` until your opponent's turn ends.

Rulings: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-052` returns "no knowledge-base entries". KB-INDEX lists 0 Q&A ids, so there is no ruling coverage to prove.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity | `compiled.linkRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` | "matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause" |
| All clauses compiled | four effect entries | "compiles the Security, On Play, When Digivolving and Link clauses" |
| `[Security]` play without cost, survivor case, then `[On Play]` chain | `trigger:"Security"`, `timing:"endOfBattle"`, `SubTrigger whenSecurityBattleEnded` -> `PlayWithoutCost` from trash, `payCost:false` | "survives its security battle, plays itself for free after it, and then restricts the attacker's board" |
| `[Security]` when the security battle deletes it | same (source zone `trash`) | "plays itself from the trash even when the security battle deletes it" |
| `[On Play]` restriction, target count and controller scope | `Restrict attackPlayers`, `untilOpponentTurnEnd`, `{controller:"opponent", kind:["Digimon"], count:1}` | "restricts exactly one opposing Digimon on a public play and pays the printed 4 memory" |
| What the restriction forbids and when it lapses | same | "blocks the restricted Digimon's player attack, allows its Digimon attack, and expires at the turn boundary" |
| Target zone scope | zone-less permanent filter defaults to battle area | "leaves a breeding-area Digimon alone when the opponent controls nothing else" |
| `[When Digivolving]` on a legal public route | `trigger:"WhenDigivolving"` same `Restrict` | "digivolves from a Black Level 3 for 2 memory, draws 1, and restricts an opposing Digimon" |
| Evolution legality | `evoCosts` | "refuses an off-color and an off-level digivolution source" |
| `[Link]` grants | `AllTurns` + `isLinked`, `SubTrigger whenLinked` -> two `GainKeyword` `untilOpponentTurnEnd` | "links to an Appmon for cost 2, adds 3000 DP, and grants Reboot and Blocker to the host" |

#### Evidence highlights

- Security clause, both battle outcomes. Attacker 3000 DP: Consulmon survives, the attacker's card lands in trash, Consulmon leaves security (0 left), is not in trash, and stands alone on the defender's battle area. Attacker 7000 DP: the `securityChecked` event reports `securityDigimonDeleted: true` and Consulmon is still played onto the board from trash. Memory is unchanged in both cases, and the `cardsMoved` event for Consulmon comes after `securityChecked`, so the play really happens at the end of the battle.
- The security play chains into `[On Play]`: the attacker's untouched second Digimon ends up restricted from attacking players, which also proves the controller framing (the security player's "opponent" is the attacking player).
- Public play costs 4 memory (6 -> 2), hand empties, exactly one of two opposing Digimon is restricted, and the controller's own Digimon is not.
- Restriction meaning and boundary, driven through the real turn loop: on the opponent's turn the restricted Digimon's player attack is rejected (`ok:false`, no security lost, attacker not suspended) while its attack on a suspended Digimon is accepted and deletes that Digimon. After the opponent's turn ends the restriction is gone. This is the printed "until their turn ends" boundary — the engine re-frames `untilOpponentTurnEnd` per target seat in `primitives.ts durationForTarget`.
- Evolution: Black Level 3 (BT2-052) source costs 2 memory, draws the one deck card, keeps the base card in the stack under Consulmon, and applies the restriction. Red Level 3 and Black Level 4 sources are both rejected with no memory or hand change.
- Link: cost 2 (memory 5 -> 3), host DP +3000, host gains both Reboot and Blocker.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-052.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` -> 1 file, 10 tests passed, ~2.2 s. Log: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-052-vitest.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` -> no errors in BT23-052 files. The run still fails on `src/cards/BT23/BT23-049.test.ts:66,232` (another lane's file). Log: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-052-typecheck.log`.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` on both BT23-052 files -> clean. `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` -> clean. `git diff --check` -> clean.

#### Defects

- No card-module defect. `BT23-052.ts` is unchanged: the restriction target, restriction kind, duration and link IR all match the printed text. The zone-less `kind:["Digimon"]` filter is safe here because permanent targeting defaults to the battle area (`interpreter/targeting/permanents.ts`, `allowsZone`); the breeding test proves it behaviorally.
- Catalog discrepancy (coordinator-owned, not edited): `cards.json` `linkEffect` for BT23-052 reads `"[When Linking] [When Linking] This Digimon gains ..."` — the `[When Linking]` tag is duplicated. `effectText` also carries a double space between the `[Security]` clause and the `[On Play] [When Digivolving]` clause. The test therefore asserts `linkRequirement` but not `linkEffect`.

#### Test-fixture note

Under the real turn loop, seat 0's main phase closes on its own once the seat has nothing left to do. The turn-boundary test keeps a spare hand card so the phase stays open after Consulmon is played; without it the engine passes the turn and the following `endPhase` intent is rejected as `not-your-turn`.

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained reds or skips.
- Not covered: Consulmon played from security during the opponent's turn and the restriction's expiry at that same turn's end (the security test asserts the restriction is applied, not its lapse). The duration path is the same one the turn-boundary test exercises.
- Blocker/Reboot granted by the link are asserted as keyword grants, not through a full block or a Reboot unsuspend.

| IR trace | 2 | Every compiled effect entry mapped to a printed clause and asserted. |

| Peer / stack proof | 1 | Real evolution route with stack identity, bonus draw and two illegal-source negatives; link proved on a real Appmon host. No cross-card stacking or Blocker/Reboot interaction test. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Coordinator-owned. |
| **Total** | **7/10** | |

### BT23-053 — Strikedramon

#### Card

Source: committed catalog record `BT23-053`. Black Digimon, level 4, play cost 5, DP 5000, Champion, Vaccine, traits Dragonkin/CS. Printed digivolution cost Black level 3 for 2.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2` — an alternate, colour-free recipe.
2. `[Your Turn] When any of your Option cards are placed in the battle area, this Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with [Cyberdramon] in its name or the [CS] in the hand with the digivolution cost reduced by 2.`
3. Inherited: `[All Turns] This Digimon gets +1000 DP.`

The catalog `effectText` carries two U+00A0 characters (`[CS] trait` and `[Cyberdramon] in`). The exact-text assertion escapes both. No catalog discrepancy against the official card text.

Rulings: `node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-053` returns no knowledge-base entries, matching KB-INDEX (`BT23-053 | 0 | None`). No Q&A ids to cover.

Peer with the identical printed wording: `BT22-037` Chirinmon (`... may digivolve into a Digimon card with [Kentaurosmon] or [Mitamamon] in its name or the [CS] trait in the hand with the digivolution cost reduced by 2`). It is used as the live [CS]-branch target below, so the shared shape is exercised, not just asserted.

#### Clause to test to IR mapping

| Printed clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity + alternate recipe | `digivolutionRequirement: [{level:3, traits:["CS"], cost:2, isAlternate:true}]` | `matches every catalog field...` (exact `effectText`/`inheritedEffectText`) |
| Clause 2 shape | `YourTurn` > `SubTrigger whenOptionPlayed` > `Digivolve` | `may digivolve from hand into Cyberdramon or a CS Digimon for 2 less...` |
| "When any of your Option cards are placed in the battle area" > name branch, cost reduced by 2 | `sourceFilter {controller:"mine", kind:["Option"]}`, `reduceCost: 2`, `from:["hand"]` | `digivolves into Cyberdramon for exactly 1 when its controller publicly plays an Option` + the unreduced control |
| "reduced by 2" baseline | — | `charges the unreduced cost of 3 for the same evolution without an Option placement` |
| "or the [CS]" trait branch | `nameOrTrait` second entry | `takes the [CS] trait branch into an off-colour CS Digimon for exactly 1` |
| "may" | `optional: true` | `leaves the board untouched when the controller declines the optional digivolution` |
| Target must actually be Cyberdramon-named or [CS] | `nameOrTrait` | `stays put and asks nothing when the hand holds no Cyberdramon-named or CS card` |
| It is still a digivolution | engine route check | `refuses hand cards that match the name or trait but have no legal digivolution route` |
| "your Option cards" | `sourceFilter.controller: "mine"` | `ignores an opponent's Option placed while its own controller's Main is open` |
| `[Your Turn]` | `trigger: "YourTurn"` | `ignores an Option the opponent publicly plays on the opponent's turn` + `does not fire for its controller's own Option placed during the opponent's turn` |
| Inherited `[All Turns] +1000 DP` | `AllTurns` > `ModifyDP 1000 permanent`, `isInherited` | `grants the inherited host +1000 DP on its controller's turn and the opponent's turn` |
| `[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS]: Cost 2` and printed Black Lv.3 for 2 | `digivolutionRequirement` + `evoCosts` | `digivolves for 2 from a black level-3 source...`, `...off-colour level-3 [CS] source...`, `rejects an off-colour level-3 source without the [CS] trait`, `rejects a level-4 [CS] source` |

#### How the clause is driven publicly

`BT23-100` Hudie Net Café is a White Option whose printed `[Main]` body draws 1 and then places itself in the battle area. A plain public `playCard` intent therefore reaches `primitives.placeOptionAsPermanent` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/primitives.ts:1239`), the only production site that fires `whenOptionPlayed`. Strikedramon itself supplies the on-field [CS] Digimon that waives BT23-100's colour requirement, so no extra board is needed. Every positive test runs inside a real `startTurnLoop()` with `advance().waitForMainPhase(0)` and a neutral playable card (`BT1-020`) in hand so Main stays open.

Two negatives cannot be reached by any public intent and say so in the file:

- an opponent's Option placed while seat 0's Main is open (the opponent cannot play a card on seat 0's turn), and
- seat 0's own Option placed on the opponent's turn (BT23-100's placement rides its own `[Main]` body).

Both drive only `advance().verb.placeOptionAsPermanent`; the turn, the phase and the watcher are production.

#### Cost evidence

`BT23-055` Cyberdramon costs 3 from a Black level 4. The reduced run pays `10 - 3 (Option play cost) - 1 = 6`; the control run, with no Option, pays `10 - 3 = 7`. The pair pins the reduction at exactly 2 rather than asserting `{ok:true}`.

The [CS] branch uses `BT22-037` Chirinmon, a **Yellow** level 5. Its only recipe that fits a Black Strikedramon is its own alternate `Lv.4 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3`, so the observed 1 also pins that the alternate requirement — not a colour recipe — was the one charged.

#### Defect found and fixed

`apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-053.ts` carried a `// @ts-nocheck` header, which suppressed type checking of the whole hand-authored IR. Removed. `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` passes clean with no other change to the module; the IR itself needed no correction.

#### Mutation evidence (each clause bites)

Each mutation was applied to the module, the focused suite was run, and the module was restored.

| Mutation | Failing tests |
| --- | --- |
| `reduceCost: 2` -> `0` | 3 (IR + both cost endpoints) |
| `sourceFilter.controller: "mine"` -> `"any"` | 2 (IR + opponent-Option-on-your-turn) |
| `trigger: "YourTurn"` -> `"AllTurns"` | 3 (IR + own Option on opponent's turn + inherited-DP turn walk) |
| `nameOrTrait` tokens scrambled | 3 |
| `from: ["hand"]` -> `["trash"]` | 3 |
| `optional: true` -> `false` | 2 (IR + decline branch) |
| inherited `amount: 1000` -> `0` | 1 |
| `digivolutionRequirement.traits: ["CS"]` -> `["ZZ"]` | 2 (IR + alternate recipe) |

Note on the `[Your Turn]` keyword: with the earlier, weaker version of the opponent-turn test the `YourTurn` -> `AllTurns` mutation stayed green, because the engine independently blocks an off-turn digivolution. The test now asserts the placement actually happened and that `state.turnSeat` is the opponent's, and the mutation fails. The clause is therefore redundantly enforced — the trigger keyword is not the only thing holding it.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-053.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file / 16 tests passed, 5.81s. Log: `logs/BT23-053-focused.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — passed with no errors. The pre-existing `src/engine/combat/controller.ts:617` failure noted in the worker brief no longer reproduces on this branch.
- `pnpm exec oxfmt apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-053.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-053.test.ts` — formatted, 2 files.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` on the same two files — 2 `no-explicit-any` warnings on the `as any` IR probes, the same pattern the accepted exemplars use. No errors.
- `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained reds and no skips. No engine seam was hit.
- The clause has no printed once-per-turn cap, so repeated Option placements in one turn should each offer the digivolution. That repeat case is not tested: the second placement would need a second Option in hand and a legal further route from the level 5 the first placement produced, which no clean fixture in this set provides. Behaviour is unmodelled rather than known-wrong.
- `[Your Turn]` is proven behaviourally but is redundantly enforced by the engine's own off-turn digivolution block, so the test does not isolate the trigger keyword alone.

### BT23-054 — Magnamon

#### Card

- Catalog: `packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json`, `cardId: "BT23-054"`.
- Name: Magnamon. Colors Black/Blue. Level 4, play cost 7, DP 7000.
- EvoCost: Black Lv.3 cost 4; Blue Lv.3 cost 4.
- Forms: Armor Form. Attributes: Free. Types: Holy Warrior, Royal Knight, CS.
- Printed text:

```
[Digivolve] [Veemon]/Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3

＜Blocker＞
＜Armor Purge＞
[On Play] [When Digivolving] ＜Draw 1＞ Then, 1 of your Digimon with the
[Royal Knight] or [CS] trait can't be returned to hands or decks by your
opponent's effects until their turn ends.
```

The catalog text matches the official card list. No discrepancy to report.

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-054` returns no knowledge-base entries, and
the Knowledge base index section of this document lists 0 Q&A ids for this card. There is no Q&A
coverage obligation, so no Q&A is left untested.

#### Printed clauses

| id | clause |
| --- | --- |
| C1 | [Digivolve] [Veemon]: Cost 3 (alternate route, bracketed exact name) |
| C2 | [Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3 (alternate route) |
| C3 | Printed EvoCost Black/Blue Lv.3 cost 4 (normal route) |
| C4 | ＜Blocker＞ |
| C5 | ＜Armor Purge＞ |
| C6 | [On Play] ＜Draw 1＞ |
| C7 | [On Play] Then, 1 of your Digimon with [Royal Knight] or [CS] can't be returned to hands or decks by opponent effects until their turn ends |
| C8 | [When Digivolving] ＜Draw 1＞ |
| C9 | [When Digivolving] the same protection tail |

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| clause | test | IR node |
| --- | --- | --- |
| card data | matches every catalog field, the printed text and the compiled shape | `digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| C6, C7 | publicly plays for exactly 7, draws 1 and protects exactly one eligible Digimon | `OnPlay` -> `Draw`, `Restrict` |
| C6, C7 | declares the same Draw-then-protect body on both [On Play] and [When Digivolving] | both trigger bodies |
| C7 | does not offer a breeding-area [CS] Digimon as the protection target | `Restrict.target.filter` (zone-less = battle area) |
| C7 | blocks an opponent effect returning the protected Digimon to hand or to deck | `restriction: beReturned` |
| C7 | still lets the controller's own effect return the protected Digimon | `byOpponentEffectsOnly: true` |
| C7 | keeps the protection through the opponent's whole turn and drops it once that turn ends | `duration: untilOpponentTurnEnd` |
| C1, C8, C9 | digivolves from [Veemon] for exactly 3 on the alternate route, drawing and protecting | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| C1, C3 | pays the printed 4 from the same [Veemon] base when the alternate route is not requested | `evoCosts` vs `digivolutionRequirement[0]` |
| C2 | digivolves from a Lv.3 [CS] base for exactly 3 even though its colors miss the printed EvoCost | `digivolutionRequirement[1]` |
| C2 | lets a base's own cost reduction stack onto the Cost 3 route (BT23-037 reduces it to 2) | `digivolutionRequirement[1]` + base static |
| C3 | pays the printed 4 from a plain Blue Lv.3 base even when the alternate flag is set | `evoCosts` |
| C1 negative | refuses an illegal source: ExVeemon / a Red Lv.3 with neither gate | `namesExact` |
| C4 | exposes ＜Blocker＞ / ＜Armor Purge＞ through the live keyword seam; ＜Blocker＞ switches a declared player attack onto Magnamon | Static keyword |
| C5 | ＜Armor Purge＞ spares Magnamon from a lost battle by trashing its own top card | Static keyword |

Both alternate-cost branches are pinned: every alternate assertion states the memory before
and after, so `useAlternateCost: true` is never proved by `{ ok: true }` alone.

#### Defect found and fixed

##### `[Veemon]` was a substring name gate

`compiled.digivolutionRequirement[0]` used `names: ["Veemon"]`. The engine matches `names`
by substring — `apps/api/src/engine/cards/cardData.ts`, `matchGatedRequirement`, comment
"Name gate: base name must contain at least one token (substring)". Every card whose name
contains "Veemon" therefore took the printed `[Veemon]` route for 3 memory, including
`ExVeemon` (BT3-025, Lv.4) and `DemiVeemon`. Because the name route carries no level gate,
this let a Lv.4 base and a Digi-Egg name stand in for a Lv.3 [Veemon].

Fix in `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-054.ts`: `namesExact: ["Veemon"]`, consumed at
`cardData.ts` "Exact-name gate: one of the base's effective names must EQUAL one token".

Red-then-green proof, test "refuses an illegal source: ExVeemon, a Lv.4 whose name only
CONTAINS Veemon":

- before the fix: `expected { ok: true } to deeply equal { ok: false, reason: "invalid-evolution" }`
- after the fix: passes.

##### Checked and NOT a defect

- Zone on the protection filter. A zone-less permanent filter already resolves to the
  battle area only (`targeting/permanents.ts`, `allowsZone` returns `zone === "battleArea"`
  when `filter.zone` is unset), so no `zone: "battleArea"` is needed. Pinned by the
  breeding-area test.
- `nameOrTrait` with `match: "trait"` reads printed forms/attributes/types, so
  `[Royal Knight]` and `[CS]` are whole-token trait identities, not substrings.

#### Prior test weaknesses replaced

The previous suite used `advance.fire(EffectTiming.OnPlay, ...)` for the whole On Play body
(structural only), asserted `{ ok: true }` on digivolution without any cost assertion or the
`useAlternateCost` flag, had no illegal-source negative, proved the keywords only through
`hasKeyword`, never exercised return-to-deck, and switched `state.turnSeat` by hand instead
of opening a resolution-source window. The new suite drives `startTurnLoop` + `playCard`,
`digivolve` with and without `useAlternateCost`, `declareBlock`, a real attack for Armor
Purge, and `enterEffectResolution` for the opponent-vs-own controller boundary.

#### Commands and results

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-054.test.ts \
  --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
Test Files  1 passed (1)
Tests  17 passed (17)
Duration  7.37s
```

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck        # clean, no BT23-054 diagnostics
pnpm exec oxlint  apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-054.ts BT23-054.test.ts   # clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check  (both files)     # All matched files use the correct format
git diff --check                          # clean
```

Regression sweep `src/engine/effects/reencoded-ir.test.ts src/engine/cards`:
208 passed, 1 failed — `src/engine/cards/irKindTier1Cluster.test.ts` "BT1-086 SetMemory"
(`endPhase` rejected with `wrong-phase`). Confirmed PRE-EXISTING and unrelated: it fails
identically with `BT23-054.ts` reverted to its committed content. It belongs to the engine
lane (turn-loop / phase changes in `GameEngine.ts`).

Logs: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-054-vitest.log`,
`BT23-054-typecheck.log`, `BT23-054-regression.log`.

#### Remaining gaps

- No Q&A exists for this card, so trait-alias questions (for example whether a granted
  [Royal Knight] trait qualifies a target) are unresolved by ruling; the tests use printed
  traits only.
- ＜Armor Purge＞ is proved on the deletion-by-battle path only. The engine's conformance
  suite already covers the keyword's other entry points, and no BT23-054 clause depends on them.
- The protection is proved against `returnToHand` / `returnToDeck`. The "returned to decks"
  wording also covers security-deck placement; the engine normalizes both into the single
  `beReturned` restriction (`interpreter/actions/restrictions.ts`), and no separate
  security-return verb is exposed to exercise it independently.
