### BT23-055 — Cyberdramon

Sources: committed catalog record `BT23-055`; the official BT23 card list capture in `logs/official-bt23.html`; KB Q5320; `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.ts` and its colocated test.

#### Card

Black level 5 Digimon, play cost 7, DP 7000, Ultimate, Vaccine, Cyborg/Hudie/CS, evo cost Black level 4 for 3, rarity R.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — an alternate digivolution route.
2. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] Delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with a play cost of 5 or less.`
3. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon would leave the battle area, by trashing 1 of your Option cards in the battle area, it doesn't leave.`
4. Inherited: `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon with [Cyberdramon] or [Justimon] in its name or the [CS] trait would leave the battle area, by trashing 1 of your Option cards in the battle area, it doesn't leave.`

The catalog text matches the official list byte for byte, including the non-breaking spaces after `[CS]` and `[Justimon]`. No catalog discrepancy.

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-055` returns one Q&A.

| Ruling | Content | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5320 | An Option card "in the battle area" is one put there by a "place this card in the battle area" effect. | Test `does not count an Option card in the hand or trash as being in the battle area (Q5320)`, plus every prevention test, which reaches its Option cards only through BT23-100 Hudie Net Cafe's public `[Main] ＜Draw 1＞ Then, place this card in the battle area`. |

#### Clause to test to IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity and both printed texts | `matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause` | `digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| Clause 2, play route and cost boundary | `deletes an opposing play-cost-5 Digimon on play and leaves the cost-6 one` | `OnPlay` -> `Delete` with `playCostLte: 5` |
| Clause 2, zone boundary | `cannot reach the opponent's breeding Digimon with the On Play deletion` | the same `Delete` target filter |
| Clause 2, digivolve route | `digivolves for 3 from a black level-4 on its printed route and deletes when digivolving` | `WhenDigivolving` -> `Delete` |
| Clause 1, alternate route | `digivolves for 3 from an off-color level-4 CS card on both cost branches` | `digivolutionRequirement` `{ level: 4, traits: ["CS"], cost: 3, isAlternate: true }` |
| Clause 1, illegal source | `rejects a level-4 source that is neither black nor CS` | the same requirement |
| Clause 3, prevention and cost | `survives a lost battle by trashing 1 of its controller's placed Option cards` | `AllTurns` -> `Replacement wouldLeavePlay`, `sourceFilter.isSelfRef`, trash cost |
| Clause 3, Q5320 cost source | `does not count an Option card in the hand or trash as being in the battle area (Q5320)` | cost filter `zone: "battleArea"`, `placedInBattleAreaByEffect: true` |
| Clause 3, once per turn | `prevents leaving only once per turn even with a second Option still placed` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Clause 3, reset | `regains the prevention on the following turn` | `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Clause 4, host protection | `protects the Digimon carrying it as a digivolution card` | inherited `Replacement`, `nameOrTrait` CS branch |
| Clause 4, no clause stacking | `grants the host exactly one prevention per turn, not one per printed and inherited clause` | inherited `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Clause 4, scope | `does not protect another CS Digimon or the opponent's CS Digimon` | inherited `sourceFilter.isSelfRef` |
| Full IR shape for both timings and both preventions | `compiles both deletion timings and both prevention clauses` | whole `compiled.effects` |

#### How the behavioral tests stay public

Every prevention test reaches an Option card in the battle area through the `playCard` intent on BT23-100 Hudie Net Cafe, paying its cost 3 out of memory and taking its `＜Draw 1＞`. The board is recomputed with `ready()` first, because Hudie Net Cafe's colour waiver is a continuous effect that a hand-laid board has not installed yet; without it the intent is refused with `color-requirement-unmet`.

Removal is driven by real combat wherever possible: Cyberdramon at 7000 DP attacks or is attacked by BT1-024 MetalTyrannomon (10000 DP) or BT1-042 LoaderLeomon (10000 DP), loses the battle, and would leave. The once-per-turn tests hand the turn to the opponent through `advance().runTurn(0)` and a driven `runOneTurn()` for seat 1, then re-arm `isSuspended` after the opponent's Main is authoritatively open, because the Active phase unsuspends the board.

Two tests use `advance().verb.deletePermanent(..., "byEffect")`: the ones about which permanent the inherited clause guards. No player intent deletes a Digimon you control outright, so the effect-driven delete primitive stands in for an opponent removal effect. Those three tests are the only non-intent-driven ones in the file.

#### Defect found and fixed

The inherited replacement guarded the wrong permanents.

- File: `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.ts`, the `isInherited: true` effect's `sourceFilter`.
- Before: `{ controllerDefault: "mine", kind: ["Digimon"], nameOrTrait: [...] }`.
- Why that is wrong: `runReplacement` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/replacement.ts` computes `protectsSelf` from `action.target?.isSelf` or `protectsFilter.isSelfRef`. With neither set, it installs the board-wide `protects` branch. That branch's seat gate reads `protectsFilter.controller` only — it never reads `controllerDefault` — so the clause protected every CS Digimon on the board, including the opponent's.
- Printed text: "When **this** Digimon ... would leave the battle area". The clause guards its own host and nothing else.
- Fix: add `isSelfRef: true` to that `sourceFilter`. The name and trait gate still applies, because the `protectsSelf` branch runs `permanentMatchesFilter` against the same filter.

Red-then-green proof, run with the fix reverted:

```
RED | does not protect another CS Digimon or the opponent's CS Digimon
RED | compiles both deletion timings and both prevention clauses
passed 12 failed 2
```

With the fix in place all 14 tests pass, including `protects the Digimon carrying it as a digivolution card`, which shows the host is still guarded.

This encoding is not unique to this card. `ST24-06`, `ST24-10` and `BT26-044` carry the same "this Digimon with X in its name or the Y trait" inherited prevention with the same `controllerDefault`-without-`isSelfRef` shape. Fixing them is out of this lane's allowed edits; the coordinator may want a sweep.

#### Behaviour confirmed as correct

- The On Play and When Digivolving deletion cannot reach the opponent's breeding-area Digimon. The zone-less target filter does not over-reach here.
- The `playCostLte: 5` boundary is exact: BT1-020 (cost 5) is deleted, BT1-019 (cost 6) is untouched.
- Digivolving from BT23-041 Kabuterimon (Green/Yellow, level 4, CS) succeeds for 3 memory. Because the CS alternate route costs the same 3 as the printed Black level 4 route, `useAlternateCost` does not change the price; the test pins both branches at 3 rather than accepting `{ok: true}` alone. BT1-037 Gorillamon (Blue, level 4, no CS) is refused with `invalid-evolution`.
- The prevention is genuinely once per turn, not once per available Option: with two placed Options, the second removal in the same turn goes through and leaves the spare Option in the battle area.
- The printed clause and the inherited clause do not stack on a host that carries BT23-055 as a digivolution card. The host gets exactly one prevention per turn.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, 14 tests passed, 2.81s. Log: `logs/BT23-055-vitest.log`.
- Typecheck: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.json` — no errors.
- Lint: `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.test.ts` — clean.
- Format: `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` on both files — clean.
- `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained reds and no skips.
- `BT23-catalog-sync.test.ts` now lists `BT23-055` as drifted, because the module changed and `packages/shared/src/effects/effects.json` is coordinator-owned. The persisted IR needs a re-sync. `BT23-055` was not in that drift list before this change.
- The `useAlternateCost` branches cannot be told apart on this card: both legal routes cost 3. If the engine ever mispriced the alternate route the test would catch it only against the value 3.
- The two `deletePermanent` tests model an opponent removal effect through the effect-driven primitive rather than a real opponent card, because no player intent deletes a Digimon its controller owns.

### BT23-056 — WereGarurumon

#### Card summary

The official list (`docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/official-bt23.html`) and the catalog record in
`packages/shared/src/cards/data/cards.json` agree field for field. No catalog discrepancy.

| Field | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Identity | BT23-056, Black, Digimon, Lv.5 Ultimate, Virus, Beastkin/CS |
| Cost / DP | Play 7 / 7000 DP |
| Digivolve cost 1 | Black Lv.4: cost 3 |
| C1 | `[Digivolve] Lv.4 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` (alternate route, any colour) |
| C2 | `＜Blocker＞` |
| C3 | `[On Play] [When Digivolving] If you have a Tamer with the [CS] trait, give 1 of your opponent's Digimon "[Start of Your Main Phase] This Digimon attacks." until their turn ends.` |
| C4 (inherited) | `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When attack targets change, ＜De-Digivolve 1＞ 1 of your opponent's Digimon.` |

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-056` returns one Q&A; `KB-INDEX.md` lists the same one.

| Id | Ruling | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5321 | You may give the effect to a Digimon that effects don't affect, but if that Digimon is still unaffected at the trigger timing, the gained effect does not trigger. | Covered by one test with both halves: the subscription installs on an immune Zephagamon (ST18-12), and at that Digimon's own main-phase start the immunity is live, so it does not attack. |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | Test | IR site |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Identity, costs, alternate requirement | "matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause" | `compiled.digivolutionRequirement`, `coverage`, `residual` |
| C2 Blocker | "blocks a real attack aimed at its controller" | `Static` -> `GainKeyword { Blocker, permanent }` |
| C3 On Play grant, opponent-only target, forced attack, duration expiry | "grants the chosen opponent Digimon a forced attack that fires in their own main phase" | `OnPlay` -> `SubTrigger { startOfYourMainPhase, on: opponent Digimon, duration: untilOpponentTurnEnd, actions: [Attack self] }` |
| C3 [CS] Tamer gate | "grants nothing without a Tamer that carries the [CS] trait" | `condition: youHave { kind: ["Tamer"], nameOrTrait: [{ tokens: ["CS"], match: "trait" }] }` |
| C1 + C3 When Digivolving | "digivolves off-colour from a Lv.4 [CS] source for 3 and grants from that route" | `WhenDigivolving` -> same SubTrigger; `digivolutionRequirement` |
| Printed Black route and illegal source | "takes the printed Black Lv.4 route for 3 and refuses a Lv.4 with neither colour nor [CS]" | catalog `evoCosts`, `digivolutionRequirement` |
| C4 inherited, once per turn, reset | "de-digivolves once per turn when a real block switches an attack target, and resets next turn" | `AllTurns` (`isInherited`, `frequency: OncePerTurn`) -> `SubTrigger { whenAttackTargetSwitched }` -> `DeDigivolve 1` |
| Q5321 | "grants onto an unaffected Digimon, which then never triggers the gained attack (Q5321)" | same `SubTrigger`, gated by the engine's `beAffected` restriction |

#### Evidence notes

Every behavioural test drives public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `declareBlock`,
`declineBlock`, `endPhase`, `surrender`) over the real turn loop (`startTurnLoop`). No test uses an
injected timing fire; the previous suite's `fireTiming(OnStartMainPhase)` and
`fireSubTrigger("whenAttackTargetSwitched")` shortcuts were replaced.

Endpoints asserted, not just the first mutation:

- Grant: subscription count on the chosen opponent permanent is 1 and on the granter's own Digimon
  is 0; the victim is unsuspended when the grant lands; at the victim's main-phase start it is
  suspended, one of its opponent's security cards is gone (3 -> 2), no attack is in flight and no
  decision is pending.
- Duration: the subscription is 0 on the next turn cycle and no further security card is checked.
- Digivolve: memory 3 -> 0, the source is the only digivolution card, exactly one card is drawn,
  and the illegal source leaves memory and the top card untouched.
- Inherited clause: the de-digivolve target is a Digimon that never enters a battle, so its stack
  change cannot be confused with a battle death. Cards removed from that stack are identified by
  instance id and located in the trash.

The two source cards used as fixtures are the printed routes: `BT23-041` (Lv.4 `[CS]`, Green/Yellow)
takes the alternate cost with `useAlternateCost: true`, `BT10-062` (Black Lv.4) takes the printed
colour route without it.

Fixture legality: security and deck contain only main-deck Digimon (`BT1-011`, `BT1-009`); no
Digi-Egg is placed outside breeding.

#### Defects found

None. The hand-authored IR in `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-056.ts` is correct as written:
`[CS]` Tamer gate present, target restricted to the opponent's Digimon, granted trigger anchored on
the chosen permanent, `untilOpponentTurnEnd` expiry, inherited clause on the attack-target-switch
bus with `OncePerTurn`. The module was not changed.

#### Interpretation risk (for the coordinator)

The inherited clause is modelled board-wide: the watcher fires on any attack-target switch, not
only on a switch involving its own host. That matches the printed English ("When attack targets
change", with no "this Digimon") and the interpreter's documented split at
`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/subTrigger.ts:417-425`, where only an
`isSelfRef` source filter narrows the event to the host. If the intended reading is host-scoped,
the IR would need `sourceFilter: { isSelfRef: true }`. No Q&A on this card settles it, so I left
the current model and flagged it rather than changing the module.

  Duration    7.09s

pnpm exec oxfmt --check apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-056.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-056.test.ts   # clean

#### Remaining gaps

  source (Green/Yellow) plus the printed Black route plus a rejection is the coverage.
- The `[When Digivolving]` grant is proven by the installed subscription rather than by running the
  granted attack a second time; the firing path is already proven end to end on the `[On Play]`
  branch and both branches compile to the identical SubTrigger.
- The board-wide vs host-scoped reading of the inherited clause is unresolved (see above).

### BT23-057 — Gankoomon

#### Card

- Lv.6 Mega, Black, Data, DP 11000, play cost 11.
- Traits: Holy Warrior / Royal Knight / CS.
- Printed digivolve costs: Black Lv.5 cost 3, Red Lv.5 cost 3.

Printed text (catalog matches the official list — no discrepancy found):

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3`
2. `When this card would be played, by returning 3 cards with [Huckmon], [Sistermon] or [Jesmon] in their names from your trash to the top or bottom of the deck, reduce the play cost by 5.`
3. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] You may play 1 [Hinukamuy] Token. (Digimon/White/6000 DP/<Alliance> <Reboot> <Blocker>) Then, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with a play cost of 6 or less. For each of your other Digimon, add 3 to this effect's play cost maximum.`

#### Rulings covered

| Id | Ruling | Where proved |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5322 | Returning only 2 of the 3 cards does not meet the "by" condition. | `refuses the reduction when only two matching cards sit in the trash (Q5322)` |

Q5322 is the only KB entry for this card (`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-057`).

#### Clause to test to IR map

| Clause | Test | IR |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Alternate digivolve Lv.5 w/[CS]: cost 3 | `digivolves from a Lv.5 [CS] source for the alternate cost of 3 and draws 1` | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 5, traits: ["CS"], cost: 3, isAlternate: true }]` |
| Alternate route needs the trait | `rejects a Lv.5 source without the [CS] trait on both routes` | same |
| Reduce play cost by 5 by returning 3 named trash cards to the top or bottom of the deck | `reduces the play cost by 5 by returning three named cards from the trash to the top/bottom of the deck` (both branches) | `Static / Replacement wouldBePlayed`, `cost.kind: "return"`, `to: "deckTopOrBottom"`, `mode: "reduceCost"`, `amount: 5` |
| The "by" cost is all-or-nothing (Q5322) | `refuses the reduction when only two matching cards sit in the trash (Q5322)` | `cost.target.count: 3` |
| The cost is a "you may" | `pays the full cost when the return cost is declined` | reducer offered through `ctx.ask.optional` |
| `[On Play]` token then delete, base ceiling 6 | `deletes only up to the base play-cost ceiling of 6 when the token is declined` | `OnPlay`: `PlayToken` (optional) then `Delete`, `playCostCeiling.base: 6` |
| Ceiling rises 3 per other own Digimon, the token included | `raises the deletion ceiling by 3 for the token and by 3 for each other own Digimon` | `playCostCeiling: { base: 6, raise: 3, per: 1, unit: "cards" }` |
| "your other Digimon" is the battle area only | `does not count a breeding-area Digimon toward the deletion ceiling` | `playCostCeiling.filter.zone: "battleArea"` (defect fix) |
| `[When Digivolving]` runs the same body | `digivolves from a Lv.5 [CS] source ...` asserts the token and the deletion | `WhenDigivolving` effect |
| Printed IR shape | `keeps the printed IR shape for the reduction and the token-then-delete body` | both trigger bodies |
| Catalog identity | `matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause` | `coverage: "full"`, `residual: []` |

All behavioral tests drive public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`) — no injected timing API.

#### Defects

##### Fixed (local, in `BT23-057.ts`)

`playCostCeiling.filter` had no `zone`, so `countMatching` (apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/scaling.ts:94-101) also counted the controller's breeding-area permanent. A Digimon in breeding raised the deletion ceiling from 6 to 9. Comprehensive rules 3-4-5-8 say cards in the breeding area can't be referenced.

Proof: `does not count a breeding-area Digimon toward the deletion ceiling` failed red before the fix (a cost-7 opponent Digimon was deleted) and passes with `zone: "battleArea"` added to both trigger bodies.

##### Interpretation recorded, not changed

"Then, delete 1 ..." resolves even when the player declines the optional token. No KB entry gates the second sentence on the first, and comprehensive rules 15-1-2 only fix the processing order. `pays the full cost when the return cost is declined` and `deletes only up to the base play-cost ceiling of 6 when the token is declined` both assert this branch.

#### Commands and results

```
pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-057.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
# 1 file, 11 tests passed, ~2.3s (log: logs/BT23-057-vitest.log)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
# no errors in BT23-057.ts / BT23-057.test.ts (the pre-existing engine/combat/controller.ts failure belongs to the engine lane)

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-057.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-057.test.ts   # clean
pnpm exec oxfmt --check <same files>                                                            # clean
git diff --check                                                                                # clean
```

#### Coordinator actions

`src/cards/BT23/BT23-catalog-sync.test.ts` now lists BT23-057 among the module/`effects.json` mismatches. The drift is exactly the added `zone: "battleArea"` on both `playCostCeiling.filter` entries. `effects.json` is coordinator-owned, so it needs that sync. Eleven other BT23 cards (029, 031, 035, 040, 044, 045, 047, 049, 050, 054, 055) were already in that list before this lane's change.

#### Remaining gaps

- No combat proof that the token's granted `<Alliance>` / `<Reboot>` / `<Blocker>` behave in an attack; only the grants are asserted through `observe(...).hasKeyword`.
- No proof of the ordering choice when the delete has several legal targets; the tests pin eligibility, not player choice.
- The `[When Digivolving]` route is proved once (through the alternate `[CS]` cost); the printed Black/Red Lv.5 route is not separately exercised.

### BT23-058 — Craniamon

#### Card

Catalog `BT23-058` Craniamon. Black, Digimon, level 6, play cost 11, DP 11000, evo cost Black level 5 for 3, form Mega, attribute Vaccine, types Holy Warrior / Royal Knight / CS, rarity R.

Printed text:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3` — alternate digivolution route.
2. `＜Reboot＞`
3. `＜Blocker＞`
4. `[All Turns] When any of your Digimon or Tamers would leave the battle area by your opponent's effects, by suspending this Digimon, 1 of those Digimon or Tamers doesn't leave.`
5. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When this Digimon suspends, delete all of your opponent's Digimon with the lowest play cost.`

No inherited effect is printed.

#### Rulings

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-058` returns `(no knowledge-base entries)`. the Knowledge base index section of this document line 64 lists BT23-058 with 0 Q&A ids. Nothing in REVIEW-NOTES.md or RUN.md mentions this card. So there is no Q&A checklist; all evidence is printed-text and comprehensive-rules driven.

#### Clause -> test -> IR plan

| # | Clause | IR node | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Digivolve Lv.5 w/[CS]: Cost 3 | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 5, traits: ["CS"], cost: 3, isAlternate: true }]` | alternate route from an off-color level-5 CS base with `useAlternateCost: true`, memory delta pinned to 3 and the bonus draw asserted; printed Black level-5 route as the control; negatives for a non-CS level-5 base and a CS level-4 base |
| 2 | ＜Reboot＞ | first `Static` effect, `keywords: [Reboot]` | live keyword read plus a turn-loop test that suspends Craniamon and proves the opponent's Active phase unsuspends it |
| 3 | ＜Blocker＞ | second `Static` effect, `keywords: [Blocker]` | live keyword read plus a real `declareBlock` against an opponent attack |
| 4 | Leave-play replacement | `AllTurns` / `Replacement` `wouldLeavePlay`, `mode: prevent`, `leaveCause: byOpponentEffect`, cost `suspend` self | opponent-effect deletion prevented and Craniamon suspended; own-effect deletion NOT prevented; refusal branch; only 1 of 2 threatened allies saved; unavailable while Craniamon is already suspended |
| 5 | Once-per-turn suspend deletion | `AllTurns` / `SubTrigger` `whenSuspended`, `Delete` `controller: opponent`, `superlative: lowestPlayCost`, `count: all`, `frequency: OncePerTurn` | attacking suspends Craniamon and deletes every tied lowest-play-cost opposing Digimon while the expensive one survives; the opponent's breeding Digimon is neither counted nor deleted; second suspend in the same turn does nothing; the next own turn re-arms it through the real turn loop |

Clause 4 and clause 5 chain: paying the replacement cost suspends Craniamon, which itself fires clause 5. That chain is asserted in the replacement test.

#### Results

Focused command: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-058.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file, 13 tests, all passed, 3.96s. Log: `logs/BT23-058-focused.log`.

`pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` passed with no errors. `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` reports the file correctly formatted. `pnpm exec oxlint` reports only two `no-explicit-any` warnings on the IR-shape test, matching the accepted style of the other BT23 suites (for example BT23-030).

##### What the behavioral tests prove

- **Blocker + deletion chain.** The opponent attacks the player; `blockWindowOpened` lists Craniamon as the only eligible blocker; a public `declareBlock` keeps security at 3, kills the 5000 DP attacker, and the block suspension deletes the cost-2 Digimon while the cost-6 Digimon survives. Endpoints asserted: security length, surviving permanent ids, `isSuspended`, and exact trash card ids.
- **Lowest-play-cost set and zone.** Craniamon attacks the player. Both cost-3 opposing Digimon are deleted, the cost-6 and cost-13 survive, and the opponent's cost-2 breeding Digimon is untouched — which also proves the deletion pool is the battle area only, since a leak into the raising area would have made the breeder the sole lowest and spared the pair. Trash is checked by instance id, not card id, because the security check puts a card in trash too.
- **Once per turn.** Through `startTurnLoop()` and public `endPhase`: the first attack deletes the cost-2 Digimon; an explicit unsuspend and a second attack in the same turn delete nothing (the surviving battle-area set is asserted, not merely awaited); on the next own turn Craniamon is already unsuspended by the Active phase and the third attack deletes the now-lowest cost-3 Digimon.
- **Reboot.** Through the same public turn loop: Craniamon is re-suspended after the own Active phase, and the opponent's Active phase unsuspends it.
- **Leave-play replacement.** An opponent-seat effect deletion is prevented, Craniamon ends suspended, and that suspension chains into the lowest-cost deletion. Negative controls: an own-effect deletion is not prevented and Craniamon stays unsuspended; the declined branch (`autoDeclineOptional`) lets the ally die; an already-suspended Craniamon cannot pay the cost; two threatened allies yield exactly one survivor.
- **Digivolution.** The alternate `Lv.5 w/[CS]` route from off-color BT23-044 with `useAlternateCost: true` moves memory by exactly 3, keeps the base permanent id, puts the exact BT23-058 instance on top with the base instance beneath, and draws the exact top deck card. The printed Black level-5 route (BT2-060) is the control at the same cost. Negatives: BT1-039 (level 5, no CS) and BT22-031 (CS, level 4) both return `invalid-evolution` with memory and hand untouched.

##### Mutation checks

To confirm the suite is not vacuous, the module was temporarily mutated and reverted:

| Mutation | Tests turned red |
|---|---|
| drop `leaveCause: "byOpponentEffect"` and flip `lowestPlayCost` -> `highestPlayCost` | 6 |
| drop `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` and set the replacement `count` to 2 | 2 |

The `count` change alone turned no behavioral test red: the engine's leave-prevention path saves one permanent per event regardless of that field, so the printed "1 of those" is proven by behavior but the IR `count` value itself is only proven by the shape test. This is an observation about the engine seam (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/leavePrevention.ts`), not a defect of this card — the observable result matches the printed text.

#### Defects

None found. The compiled IR matches the printed text on every clause, including the battle-area-only default for the deletion pool and the `byOpponentEffect` cause on the replacement. No catalog discrepancy: the committed `effectText` matches the printed card, and no knowledge-base entry contradicts it.

Fixture hygiene note: the previous suite used Digi-Egg cards (BT1-001..BT1-003) in security and relied on `advance().fireSubTrigger("whenSuspended", ...)` for its main deletion proof. Both are gone. Security now uses main-deck Digimon and every clause is driven by public intents (`attack`, `declareBlock`, `digivolve`, `endPhase`, `startTurnLoop`).

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained reds or skips.
- The replacement's `count: 1` field is not independently falsifiable through behavior (see the mutation table).
- The Reboot test asserts the unsuspend at the opponent's Active phase but re-suspends Craniamon by writing `isSuspended` directly, because no public intent suspends an own Digimon during the own turn after the Active phase has already run.

| IR trace | 2 | Every compiled node asserted and two mutation rounds prove the assertions bite |

| Peer / stack proof | 2 | Real peers used as evolution sources and deletion candidates (BT23-044, BT2-060, BT1-039, BT22-031, BT23-068, BT1-009..BT1-011), with source-stack identity asserted |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Coordinator owned |

Worker total: 8/10.

### BT23-059 — Justimon: Blitz Arm

#### Card

Black Lv.6 Mega Vaccine, play cost 11, DP 11000, types Cyborg / Hudie / CS.
Printed evoCost: Black Lv.5, cost 3.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] [Justimon: Accel Arm]/[Justimon: Critical Arm]: Cost 1`
2. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[CS] trait: Cost 3`
3. `＜Blocker＞`
4. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] [When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] By trashing 1 Option card in the battle area, delete 1 of your opponent's Digimon with the lowest play cost.`
5. `[All Turns] [Once Per Turn] When Option cards in the battle area are trashed, this Digimon unsuspends. Then, your opponent's Digimon's effects don't affect this Digimon for the turn.`

The catalog text matches the official BT23 list. No catalog discrepancy.

#### Rulings (KB-INDEX lists 7 Q&A)

| Q&A | Content | Coverage |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5323 | The trash cost takes an Option placed in the battle area by a "place this card in the battle area" effect. | Covered. `placedInBattleAreaByEffect: true` in the IR; the behavioral runs get their Option there only by publicly playing BT23-100 Hudie Net Cafe, whose `[Main]` effect places it. An Option in hand or trash is proved not to pay the cost. |
| Q5324 | "Effects don't affect" — the Digimon is not suspended and its DP is not reduced. | Covered behaviorally: an opponent BT23-028 Coordemon replayed from security applies `-3000 DP`; Blitz Arm stays at 11000 with immunity and drops to 8000 without it (matched control). |
| Q5325 | An unaffectable card can still be chosen. | Not covered — modelling gap, see below. |
| Q5326 | An unaffectable card can be given an effect but is not considered to have it. | Not covered — no local test seam; the grant/immunity interaction lives in the continuous ledger, not in this card. |
| Q5327 | Gaining immunity ends an effect already applying. | Not covered — the engine applies the `beAffected` restriction at grant time and does not retroactively strip live modifiers. |
| Q5328 | Losing immunity re-applies an effect the card was given. | Not covered — follows Q5327. |
| Q5329 | A trigger given to an unaffectable Digimon does not fire while it is unaffectable. | Not covered — no local test seam. |

##### Modelling gap (Q5325 / Q5327 / Q5329) — engine lane, not this card

`immuneToOpponentDigimonEffects` compiles to a `beAffected` restriction qualified to
`fromSourceKind: ["Digimon"]` (`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/grantStatic.ts:472`).
The target-resolution path then EXCLUDES the immune permanent from the candidate set
(`apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/targeting/permanents.ts:102-130`). The rules say the
card is still chosen and simply not affected (Q5325). For a single-target "suspend / -DP"
effect the observable outcome is the same, which is why the behavioral test above is green;
it differs where a card counts what it chose, or resolves a second clause off the choice.
Q5327 and Q5329 need the same seam. No `it.fails` reproducer is retained here because
writing one would assert on engine internals rather than on this card's behavior; the gap is
handed to the engine lane.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | IR node | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | `digivolutionRequirement[0]` — names, cost 1, `isAlternate` | `digivolves for 1 from either named Justimon…` — public `digivolve` from BT11-073 and BT10-067, memory 6 -> 5, stack identity and the bonus draw pinned |
| 2 | `digivolutionRequirement[1]` — level 5 + `CS`, cost 3 | same test, base BT23-044 Lilamon (off-colour Green Lv.5 CS), memory 6 -> 3 |
| printed evoCost | catalog `evoCosts` | same test, base BT10-064 Gogmamon (Black Lv.5), memory 6 -> 3, no alternate flag |
| illegal source | — | `refuses an illegal source` — BT1-039 (Blue Lv.5, no CS, wrong name) rejected `invalid-evolution`, memory and hand unchanged |
| 3 | `Static` keyword `Blocker` | `exposes Blocker through the live keyword seam and blocks a real attack` — live keyword read plus a real `declareBlock`: attacker trashed, security untouched, blocker suspended |
| 4 | `OnPlay` / `WhenDigivolving` / `WhenAttacking`, `frequency: OncePerTurn`, `sharedUseKey: ir-shared-0`, `cost.kind: trash` over `zone: battleArea` + `kind: ["Option"]` + `placedInBattleAreaByEffect`, `Delete` on `controller: opponent` + `superlative: lowestPlayCost` | `trashes the publicly placed Option when digivolving…` (cost paid, cost-2 Monodramon deleted, cost-7 MetalTyrannomon kept), `unsuspends itself mid-attack…` (When Attacking window), `declining the optional cost…`, `does nothing when no Option is in the battle area…`, `accepts an Option in the OPPONENT's battle area…` |
| 4, once per turn | shared `sharedUseKey` across three triggers | `uses the shared Once Per Turn only once a turn and resets it on the next own turn` — real turn loop, two Options on board, second same-turn attack trashes nothing, next own turn trashes the second Option |
| 5 | `AllTurns` SubTrigger `whenOptionInBattleAreaTrashed` -> `Unsuspend` + `GrantStatic immuneToOpponentDigimonEffects` `forTheTurn` | `unsuspends itself mid-attack…` (attacker suspends on declaration and is unsuspended again before the attack ends), `ignores an opponent Digimon's DP reduction…` (matched pair 11000 vs 8000) |

Every behavioral test drives public intents only — `playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`,
`declareBlock`, `endPhase`, `startTurnLoop`. No `fireTiming` / `advance.fire` injection
remains. The single exception is documented inline: placing an Option into the OPPONENT's
battle area uses the production `placeOptionAsPermanent` verb, because no public intent for
seat 0 can put a card there during seat 0's turn.

#### Defects found and fixed

1. The previous test file proved the three trigger windows with injected timings
   (`engine.fireTiming(EffectTiming.OnPlay, …)` and `advance.fire`). Those earn no behavioral
   credit and never exercised the shared once-per-turn key, the `[All Turns]` rider under a
   real attack, or the digivolve costs. Replaced with public-intent runs.
2. The previous digivolve test asserted `{ ok: true }` alone, which proves nothing about the
   cost. Costs are now pinned by memory delta on all four legal routes.
3. The previous "unsuspends" assertion ran on a hand-armed `suspended: true` permanent, so it
   could not distinguish the rider firing from the fixture. It now runs off a real attack
   declaration.
4. No defect found in `BT23-059.ts`. The IR is correct on the points this set gets wrong
   repeatedly: the cost filter carries `zone: "battleArea"` and no `controller`, the delete
   target carries `kind: ["Digimon"]`, and the three windows share one `sharedUseKey`.
   `BT23-059.ts` is unchanged.

#### Notes for the coordinator

- `useAlternateCost` is unobservable for this card. `apps/api/src/engine/actions/digivolve.ts:493-503`
  takes an alternate route without the flag whenever it is the only matching path, and no
  legal source for BT23-059 matches both a printed evoCost and an alternate requirement.
  The test pins the no-flag branch (BT10-067 still costs 1) rather than claiming a negative
  that does not exist.
- The `[All Turns]` clause fires from ANY Option leaving the battle area for the trash,
  including the one this card's own cost trashes. That self-chain is what the attack test
  observes.

```

Fixture correction after coordinator review: the once-per-turn test seeded both seats'
security with BT1-001 / BT1-002 / BT1-003, which are Digi-Eggs and illegal in security.
Replaced with BT1-009 / BT1-010 / BT1-011 (real main-deck Lv.3 Digimon). No expectation
changed: Blitz Arm attacks at 11000 DP into 3000 / 2000 / 1000 DP security Digimon, so each
security battle deletes the security Digimon and the attacker survives, exactly as before.

### BT23-060 — Machinedramon

#### Card summary

Committed catalog `BT23-060`: Black/Red level 6 Digimon, play cost 12, DP 12000, Mega, Virus, traits Machine/Zaxon/CS. Printed evolution costs Black level 5 for 4 and Red level 5 for 4. No printed inherited effect. The catalog text matches the official card list; no discrepancy to report.

Printed clauses:

1. `[Digivolve] Lv.5 w/[CS] trait: Cost 4` — alternate route, any color.
2. `＜Security A. +1＞`
3. `＜Reboot＞`
4. `[On Play] [When Digivolving] ＜De-Digivolve 1＞ 1 of your opponent's Digimon. Then, delete 1 of their Digimon with 8000 DP or less.`
5. `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] Activate 1 [On Play] effect on a face-up [Zaxon] Digimon card in your security stack as an effect of this Digimon.`

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-060` returns two Q&A. Both are covered.

| Q&A | Content | Covering test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Q5330 | The When Attacking effect is mandatory and consumes the Once Per Turn use even with no face-up [Zaxon] card in security. | `consumes its once-per-turn use even when no face-up Zaxon security card exists`; `optional: false` pinned by the IR trace test |
| Q5331 | A borrowed BT23-045 On Play must process its "by" cost; a trash source is used before a hand fallback. | `forces the borrowed BT23-045 processing condition…`, `uses an eligible trash card before a hand card…`, `falls back to an eligible hand card…`, and the non-forced control `does not force another eligible Zaxon borrower's costless optional On Play follow-up` |

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Clause | IR | Tests |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity, alternate route | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 5, traits: ["CS"], cost: 4, isAlternate: true }]` | `matches every catalog field and complete compiled clause` |
| Alternate CS route legality and cost | same | `digivolves for 4 from an off-color level-5 CS card and rejects a non-CS peer` (Green BT23-044 source, so only the CS route can apply; BT1-039 Blue non-CS rejected with `invalid-evolution`); `pays exactly 4 on the alternate CS route, keeps the source in the stack and draws 1` (memory 4 -> 0, exact source instance in the stack, exact drawn instance in hand) |
| ＜Security A. +1＞ | `Static` / `GainKeyword` `SecurityAttack` amount 1, `duration: "permanent"` | `exposes Security Attack +1 and Reboot through the live keyword seam`; `checks two security cards on a player attack through Security Attack +1` (3 security -> 1 left, 2 exact cards in trash, attacker survives) |
| ＜Reboot＞ | `Static` / `GainKeyword` `Reboot`, `duration: "permanent"` | keyword seam test; the turn-loop test also observes the attacker suspended after attacking and unsuspended on its next own turn |
| On Play / When Digivolving de-digivolve then delete | `DeDigivolve` amount 1 then `Delete` with `dp lte 8000` | `plays for 12, de-digivolves first, then deletes only the opponent's resulting 4000-DP Digimon` (public `playCard`, memory 12 -> 0, both stack cards in the opponent trash, own 3000-DP Digimon untouched); `de-digivolves first, then deletes the resulting 8000-DP-or-lower Digimon`; `leaves a 9000-DP opposing Digimon alive because the delete stops at 8000 DP`; structural ordering test for both triggers |
| When Attacking borrow, mandatory, once per turn | `ActivateForeignEffect` zone `security`, `fromTriggers: ["OnPlay"]`, filter `controller: "mine"`, `kind: ["Digimon"]`, `faceUp: true`, trait `Zaxon`, `optional: false`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` | `activates a face-up Zaxon security card's On Play effect when attacking`; `does not borrow from a face-down Zaxon security card`; `does not borrow from a face-up security Digimon without the Zaxon trait`; `does not borrow from the opponent's face-up Zaxon security card`; `consumes its once-per-turn use even when no face-up Zaxon security card exists`; `resets the once-per-turn borrow on the next own turn through the real turn loop` |
| Q5331 borrowed-cost override | `borrowedEffectOverrides` scoped to BT23-045 On Play | the four override tests, plus `declares the Q5331 override only for BT23-045 On Play` |

#### Duration audit

The module carries exactly two `duration:` strings, both `"permanent"`. `EffectDurationRef` (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/durations.ts`) lists `"permanent"`, which maps to the never-clearing `EffectDuration.Permanent`. No unknown duration string is present, so neither keyword grant silently expires at turn end. The live keyword seam test confirms both grants are readable on the board.

#### Once-per-turn evidence

- Same-turn refusal: `consumes its once-per-turn use even when no face-up Zaxon security card exists` attacks once with the lender face down (Q5330 still burns the use), flips the lender face up, then attacks again in the same turn; the opponent's Digimon survives. The second attack needs the attacker unsuspended, which this test does through `advance().verb.unsuspend`. That is an injected step, so the refusal proof is public in the attack and the lender state, but not in the unsuspend.
- Next-turn reset: `resets the once-per-turn borrow on the next own turn through the real turn loop` uses `startTurnLoop()`, `waitForMainPhase`, and public `endPhase` intents. It asserts the intermediate state explicitly — exactly one of two named victims deleted after the first attack, the attacker suspended, the attacker unsuspended after the real Unsuspend phase — then proves the second victim is deleted by the borrow on the next own turn.

#### Mutation evidence for the borrow filter

Three temporary mutations of `BT23-060.ts` were run to check which assertions actually discriminate (the module was restored afterwards; the working tree carries the original filter).

| Mutation | Result |
| --- | --- |
| Drop `nameOrTrait` `Zaxon` | 2 failures: the IR trace test and `does not borrow from a face-up security Digimon without the Zaxon trait`. The trait restriction is behaviorally load-bearing. |
| Drop `faceUp: true` | 1 failure: the IR trace test only. |
| Drop `controller: "mine"` | 1 failure: the IR trace test only. |

Cause, not a card defect: `collectForeignCandidates` in `apps/api/src/engine/effects/interpreter/actions/borrowed.ts` (lines ~131-139) hardcodes both properties for `zone: "security"`. It reads the seat as `action.filter.controller === "opponent" ? opponent : mine`, and it pushes only `card.faceUp` security cards. So `faceUp: true` and `controller: "mine"` in this card's IR document the printed text but cannot change behavior. The two behavioral negatives still prove the printed rule holds; they just do not isolate those two IR fields. Flagged for the coordinator as an engine observation, not a card change.

#### Defects found

None in the card module. Two notes:

- The `Delete` target uses `controllerDefault: "opponent"` rather than `controller: "opponent"`. `controllerDefault` is documented as seat-enumeration only, but `matching/permanent.ts:130` resolves the scope as `filter.controller ?? filter.controllerDefault`, so the opponent restriction is enforced. `plays for 12, de-digivolves first, then deletes only the opponent's resulting 4000-DP Digimon` proves it behaviorally: the controller's own 3000-DP Digimon is never chosen. Left as-is; changing the structural signature is a catalog-shaped decision, not a local card fix.
- `borrowedEffectOverrides` is deliberately narrowed to BT23-045's On Play. Q5331's answer states a general principle ("if the effect will activate, you must process the 'by' condition"), so a future Zaxon lender with a `by` cost will need the same treatment. Today BT23-045 is the only [Zaxon] Digimon whose On Play carries one, so the narrow scoping is correct and the control test proves other lenders are not force-processed.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-060.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file / 21 tests passed, 4.49s. Log: `logs/BT23-060-focused.log`. The suite grew from 13 to 21 tests in this re-audit.
- Typecheck: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — zero errors in `BT23-060.ts` and `BT23-060.test.ts`. The run as a whole fails on six pre-existing errors in `src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.test.ts` (`Type '"digimon"' is not assignable to type '"player" | "permanent"'`), which belong to another lane.
- Lint: `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-060.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-060.test.ts` — zero errors, five pre-existing `no-explicit-any` warnings in the IR-shape assertions of the test file, matching the style of the sibling BT23 suites.
- Format: `pnpm exec oxfmt --check` on both files — correctly formatted. `git diff --check` — clean.

#### Remaining gaps

- No retained red and no skip. No engine seam blocks this card.
- The same-turn once-per-turn refusal still uses one injected unsuspend; there is no public way to attack twice in one turn with this fixture.
- The When Digivolving branch of clause 4 is proved structurally and through the shared On Play behavior, not through a separate public digivolve that also has an opposing target. The public digivolve test proves cost, source retention, and the draw with an empty opposing board.
- Borrowing a lender other than BT23-015 and BT23-045 is untested; those two cover the forced-cost and costless branches.

#### Worker score

| Column | Score | Basis |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog / rules | 2 | Every printed clause and both Q&A mapped; catalog verified against the official text; duration strings checked against `EffectDurationRef`. |
| IR trace | 2 | Every action, filter, frequency and override pinned, with mutation evidence showing which fields are load-bearing. |
| Behavioral proof | 2 | Public `playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `endPhase` and `surrender` intents with exact instance ids, memory, DP, security counts, and trash endpoints; boundary and negative branches present. |
| Peer / stack proof | 2 | BT23-015 and BT23-045 lender pair, non-Zaxon and opponent-seat lender negatives, off-color CS evolution source with an illegal non-CS peer. |
| Delivery gates | 0 | Worker lane; the coordinator owns gates. |

Total: 8/10.

#### Accepted score

9/10 — catalog/rules 2/2; IR trace 2/2; behavioral proof 2/2; peer and stack proof 2/2; delivery gates 1/2. Reviewed; evidence; collection gates passed on the working tree (sync/check, typecheck, lint, format, regression 3542/3542); commit and PR outstanding

### BT23-061 — Ghostmon

#### Card summary

Committed catalog `BT23-061`. Purple level 3 Rookie, Data, traits Ghost / LIBERATOR. Play cost 3, DP 1000, digivolve cost 0 from a level 2 Purple source. Rarity C.

Printed clauses:

1. `[On Play] [On Deletion] 1 of your Digimon with the [Ghost] trait gains ＜Blocker＞ until your opponent's turn ends.` — one clause body shared by two timing windows. Mandatory, exactly one recipient, own Digimon only, Ghost trait only.
2. Inherited: `[On Deletion] Gain 1 memory.`

Catalog text matches the official BT23 card list verbatim (`logs/official-bt23.html`, entry BT23-061). No discrepancy to report.

#### Rulings

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-061` returns `(no knowledge-base entries)`. `KB-INDEX.md` agrees: 0 Q&A ids. So there is no Q&A or errata to cover, and every clause below is proven against the comprehensive rules plus the printed text.

One comprehensive-rules point drives a test: CR §3-4-5-3 — cards in the breeding area cannot be affected by effects unless the effect names the breeding area. The compiled filter has no `zone` field, so the breeding exclusion had to be proven, not assumed.

#### Clause -> test -> IR mapping

| Printed clause | IR | Test |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Catalog identity and printed strings | — | `matches every catalog field and the printed clause text` |
| Both windows carry the same body | `OnPlay` + `OnDeletion` `GainKeyword` actions | `compiles both windows to one own-Ghost Blocker grant...` |
| `[On Play]` grants Blocker | `OnPlay` -> `GainKeyword` | `grants Blocker to itself on a public play from hand, paying the full 3 cost` |
| "1 of your Digimon with the [Ghost] trait" — own, Ghost, battle area, exactly one | `filter.controller: "mine"`, `kind: ["Digimon"]`, `nameOrTrait: [{tokens:["Ghost"], match:"trait"}]`, `count: 1` | `offers only own battle-area Ghost Digimon and grants Blocker to exactly one of them` |
| "until your opponent's turn ends" | `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` | `keeps the Blocker through the opponent's whole turn and drops it on the controller's next turn` |
| `[On Deletion]` grants Blocker | `OnDeletion` -> `GainKeyword` | `grants Blocker to a surviving Ghost when Ghostmon dies in a public battle` |
| `[On Deletion]` with no legal recipient | same | `resolves the deletion clause with no legal recipient without rejecting anything` |
| Inherited `[On Deletion] Gain 1 memory.` | `isInherited: true` -> `GainMemory { amount: 1 }` | `gains exactly 1 memory from the inherited clause when a Ghostmon-stacked host dies in battle` |
| Printed digivolve requirement (level 2 Purple, cost 0) | catalog `evoCosts` | `publicly evolves from a level 2 Purple source for 0 memory and rejects an off-color source` |

#### Evidence detail

All acting is through public intents (`playCard`, `digivolve`, `attack`, `endPhase`, `surrender`) plus `startTurnLoop()`. The Advance Surface is used only for `waitForMainPhase` / `endMainPhaseIfOpen`, never to fire a timing directly. The prior test file fired `EffectTiming.OnPlay` through an injected cast and deleted permanents with `advance().verb.deletePermanent`; both are replaced by real play and real combat.

- **On Play, public.** Ghostmon is played from hand with memory 3. Endpoints asserted: memory 0, hand empty, the exact hand instance is now the permanent's top card, DP 1000, Blocker on itself, and `blockerHolders` for the seat equals exactly that one permanent id. `assertNoLoudGap` confirms no unsupported-effect rejection.
- **Target legality.** Fixture holds an own second Ghost (BT20-063), an own non-Ghost (BT1-009 Monodramon), an own Ghost in **breeding**, and an opponent Ghost. The recorded `chooseTargets`/`selectCards` candidate set is resolved to permanent ids and asserted to contain the two own battle-area Ghosts and to exclude the non-Ghost, the opponent Ghost, and the breeding Ghost. `preferInstanceIds` steers the pick to the second Ghost; the seat's Blocker holder list is exactly that permanent, and the opponent seat has none. The engine therefore already applies the CR §3-4-5-3 breeding exclusion without a `zone: "battleArea"` field in the IR, so no module change was needed there.
- **Duration.** Real turn loop. Ghostmon is played on seat 0's Main; one holder is recorded. After passing to seat 1's Main the holder list is asserted to still equal that same permanent id (the grant survives the whole opponent turn). After passing back to seat 0's Main the holder list is asserted empty. Both intermediate states are asserted explicitly. A neutral playable card is kept in each hand so Main does not auto-pass before the assertions.
- **On Deletion, public.** Ghostmon (1000 DP) attacks a suspended BT1-021 (larger DP) and loses the battle. After the attack settles, Ghostmon's exact instance is in trash and the surviving own Ghost is the sole Blocker holder; the non-Ghost has none.
- **On Deletion, no recipient.** Same battle with only a non-Ghost surviving. No Blocker is granted anywhere, no `actionRejected` event is emitted, and no loud gap fires. This proves the mandatory clause fizzles cleanly rather than erroring.
- **Inherited memory.** Ghostmon is publicly digivolved into BT4-080 Bakemon (level 4 Purple Ghost, no printed effects — chosen so the memory swing is unambiguous). Cost 2 is asserted (memory 2 -> 0), Ghostmon's exact instance is asserted as the single digivolution card, and the digivolve bonus draw is asserted by exact drawn instance in hand. Bakemon then loses a battle; both cards reach trash and memory is exactly 1. Ghostmon's own top-card clause is inactive as a digivolution card, so the whole +1 is the inherited clause.
- **Evolution.** Legal: BT10-006 Tokomon (level 2 Purple) in breeding digivolves into Ghostmon at memory 0 with memory unchanged (cost 0), Tokomon retained as the digivolution card, and the bonus draw landing as an exact instance in hand. Illegal negative: BT1-002 Bebydomon (level 2 **Red**) is refused; the intent returns `{ ok: false }`, the source's top card is unchanged, and memory is untouched.

#### Defects found and fixed

- `apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-061.ts` carried a `// @ts-nocheck` header. Removed. `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` reports no error in this module or its test, per the brief's instruction that a suppressed module hides duration-type errors like the BT23-047 one.

No IR defect was found. Checked specifically for the pitfalls listed in the worker brief: `controller: "mine"` is present, `kind: ["Digimon"]` is present, trait matching is `match: "trait"` (no name/substring risk — the card has no bracketed name condition), and `duration: "untilOpponentTurnEnd"` is a real member of `EffectDurationRef` (`packages/shared/src/effects/ir/durations.ts:8`), so `toDuration` does not silently downgrade it. The missing `zone: "battleArea"` is not a defect here: the breeding-exclusion test proves the engine already excludes breeding from this filter.

#### Mutation checks

The suite was validated by mutating the module and confirming red, then restoring:

- `"Ghost"` -> `"Zzz"` in the trait tokens **and** `untilOpponentTurnEnd` -> `endOfTurn`: 5 failed / 4 passed.
- `untilOpponentTurnEnd` -> `endOfTurn` alone: 2 failed / 7 passed — killed by the IR-shape test and by the turn-loop duration test, which is the behavioral kill.

#### Commands and results

- Focused: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-061.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism` — 1 file / **9 tests passed**, 5.86s. Log: `logs/BT23-061-focused.log`.
- Typecheck: `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` — no error in `BT23-061.ts` or `BT23-061.test.ts`. The only remaining errors in the run are six pre-existing `TS2322` errors in `src/cards/BT23/BT23-055.test.ts` (another lane's file, `target: { kind: "digimon" }`), which the coordinator should route to that owner.
- Lint: `pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-061.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-061.test.ts` — clean.
- Format: `pnpm exec oxfmt` applied to the test file; `--check` now clean on both files. `git diff --check` clean.

#### Retained reds and skips

None. No `it.fails` or `it.skip` was needed; no engine seam blocked a correct assertion for this card.

#### Remaining gaps

- The Blocker grant is proven to exist and to expire, but no test exercises the granted Blocker in an actual block window (declaring a block with the recipient). That is a keyword-mechanic proof rather than a BT23-061 clause proof, and the shared Blocker mechanic is covered elsewhere in the engine suite.
- `[On Deletion]` is proven through battle loss only. Deletion by an opponent effect is not separately covered; the clause has no by-battle or by-effect qualifier, so the distinction is not printed on the card.
- The illegal-evolution negative covers a wrong-colour level 2 source. A wrong-level source is not separately covered.

| IR trace | 2 | Every IR field mapped to a printed phrase; both windows and the inherited clause asserted; `@ts-nocheck` removed and the duration string verified against `EffectDurationRef`. |

### BT23-062 — Dracmon

#### Card

Lv.3 Purple Rookie/Virus, DP 1000, play cost 3, digivolve cost 0 from Lv.2 Purple.
Types: `Undead`, `CS`.

Printed text (official card list, `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/official-bt23.html`):

| # | Clause |
| - | ------ |
| C1 | `[Digivolve] Lv.2 w/[CS] trait: Cost 0` |
| C2 | `[Start of Your Main Phase] By trashing 1 card with the [Undead], [Dark Animal] or [CS] trait from your hand, gain 1 memory.` |
| C3 | inherited: `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] 1 of your Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with the [Undead] or [Dark Animal] trait in the trash.` |

The catalog record matches the official card list field by field (colors, level, DP, cost,
evo cost, form, attribute, types, both text blocks). No catalog discrepancy.

#### Rulings covered

| Q&A | Statement | Test |
| --- | --------- | ---- |
| Q5333 | A Digimon that attacks with BT23-062 in its digivolution cards and digivolves through BT23-062's inherited effect can **not** then activate its own — now inherited — `[When Attacking]` effect. | `does not activate the newly inherited When Attacking effect after digivolving mid-window (Q5333)` |

Rules basis: CR 15-16-5-1 (`[When Attacking]` triggers at the point of the attack
declaration), so a card that only gains the effect later never triggered at all.
CR 15-4-4-3/15-4-4-4 cover the neighbouring case where an already-pending effect is lost
because its card became a new card.

The Q5333 test isolates the ruling: BT24-069 Vilemon has an inherited (and only inherited)
`[When Attacking]` effect — "trash the top card of both players' decks" — with no upper
`[When Attacking]`. Dracmon's inherited effect turns Vilemon into a digivolution card
mid-window; the test asserts neither deck loses a card.

The companion test `still resolves its own inherited effect after the top card's When
Attacking digivolution` pins the neighbouring, legal case on Q5333's literal board
(BT23-063 Sangloupmon over BT23-062): both `[When Attacking]` effects trigger at the
declaration, Dracmon stays a digivolution card throughout, so **two** digivolutions happen
(Myotismon for 2, then SkullMammothmon for 4). This is not the ruling's case and the engine
handles it correctly; the test exists so a future "fix" cannot silently break it.

#### Clause -> test -> IR map

| Clause | IR | Tests |
| ------ | -- | ----- |
| identity | catalog record | `matches every catalog field and printed text` |
| C1 | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 2, traits: ["CS"], cost: 0, isAlternate: true }]` | `digivolves from a level 2 CS source of another colour for 0` (both `useAlternateCost` branches), `refuses a level 2 source that is neither purple nor CS` (both branches) |
| C2 | `StartOfYourMainPhase` -> `GainMemory 1`, mandatory `trash` cost, `abortOnDecline: true` | `compiles the main-phase clause as a mandatory trash cost, not an optional effect`; `gains 1 memory at the start of your own main phase by trashing a matching hand card`; `accepts %s as the %s trait cost` (Undead / Dark Animal / CS); `does not gain memory when no hand card carries a matching trait`; `stays silent on the opponent's main phase and fires again on your next turn` |
| C3 | inherited `WhenAttacking`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"`, `Digivolve` `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: true`, `optional: true` | `compiles the inherited clause as a once-per-turn optional trash digivolution`; `digivolves a carrier into an Undead card from the trash when attacking`; `leaves the board untouched when the optional inherited digivolution is declined`; `is once per turn and resets on your next turn`; the two Q5333 tests |

All behavioral tests drive public intents (`attack`, `digivolve`, `endPhase`, `surrender`)
through the real turn loop. No injected timing API is used; the previous suite's
`fireTiming(OnStartMainPhase)` calls are gone.

Notes on specific proofs:

- **C1 route.** The alternate route's cost (0) equals the printed purple Lv.2 route's cost,
  so memory cannot separate them. The discriminator used is colour: BT23-003 Motimon is
  **Black** with `[CS]`, so only the alternate clause makes it a legal source. Both
  `useAlternateCost` branches are pinned; both succeed at cost 0 and draw 1, because the
  clause is a legality route, not a cost reduction. BT1-005 Kyaromon (Yellow, no `[CS]`) is
  refused with `invalid-evolution` on both branches.
- **C1 fixture.** The source is placed in the **breeding area**. A Lv.2 permanent has no
  legal battle-area route, and the engine correctly refuses to digivolve one there — the
  previous suite asserted `{ ok: true }` on a battle-area Lv.2 source, which the engine
  returns while doing nothing at all (no stack change, no memory, card still in hand). That
  assertion proved nothing.
- **C2 turn boundary.** One test runs three main phases (yours, the opponent's, yours) and
  asserts the trash contents at each: one cost card spent on your first main phase, nothing
  on the opponent's, the second cost card spent on your next one.
- **C3 once per turn.** The same turn's second attack (after
  `advance.verb.unsuspend`) leaves the stack and memory untouched with a legal Lv.6 Undead
  card sitting in the trash; the next own turn's attack digivolves into it, so both the
  refusal and the reset are proved against real state, not an absence of errors.

#### Commands and results

- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-062.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism`
  -> **1 file, 18 tests, all passing**, 10.9 s. Log: `docs/audits/BT23-reaudit/logs/BT23-062-vitest.log`.
- `pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` -> no errors in `BT23-062.ts` or `BT23-062.test.ts`.
  Pre-existing failures elsewhere (`src/engine/effects/collect.ts`, `BT23-065.test.ts`,
  a stray `zz-dbg.test.ts`) belong to other lanes.
- `pnpm exec oxlint` / `pnpm exec oxfmt` on both files -> clean.

#### Defects found

1. **`// @ts-nocheck` on the module** — removed. The module typechecks without it, so type
   errors in this card's IR now surface (per the BT23-047 `duration:` lesson). The module has
   no `duration:` field, so nothing else needed checking. This is the only module change; the
   compiled IR itself is correct as written.
2. **Weak prior evidence** (fixed in the test file, not a product defect):
   - the main-phase clause was proved through the injected `fireTiming` seam;
   - the alternate digivolution "proof" asserted `{ ok: true }` on a battle-area Lv.2 source
     while the engine performed no digivolution at all;
   - no once-per-turn refusal/reset, no controller boundary, no decline branch, and no Q5333
     coverage existed.

No engine defect found. No retained red tests, no skips.

#### Remaining gaps

- The alternate route's `cost: 0` is not distinguishable from the printed route's `cost: 0`
  by any observable. If a later engine change makes `useAlternateCost` a cost selector rather
  than a legality flag, this card cannot detect it.
- `[Once Per Turn]` is proved per source card on one carrier. Two separate Dracmon copies
  each granting their own use is not tested (no ruling asks for it).

| IR trace | 2 | Every compiled clause asserted structurally and mapped to behavior; `@ts-nocheck` removed so the IR is now typechecked. |

### BT23-063 — Sangloupmon

#### Card

Purple Digimon, Lv.4 Champion, Virus, [Dark Animal]/[CS], DP 4000, play cost 4, EvoCost purple Lv.3 for 2.

Printed clauses (official list, `logs/official-bt23.html`, matches `cards.json` exactly):

| # | Clause |
| - | ------ |
| C1 | `[Digivolve] Lv.3 w/[CS] trait: Cost 2` |
| C2 | `[When Attacking] This Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with the [Undead] or [CS] trait in the trash.` |
| C3 | inherited: `[When Attacking] [Once Per Turn] 1 of your Digimon may digivolve into a Digimon card with the [Undead] or [Dark Animal] trait in the trash.` |

No catalog discrepancy.

#### Rulings covered

`node tools/kb/query.mjs card BT23-063` lists two Q&A, both covered by behavioral tests.

| Q&A | Ruling | Test |
| --- | ------ | ---- |
| Q5332 | After C2 digivolves this Digimon, its own inherited [When Attacking] can't then be activated. | "Q5332 does not activate its own inherited effect after the printed one digivolves it" |
| Q5333 | Same when BT23-062 Dracmon's inherited effect digivolves this Digimon. | "Q5333 does not activate its inherited effect after BT23-062 digivolves it" |

Both are proved by a card that only the inherited effect could reach (a level-6 [Dark Animal] card) staying in the trash, plus the memory total showing exactly one digivolution cost was paid.

#### Clause -> test -> IR

| Clause | Tests | IR |
| ------ | ----- | -- |
| C1 | "publicly evolves from a purple level 3 for 2", "takes the [CS] alternate from an off-colour level 3 for 2", "refuses the alternate route from a level 3 without the [CS] trait" | `digivolutionRequirement: [{ level: 3, traits: ["CS"], cost: 2, isAlternate: true }]` |
| C2 | "digivolves itself out of the trash on the [Undead] branch / the [CS] branch", "offers nothing when the trash holds neither an [Undead] nor a [CS] Digimon", "leaves the trash card alone when the controller declines", "does not fire on the opponent's attack" | `WhenAttacking` -> `Digivolve` with `target.isSelf`, `from: ["trash"]`, `payCost: true`, `optional: true` |
| C3 | "inherited: digivolves another of your Digimon into an [Undead] card / a [Dark Animal] card", "inherited: offers nothing for a trash card with neither trait", "inherited: never reaches a Digimon in the breeding area", "inherited: refuses a second use in the same turn and works again on the next own turn", "inherited: does not fire while Sangloupmon is the top card of the attacking stack" | `WhenAttacking`, `isInherited: true`, `frequency: "OncePerTurn"` |
| Q5332 / Q5333 | see above | no IR of its own; the window semantics are the engine's |

Every behavioral test drives the public `attack` intent. No injected timing API is used anywhere in this suite (the previous version of this file drove `fireTiming` directly and asserted nothing about the attack itself).

Endpoints asserted per test: the physical instance id on top of the stack, the full digivolution-card list, memory before and after, the opponent's remaining security count, trash contents by instance id, and `pendingDecision === undefined`. `assertNoLoudGap` runs on the two positive digivolution tests.

#### Fixtures

Chosen so one trait branch is isolated and nothing else fires inside the window:

- BT2-075 Myotismon — purple Lv.5 [Undead], no effects at all.
- BT23-067 LadyDevimon — [CS] without [Undead]. Its multi-route EvoCost raises a `chooseOption` prompt, so that case sets `autoChooseOption`.
- BT4-085 Phantomon — purple Lv.5 [Ghost], the negative for both trait lists.
- BT4-082 Dobermon / BT4-083 Cerberusmon / BT9-079 GranDracmon — [Dark Animal] without [Undead] or [CS], reachable only through the inherited effect.
- BT23-048 Gotsumon (black/red Lv.3 [CS]) and BT1-009 Monodramon (red Lv.3, no [CS]) for the alternate route and its negative.

Security stacks use main-deck Digimon only; no Digi-Egg is placed in a deck or security.

#### Defects found

1. **Structural-only prior suite.** The previous `BT23-063.test.ts` proved C2 through `engine.fireTiming(EffectTiming.OnUseAttack, ...)` and proved C3 only by shape-matching the IR. It never declared an attack, never covered Q5332/Q5333, had no once-per-turn proof, no decline branch, no negative trait case and no controller-boundary case. Replaced.
2. **Prior alternate-route test was weak.** It asserted `{ ok: true }` for a plain `digivolve` intent without `useAlternateCost`, which the brief flags as proving nothing about the cost. The new tests pin the alternate branch explicitly (`useAlternateCost: true`, `alternateRequirementIndex: 0`) and assert memory reaching 0 from 2 plus the evolution bonus draw.
3. **`// @ts-nocheck` removed** from `BT23-063.ts`. The module typechecks without it (`pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck` reports no `BT23-063` diagnostic), so a future bad `duration:` or filter key will now surface.

No behavioral defect was found in the IR. The inherited target filter carries no `zone` key, which the brief warns can leak into the breeding area; the added breeding-area test shows the interpreter already excludes it (§3-4-5-3), so the module was left unchanged rather than edited without a red proof. The test asserts the filter as it actually is, with a comment naming the test that covers the risk.

#### Retained reds / skips

None. All 18 tests are green.

  -> Test Files 1 passed (1); Tests 18 passed (18); 8.82s

pnpm --filter @aegis/api exec vitest run src/cards/BT23/BT23-032.test.ts src/cards/BT23/BT23-062.test.ts --maxWorkers=1 --no-file-parallelism
  -> Test Files 2 passed (2); Tests 40 passed (40)   (peers that use BT23-063 as a fixture)

pnpm --filter @aegis/api typecheck
  -> no BT23-063 diagnostic; pre-existing unrelated failures in src/cards/BT23/BT23-065.test.ts
     and src/engine/effects/collect.ts belong to other lanes

pnpm exec oxlint apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-063.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-063.test.ts   -> clean
pnpm exec oxfmt apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-063.ts apps/api/src/cards/BT23/BT23-063.test.ts    -> formatted
```

Logs: `logs/BT23-063-vitest.log`, `logs/BT23-063-typecheck.log`.

#### Remaining gaps

- The two `chooseOption` routes for the [CS] fixture both cost 3, so the test does not distinguish which digivolution route the engine picked. A fixture whose routes differ in cost would pin that, but it is BT23-067's behavior, not BT23-063's.
- C2 is not proved against a target that is legal for the printed effect but illegal as a digivolution (wrong level or colour). The engine filters the candidate list before the prompt, so such a case is indistinguishable from the "no legal target" test already present.
- The opponent-turn boundary is proved for C2 only; C3 on an opponent's attack is the same `WhenAttacking` window and the same controller check.
